生成模型/扩散3D视觉
2026-10-16

Persistent Planar Memory for Video World Models

Persistent Planar Memory for Video World Models

Yuze He、Bin Tan、Zelin Gao、Yujun Shen、Yong-Jin Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Video world models that generate and explore environments at scale need a persistent 3D memory of what they have produced. Plane primitives offer a natural candidate: a compact set of oriented planes captures the dominant structural elements (e.g., walls, floors, facades) of indoor and outdoor scenes, while requiring only 7 parameters per primitive. We present PPMem, a video world model that adopts plane primitives as its persistent 3D memory. PPMem augments a video diffusion transformer with a planar prediction head and a Depth Token Fusion (DTF) module: each autoregressive chunk jointly produces RGB video and planar geometry, which merges into a persistent plane memory that conditions subsequent chunks via rendered depth. The resulting memory is three orders of magnitude more compact than per-pixel point clouds (~100K primitives, 5MB for a full scene), geometrically explicit (metric depth, normals, exportable surfaces), cross-view consistent (jointly predicted by the shared backbone), and adaptive (refined by the merging procedure as generation proceeds). On DL3DV and RealEstate10K, PPMem outperforms prior 3D-grounded world models in video quality while jointly producing metric-scale depth, surface normals, and exportable planar geometry, sustaining coherence across hundreds of frames under large viewpoint changes. Project page: https://hyzcluster.github.io/ppmem.

目标检测/分割
2026-10-16

A Paired RGB-Sonar Dataset for Fish Monitoring with Consumer Sonar

A Paired RGB-Sonar Dataset for Fish Monitoring with Consumer Sonar

Mourad Derbedrosian、Michael Hobley、Pietro Perona

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Imaging sonar enables underwater observation in conditions where optical imaging is ineffective due to darkness and turbid water. However, imaging-sonar systems previously used for ecological monitoring have typically been expensive, limiting their accessibility. Consumer forward-looking sonar has the potential to make fish monitoring more accessible, but its noisy imagery makes annotation and the development of automated methods challenging. We introduce the Huntington and Caltech Koi RGB and Sonar dataset (HaCKRS), which pairs Garmin LiveScope imaging-sonar recordings with top-down RGB video of koi carp. The dataset contains more than 105,000 temporally matched frame pairs, spanning approximately 3.3 hours of recordings across four range and orientation configurations. We provide frame-wise metadata, sonar geometry and coordinate information, synchronization data for all four configurations, and planar registration for the three horizontal configurations. The release focuses on paired observations and calibration rather than task-specific fish annotations. We intend HaCKRS to support annotation and the development of fish detection, tracking, and counting methods, and investigations into the potential of consumer sonar for ecological monitoring.

目标检测/分割医学图像
2026-10-16

Beyond Multi-Stage Pipelines: A Label-Correlation Ensemble for Multi-Label Retinal Fundus Disease Classification

Beyond Multi-Stage Pipelines: A Label-Correlation Ensemble for Multi-Label Retinal Fundus Disease Classification

Md Asif Uddin、Jannatul Ferdous、Asif Islam、Zobaer Mahmud Zisan、Promit Ghosh 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Retinal fundus images frequently carry more than one disease, and public fundus datasets are heterogeneous and severely long-tailed. Clinical triage suggests a staged design, in which a screening gate, a disease-group router and group-specific classifiers operate in sequence, but it is unclear whether this hierarchy earns its complexity. We present a controlled comparison of three architectures on a unified 39-target label space built from RFMiD 1.0, RFMiD 2.0 and ODIR-5K (12,652 images): (i) a three-stage gated cascade of eight models, (ii) a flat model with 39 independent sigmoid outputs, and (iii) a label-correlation model that replaces the flat linear head with 39 learned label queries and a two-layer transformer decoder. All three share one Swin-V2-B backbone, one preprocessing pipeline and one training protocol, so differences are attributable to the architecture. Staging offers no consistent advantage: tracing every missed disease instance to the stage that lost it shows that the two coarse gates discard 34.2% of true instances while the fine-grained classifiers lose only 6.7%, and the gates' hard zeros depress the cascade's macro-AUC to 0.747. Label-correlation modeling gives the largest single gain, raising the detection rate from 0.621 to 0.682 (0.689 with test-time augmentation). Blending the flat and label-correlation models under four-view test-time augmentation is the best configuration on six of eight metrics (exact match 0.694, micro-F1 0.678, macro-F1 0.456, macro-AUC 0.972). On definition-matched class subsets it matches or exceeds four recent studies on macro- and micro-F1, most clearly on low-support pathologies. We also show that ensembling a gated cascade with continuous-output models is harmful, and that per-source negative sampling is needed to remove a dataset shortcut that otherwise yields a spurious AUC of 1.000.

2026-10-15

Material and Defocus Cues for Occluded Object Localization in Single Hyperspectral Images: An Exploratory Study

Material and Defocus Cues for Occluded Object Localization in Single Hyperspectral Images: An Exploratory Study

Ali Zia、Jun Zhou

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Partial occlusion makes object detection difficult when visible regions have weak texture or similar appearance. Hyperspectral imaging provides material-dependent spectra and wavelength-dependent optical blur, which offer complementary evidence for separating objects. We investigate a pipeline that combines nonnegative-matrix-factorization-based material grouping, divergence-based optical cues, and graph Laplacian propagation. The propagated scores support region extraction and bounding-box localization without requiring a separate depth sensor at inference. A collection of 30 indoor and six outdoor hyperspectral scenes acquired using two camera systems provides qualitative examples spanning different materials, depth arrangements, and partial occlusions. The examples illustrate how material information can support separation when optical cues are weak, and how optical cues can distinguish some surfaces with similar material properties. Objects sharing both material and depth remain challenging. This exploratory study establishes the sensing rationale and mathematical formulation, while identifying the quantitative evaluation and implementation details needed to assess detection performance systematically.

生成模型/扩散视频理解
2026-10-16

Agentic Physics-Grounded Video Generation via Memory-Augmented In-Context Reasoning

Agentic Physics-Grounded Video Generation via Memory-Augmented In-Context Reasoning

Xikang Guan、Shaocong Yang、Yanqi Bao、Can Wang、Zheng Gu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Physical plausibility is essential for realistic video generation. Existing methods impose physical guidance before synthesis via simulations or refined prompts, but this can limit dynamic diversity, miss errors that emerge only after generation, and require repeated regeneration when inconsistencies remain. We therefore study a generate-diagnose-repair paradigm that preserves the original generation capability and selectively repairs physical inconsistencies afterward, raising two key questions: what to repair and how to repair? To address them, we introduce PhysRepair, an agentic video generation framework that combines causal reasoning over the observed physical failure with prior repair experience to enable localized and adaptive correction. Specifically, Spatiotemporal Causal Constraints identify the first invalid physical state and its causally affected states, producing a repair specification that guides physically plausible local regeneration while preserving unaffected content. Experience-augmented In-context Learning combines the ongoing repair context with relevant experience from previous repairs, allowing the agent to adapt its repair decisions across diverse physical failures and accumulate completed trajectories for future cases. Experiments on Physics-IQ Verified across solid mechanics, fluid dynamics, optics, thermodynamics, and magnetism demonstrate that PhysRepair consistently improves the physical plausibility of generated videos over existing methods. Our project page is available at https://xxkkxxx.github.io/PhysRepair-Website/.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM
2026-10-16

Not Every Mention Is a Claim: Rethinking Object Hallucination Detection in Vision-Language Models

Not Every Mention Is a Claim: Rethinking Object Hallucination Detection in Vision-Language Models

Hsin-Ling Hsu、Neeraja Kirtane、Kuan-Hao Huang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Object hallucination in vision-language models (VLMs) is usually measured by checking each object a caption names against the image. Most benchmarks and detectors alike treat every object mention as a claim that the object is present. This assumption fails when a caption names an object without asserting it, as in "a bench for people to sit on", which mentions people without claiming that any are in the image. We call such mentions non-assertive. Treating them as hallucinations inflates the error rates attributed to models that use such language. To separate them from real hallucinations, we introduce NAME-Bench (Non-Assertive Mention Evaluation Benchmark), 25,497 object mentions from captions that five VLMs generate for COCO images. Each mention is labelled concrete (asserted and present), hallucinated (asserted and absent), or non-assertive, and non-assertive mentions are further divided into four subtypes: depictive, purpose, negation and hedged. All the hallucination detectors that we evaluate flag non-assertive mentions as hallucinations, at rates between 14% and 80%. Adding non-assertive mentions to detector training as negatives largely corrects this on LLaVA-1.5. On the four newer VLMs, a comparable correction takes five to ten times more data, and the detectors then catch fewer real hallucinations. These findings show that current detectors conflate mentioning an object with asserting it, and that this conflation is harder to undo on the four newer VLMs than on LLaVA-1.5. Hallucination evaluation should therefore separate what a caption asserts from what it merely mentions.

3D视觉
2026-10-16

Learning to Discriminate Scene Structures Makes Self-Supervised Depth Learning Scalable

Learning to Discriminate Scene Structures Makes Self-Supervised Depth Learning Scalable

Mengtan Zhang、Yuchuan Cui、Yu Ma、Zizhan Guo、Wei Ye 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Self-supervised monocular depth estimation is appealing for its potential to scale with unlabeled data, yet existing methods often degrade when trained on diverse scenes. This study finds that, under self-supervision, models tend to encode dataset-specific structural biases rather than transferable scene geometry, leading to cross-domain optimization conflicts. To address this issue, this study proposes the MaSS framework, aiming to make self-supervised depth learning scalable to diverse data. MaSS introduces a dedicated structural representation pathway explicitly decoupled from contextual features, which is guided by a novel scene-structure discrimination objective to learn generalized, layout-aware structural representations for depth decoding. Trained and evaluated on four diverse datasets, both individually and jointly, MaSS substantially outperforms prior self-supervised methods and is the first to consistently benefit from mixed-domain training. Zero-shot evaluation on eight additional datasets further demonstrates its remarkable generalizability, particularly on those entirely out-of-distribution scenes. The source code is publicly available at https://mias.group/MaSS.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM医学图像
2026-10-16

TAN: A Size- and Form-Adaptive Masked-Convolution Encoder for Tissue Graphs

TAN: A Size- and Form-Adaptive Masked-Convolution Encoder for Tissue Graphs

Jan Herschel、Anselm von Gladiss

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Tissue graphs represent histology images as graphs over tissue-conforming superpixels, which keeps homogeneous tissue regions intact instead of cutting them into rectangular patches. To embed superpixels, current encoders crop them to bounding boxes and then pad, resize, or mask them, so background or fill values can leak into the node features. This matters in practice, and especially in medical imaging, where a prediction should depend on the tissue and not on an arbitrary preprocessing choice: on BACH, changing only the fill of the masked region shifts the accuracy of frozen encoders by up to 6 percentage points, larger than the differences among the four best encoders under mean fill, and the preferred fill differs between datasets, so it cannot be fixed once and for all. We present TAN (Tissue-Aware Network), a compact convolutional encoder (0.41M parameters) that processes each superpixel at its native shape and extent and excludes background from every operation, which makes its embeddings exactly invariant to the fill by construction. Under identical masks and an identical graph classifier, TAN's accuracy on BACH is not significantly different from that of the 303M-parameter foundation model UNI (81.1% vs. 80.7-82.6%, depending on the fill), with ~740x fewer parameters, ~1,190x fewer FLOPs, and no pretraining. On BRACS, TAN is close to a ResNet18 trained under the same protocol and trails the histopathology foundation models. An ablation with TAN's architecture but standard processing of fill-padded crops shows that masking keeps accuracy similar (nominally higher on BACH, lower on BRACS, within noise) at less than half the FLOPs.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM医学图像
2026-10-16

When Evidence Vanishes: Distilling Visual-Evidence Dependency in Medical Vision-Language Models

When Evidence Vanishes: Distilling Visual-Evidence Dependency in Medical Vision-Language Models

Qizhen Lan、Yiling Ma、Ruiqi Chen、Mengchen Fan、Xi Xiao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Compact medical vision-language models (VLMs) can answer clinical questions correctly without reliably using the image, relying instead on question wording or learned priors. Standard knowledge distillation copies a teacher's answer preference on the original image, but it leaves unchecked whether that preference is conditional on the visual evidence required by the question. We study this missing target as medical visual evidence dependency: support for the correct answer should decrease when the matched evidence is absent. We introduce Evidence-Conditioned Dependency Distillation (ECDD), which pairs each training image with a control matched to the evidence level and distills the teacher's drop in answer margin across the pair. A lower bound objective transfers positive dependency by penalizing only shortfalls, while original-image supervision preserves task accuracy. The same formulation applies when the evidence is a queried clinical region, a surgical instrument, or whole-image visual content. Across three medical VQA settings, ECDD keeps original-image accuracy competitive with distillation and alignment baselines while making answer support more sensitive to evidence removal. Under the DME headline objective, for example, the positive-case margin response to target rather than random masking rises from 0.786 with KD to 2.023 with ECDD, while original-image accuracy differs by 0.11 points. These results suggest that medical VLM distillation should transfer both the teacher's answer preference and its dependence on visual evidence.

目标检测/分割遥感
2026-10-16

ShadowCast: On-the-Fly Context-Aware Shadow Augmentation for Remote Sensing Image Segmentation

ShadowCast: On-the-Fly Context-Aware Shadow Augmentation for Remote Sensing Image Segmentation

Yechan Kim、Dongho Yoon、Jongmin Joo、JongHyun Park、Moongu Jeon 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Shadows cast by elevated objects alter the spectral appearance and obscure the boundaries of objects in remote sensing images, thereby degrading semantic segmentation performance. Most existing approaches handle shadows as degradations to be detected and removed, requiring additional costs from dedicated networks and shadow-specific annotations. We propose ShadowCast, which takes the opposite approach: it synthesizes shadows and injects them during training for remote sensing segmentation. It extracts masks of elevated objects, such as buildings and trees, directly from the segmentation labels and estimates an image-specific cast-shadow direction cue by probing the brightness around them. It then projects synthetic shadows along the inferred cast-shadow direction, requiring no learned network and no acquisition metadata. Experiments on three widely used remote sensing benchmarks, Vaihingen, Potsdam, and LoveDA, show that ShadowCast consistently outperforms the baseline across diverse state-of-the-art segmentation architectures. Moreover, it brings additional performance gains when combined with standard augmentation and regularization techniques, indicating that stochastic shadow injection serves as a simple and complementary augmentation strategy for remote sensing segmentation.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM
2026-10-16

mmPencil: Toward Writing-Style-Independent In-Air Handwriting Recognition via mmWave Radar and Large Vision-Language Model

mmPencil: Toward Writing-Style-Independent In-Air Handwriting Recognition via mmWave Radar and Large Vision-Language Model

Yifan Guo、Zhu Wang、Qian Qin、Yangqian Lei、Qiwen Gan 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

In-air handwriting recognition based on radio frequency (RF) signals offers a promising solution for human-computer interaction, yet existing methods are often limited by predefined writing planes, constrained writing styles, and small recognition vocabularies. To address these limitations, we propose m^2VLMs, a modality mapping architecture that bridges millimeter-wave (mmWave) radar sensing and large vision-language models (VLMs), and develop mmPencil, a 3D in-air handwriting word recognition system. mmPencil reconstructs 3D handwriting trajectories from mmWave signals using a multi-stage spatial trajectory reconstruction algorithm and maps them into visual representations through a spatial-to-visual mapping algorithm, enabling VLMs to recognize handwriting while reducing sensitivity to writing styles and environmental factors. We collect and release a 3D mmWave handwriting dataset containing 7,664 samples of 200 words from 12 users across 22 writing scenarios. Extensive experiments demonstrate that mmPencil achieves robust word recognition under variations in writing position, range, angle, speed, size, direction, and user movement. It achieves 97.60% accuracy on 200 word classes for seen users and 92.50% zero-shot accuracy on 50 word classes for unseen users, outperforming state-of-the-art baselines.

In-air handwriting recognition based on radio frequency (RF) signals offers a promising solution for human-computer interaction, yet existing methods are often limited by predefined writing planes, constrained writing styles, and small recognition vocabularies. 多模态/VLM
2026-10-15

Towards Reconstructing Geographically Diverse Architecture with 3D Foundation Models

Towards Reconstructing Geographically Diverse Architecture with 3D Foundation Models

Nicky Kriplani、Yiwen Zhang、Angelina Wang、Hadar Averbuch-Elor

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Internet-scale photo collections of real-world landmarks have driven progress in 3D computer vision yet remain highly challenging for modern 3D foundation models (3DFMs) that estimate scene structure in a single feed-forward pass. In this work, we introduce ArchWorld, a benchmark of 3D architectural landmarks with high geographic coverage and rich metadata. We use this benchmark to conduct a thorough error analysis of current 3DFMs to understand where they fall short and how they can be improved. We examine socially relevant disparities in model error and find that model performance varies by geographic region; however, unlike typical fairness regimes, the trends only partially match training data representation. Our investigation reveals that the observed geographic disparities reflect not only differences in data representation and scene size but also differences in region-specific architectural features and background distractors that make scenes from different regions inherently vary in difficulty. Leveraging insights from these model failings, we introduce an intervention scheme that improves 3DFM performance while simultaneously reducing geographic disparities.

Internet-scale photo collections of real-world landmarks have driven progress in 3D computer vision yet remain highly challenging for modern 3D foundation models (3DFMs) that estimate scene structure in a single feed-forward pass. In this work, we introduce ArchWorld, a benchmark of 3D architectural landmarks with high geographic coverage and rich metadata. We use this benchmark to conduct a thorough error analysis of current 3DFMs to understand where they fall short and how they can be improved. We examine socially relevant disparities in model error and find that model performance varies by geographic region; however, unlike typical fairness regimes, the trends only partially match training data representation. KFS$^4$LAM: Adaptive KeyFrame Selection with Structure-Aware Mapping for Endoscopic Gaussian Splatting SLAM

KFS$^4$LAM: Adaptive KeyFrame Selection with Structure-Aware Mapping for Endoscopic Gaussian Splatting SLAM

Xinwei Ju、Zhehua Mao、Tianyi Song、Danail Stoyanov、Sophia Bano 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Gaussian-splatting Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (SLAM) augments conventional SLAM with a dense, renderable 3D scene representation, enabling high-quality reconstruction and novel-view synthesis. However, its localization, reconstruction, and rendering performance depends strongly on which observations are retained for mapping. Existing systems commonly rely on fixed temporal intervals or simple pose- and overlap-based criteria for keyframe selection. In colonoscopy, non-uniform camera motion, repetitive anatomy, specularities, and visual artifacts can make these criteria unreliable, leading to either redundant keyframes or missed informative viewpoints. We present KFS$^4$LAM (KeyFrame Selection with Structure-aware mapping for endoscopic Gaussian Splatting SLAM), which couples motion-guided adaptive keyframe selection with structurally supervised mapping and restricted temporal deformation. Accumulated motion guides adaptive keyframe selection, with complementary cues suppressing redundant or unreliable candidates. Density-aware mapping with anatomical contour supervision (colon folds) emphasizes structurally informative regions, while restricted temporal deformation accommodates local tissue dynamics without unconstrained geometric drift. We evaluate KFS$^4$LAM against Gaussian-SLAM baselines on C3VD and C3VD-v2, together with cross-backbone and component ablations. KFS$^4$LAM achieves strong overall performance across trajectory, rendering, and depth metrics, with pronounced gains on more challenging C3VD-v2 dataset. In the matched comparison with SplaTAM on C3VD-v2, it reduces rotational relative pose error by 20.3% and depth RMSE by 38.4%, while improving PSNR by 0.78 dB. Cross-backbone experiments further show that the framework is not tied to a single underlying SLAM architecture. Code and visualization results are available at https://xinweiju.github.io/KFSLAM/.

An Agentic ENF Framework for Forensic Video Authentication Against Generative AI

An Agentic ENF Framework for Forensic Video Authentication Against Generative AI

Ryan Wyton、Bacha Rehman

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Generative models now synthesise video that is hard to distinguish from genuine footage, and learned detectors that key on a generator's artefacts degrade sharply on unseen models. This paper develops a forensic framework that authenticates video by a signal that generation does not control: the electric network frequency (ENF), the mains-driven hum in audio and flicker in video. A unified two-channel detector extracts the ENF from audio and from rolling-shutter video through one second harmonic estimator and a shared network; a synthetic-injection training method, driven by an Ornstein-Uhlenbeck drift model and anchored to real recordings, overcomes the scarcity of labelled ENF data; a reference-matching channel verifies a claimed time of recording and recovers an unknown one; and a two-axis agentic orchestrator fuses recording authenticity with content-manipulation evidence under an asymmetric rule, escalating expensive detectors only when needed. A central finding reframes the task: an ENF-band flicker is present in generated video at a prominence indistinguishable from real footage, so its presence is not proof of authenticity, and authentication must rest on consistency with an independent reference rather than on presence. On held-out real recordings the detector accepts genuine audio and video at 93% and 100% against naive baselines of 53% and 15%; claimed-time authentication reaches an area under the curve of 1.0; and the agentic cascade matches a flat ensemble while cutting wall-clock time by 54% and frames decoded by 64%, without loss of accuracy.

ECHOES: Seeing Beyond the Current Image via Recursive History Compression and Privileged On-Policy Distillation

ECHOES: Seeing Beyond the Current Image via Recursive History Compression and Privileged On-Policy Distillation

Tengda Guo、Jiawang Hu、Yifei Tao、Yin Wang、Haotian Hu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Long-horizon GUI agents rely on visual history to maintain task states, but retaining historical screenshots increases visual-token usage and inference costs, while textual summaries can discard critical state information, undermining long-horizon reliability. We propose ECHOES, which leverages training-time privileged information to guide textual history compression and strengthen long-horizon reasoning. Specifically, ECHOES adopts a Compression-Think-Action (CTA) format to recursively update a textual history state and reason. The same model, given privileged multi-image inputs, serves as a teacher that provides dense token-level on-policy distillation on student-generated CTA rollouts. Our Perception-Memory Fidelity (PMF) Gap-guided OPD-RL framework dynamically balances privileged distillation and task-reward optimization, with periodic teacher updates supporting continued improvement. We further introduce ECHOESBMK, a fine-grained diagnostic benchmark derived from real GUI agent failure trajectories to jointly evaluate visual perception and cross-modal state retention. Experiments on four GUI benchmarks and ECHOESBMK demonstrate substantial gains in task performance and memory fidelity. Without retaining historical screenshots at deployment, ECHOES achieves competitive performance with a favorable performance-inference-cost trade-off and exhibits positive zero-shot transfer to unseen visual planning tasks.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM3D视觉
2026-10-16

Frame-Consistent Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting from Sparse Unposed Images

Frame-Consistent Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting from Sparse Unposed Images

Viruthshaan Vinasirajan、Ranga Rodrigo

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Feed-forward Gaussian models have made novel view synthesis almost instantaneous. Feed-forward methods predict Gaussians directly, either in two stages or in a single merged pass. Using two separately trained models is attractive because each can specialize in its own objective: one for accurate pose estimation and the other for high-quality reconstruction, rather than forcing a single jointly trained network to compromise between the two. A natural instantiation pairs a frozen pose estimator, VGGT, with a frozen reconstruction backbone, TokenGS. Prior work, such as VGGT-X and 3R-GS, also adopts a two-stage design, but attributes the resulting quality degradation to noisy pose estimates and introduces learned refinement. We show that this is not the primary source of the problem. Through per-camera analysis across 107 scenes from two datasets, DL3DV and MipNeRF360, using COLMAP poses as the reference, we find that the rotation error after alignment is small (median 1.6 degrees), whereas the translation scale is offset by a large, dataset-consistent factor. Controlled ablations over 140 DL3DV scenes show that the primary obstacles are two integration failures rather than pose error: a coordinate-frame inconsistency and a translation-scale mismatch, both of which can be corrected deterministically. With these fixes, a frozen VGGT driving a frozen TokenGS achieves 22.47 PSNR, within 0.51 dB of the COLMAP reference, with no additional training, when poses are estimated from all scene views. In the sparse setting, where reconstruction uses only four context images under the standard pose-free protocol, the same pipeline matches this all-view quality, yielding a fully COLMAP-free pipeline without any refinement step.

FlashReFocus: Interactive Image Refocusing in Seconds

FlashReFocus: Interactive Image Refocusing in Seconds

Ching-Heng Cheng、Chia-Ming Lee、Ming-Ching Chang、Xin Li、Yu-Lun Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Single-image refocusing changes the focal plane and depth of field of a photograph after capture. It is inherently interactive: users try many focal points and blur strengths on one image, so each edit should be fast and change only the blur. Recent generative methods, however, rerun large diffusion models for every edit. We observe that the two sub-problems differ in kind: recovering the all-in-focus image is ill-posed and needs a generative prior, whereas rendering blur is largely determined by depth and the focal plane. We therefore propose FlashReFocus, which runs diffusion once per photograph and renders every edit with a lightweight network. To address the ill-posed restoration, a one-step diffusion deblurrer with a decoder refiner recovers fine detail efficiently while balancing fidelity and perceptual quality. For precise control, the renderer is conditioned on an explicit defocus map and uses defocus-bias attention, which lets pixels at similar defocus share information without interference from pixels at other depths; in-focus content stays unchanged, and editing the map yields tilt-shift and two focal planes. To further test generalization to unseen cameras, we collect 3CReal, a three-camera benchmark of 89 pairs. FlashReFocus achieves the best perceptual fidelity for zero-shot bokeh rendering on EBB! and 3CReal, refocuses 148.3x faster than GenRefocus, and takes about half a second per further edit, 462x faster.

Reliability-Consistent Appearance Modeling for RGB-Infrared Multi-Object Tracking

Reliability-Consistent Appearance Modeling for RGB-Infrared Multi-Object Tracking

Bingyang Wang、Jiazuo Yu、Lu Zhang、Lihe Zhang、Huchuan Lu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Recent RGB-infrared multi-object tracking (MOT) methods have substantially improved cross-modal representation learning and multimodal information integration. However, most existing methods compress RGB and infrared evidence into a unified descriptor. When one modality is temporarily degraded, the tracker can no longer regulate its contribution separately; unreliable evidence may distort association and contaminate appearance memory. We therefore propose RCA-MOT, a reliability-consistent appearance modeling framework that preserves cross-modal complementarity while retaining post-representation evidence controllability. RGB-specific, infrared-specific, and fused identity representations are maintained as jointly accessible appearance states. For each detection, a simple training-free target-scene estimator produces branch-wise appearance reliability used across the two temporal roles of appearance: relative reliability regulates different identity branches, absolute reliability calibrates borderline candidates near the ReID acceptance boundary, and the same branch-wise reliability controls how strongly matched descriptors update historical appearance memory. The individual components are intentionally simple, focusing evaluation on reliability-controllable appearance organization rather than elaborate fusion or learned reliability-estimation modules. RCA-MOT sets new SOTA results on VT-MOT and UniRTL, improving the previous best HOTA by 5.402/6.075 points and IDF1 by 11.549/12.789 points, respectively, while post-hoc analyses further validate reliability as an informative control signal. Code will be released upon acceptance.

2026-10-16

Simultaneous Force and Motion Control in Generalised Coordinates

Simultaneous Force and Motion Control in Generalised Coordinates

Jon Woolfrey

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robot manipulators often come into contact with rigid objects when performing work. This imposes physical constraints on their motion. Simultaneous force & motion control was developed to regulate contact forces commensurate with these constraints, whilst projecting motion control in to the orthogonal, unconstrained directions. This is typically formulated in the task coordinates, i.e. operational space, then mapped to joint torques to command the robot. However, most constraints on the robot control, such as joint limits, collision avoidance, singularity avoidance, etc. are naturally expressed in generalised coordinates (joint space). Thus, formulating the control problem in task space makes it difficult to guarantee control feasibility. In this paper Gauss' principle of least constraint is used to solve the control as a quadratic programming (QP) problem in generalised coordinates. It is shown that the theoretical solution to this problem produces the correct constrained dynamics for accurate contact force control. Moreover, inequality constraints may be appended to ensure feasibility and safety. Simulation results were conducted in MuJoCo and compared to the operational space formulation. The proposed method is able to simultaneously maintain a desired contact force, minimise trajectory tracking error, and adhere to practical inequality constraints.

Direct Conditional Parameterization for N-Dimensional Splatting

Direct Conditional Parameterization for N-Dimensional Splatting

Zhongpai Gao、Benjamin Planche、Van Nguyen Nguyen、Meng Zheng、Anwesa Choudhuri 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

N-dimensional splatting extends 3D Gaussian Splatting with view direction and time to model view-dependent and dynamic effects. Each primitive is lifted into a joint distribution over position and conditioning variables, then conditionally sliced at each rendering query. N-DGS and UBS share this step across Gaussian and Beta kernels: recovering the conditional mean, opacity, and covariance requires per-primitive matrix inversions and regression products in every forward pass. We introduce direct conditional parameterization, replacing this covariance-derived readout with explicit per-effect parameters: a Cholesky precision factor for opacity and a factorized query-to-position regression operator with learnable per-dimension coupling. Unrestricted conditional coordinates recover a valid joint covariance; our factorization adds spatial-scale and coupling priors. Applied to both kernels, the parameterization yields dGS (Gaussian) and dBS (Beta). Across five static and dynamic benchmarks, they match or exceed their covariance-derived counterparts on quality, with gains up to +1.26 dB in the main MCMC evaluation. Production slicing is 6.9-7.7 times faster than on-the-fly covariance-derived conditioning; complete-render profiling gives 1.25-1.91 times speedups on four trained scenes. Direct conditioning provides a structured, kernel-agnostic alternative for N-dimensional splatting. Project page: https://gaozhongpai.github.io/dgs/.

HilbertGen-3D:Hilbert--Multifractal Conditioning for Topology-Aware 3D Generation

HilbertGen-3D:Hilbert--Multifractal Conditioning for Topology-Aware 3D Generation

Ziyi Zhang、Shixuan Li、Yorie Nakahira、Paul Bogdan、Guannan Qu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

High-fidelity 3D generative modeling underpins game asset creation, medical imaging, and VR/AR environmental design, where geometric structure directly influences perception and decision-making. Diffusion models represent a powerful paradigm for point cloud generation. However, existing approaches rely heavily on local voxel or patch features and often miss global structural organization, and much less work has studied how global conditioning can be used to improve generation quality. In this paper, we introduce HilbertGen-3D, a novel topology-aware conditional diffusion framework based on Hilbert-Multifractal Conditioning (HMC). HMC computes multi-scale voxel measures, serializes them via a 3D Hilbert curve to expose structured spatial dependencies, and estimates the generalized fractal dimensions from scale-dependent partition functions. We encode these signals as global embeddings and multi-scale tokens, and inject them into a diffusion denoiser through an efficient bottleneck fusion module. Across the Chair, Airplane, and Car categories of ShapeNet, our two conditioning variants reduce the absolute deviation of 1-Nearest Neighbor Accuracy (1-NNA) from 50% by up to 8.90 percentage points and increase coverage by up to 5.88 percentage points relative to TopoDiT-3D. This shows that combining local density cues with global Hilbert-multifractal structure provides a simple and effective recipe for high-fidelity and diverse 3D generation.

Deep Learning for Deepfake Image Detection: Comparative Model Evaluation and Gradient-Based Visual Attribution

Deep Learning for Deepfake Image Detection: Comparative Model Evaluation and Gradient-Based Visual Attribution

Saad Korchi、Abderrahim Waga

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Synthetic facial imagery has made the distinction between authentic and generated content increasingly important for digital forensics and online trust. This study examines an explainable deep-learning workflow for image-level deepfake detection using two contrasting classification strategies: a compact convolutional model trained from scratch and an ImageNet-pretrained MobileNetV2 model adapted through two-stage transfer learning. Experiments were conducted with the balanced 140k Real and Fake Faces dataset, comprising 140,000 facial images. Under the recorded validation protocol, the augmented from-scratch model reached 84.15% accuracy (loss 0.3625), compared with 95.90% accuracy (loss 0.1074) for the fine-tuned pretrained model. Gradient-weighted class activation mapping (Grad-CAM) was used to visualize image regions associated with predictions, and a Flask-based prototype was developed for side-by-side inspection. These observations support the practical value of pretrained visual representations in the studied setting while underscoring the need to interpret validation performance alongside dataset provenance, explanation limitations, and evaluation scope. The reported results are validation results, not held-out test estimates.

2026-10-15

LGGS: Longitudinal MRI Reconstruction via Guided Group Sparsity

LGGS: Longitudinal MRI Reconstruction via Guided Group Sparsity

Nikola Janjušević、Hersh Chandarana、Yao Wang、Li Feng

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Serial MRI is routinely performed in current clinical practice for disease surveillance and applications such as MR-guided radiotherapy. However, long MRI scan times increase costs and limit patient access, making accelerated image reconstruction of broad clinical importance. Previous scans of the same patient contain image information highly correlated with the current exam, yet existing reconstruction methods rarely exploit this information. Major challenges in the effective use of prior scans include inter-visit misregistration, pathological changes, and image phase variation. In this work, we propose Longitudinal Guided Group Sparsity (LGGS), an interpretable deep-learning framework for longitudinally informed MRI reconstruction derived from classical group sparsity. The prior scan is incorporated into the reconstruction regularizer through learned phase-invariant circulant attention that identifies corresponding features across exams. This formulation provides robustness to local in-plane and through-plane misregistration as well as inter-visit phase variation. Across acceleration rates, LGGS achieves state-of-the-art performance on longitudinal T1-weighted multi-coil brain MRI data, outperforming the evaluated unguided and longitudinally informed reconstruction methods, with substantially fewer learned parameters. We further evaluated the contributions of multiple guide slices and phase-invariant similarity and demonstrated that accelerated prior images can also serve as effective guides with minimal degradation in reconstruction quality.

Serial MRI is routinely performed in current clinical practice for disease surveillance and applications such as MR-guided radiotherapy. However, long MRI scan times increase costs and limit patient access, making accelerated image reconstruction of broad clinical importance. Previous scans of the same patient contain image information highly correlated with the current exam, yet existing reconstruction methods rarely exploit this information. Major challenges in the effective use of prior scans include inter-visit misregistration, pathological changes, and image phase variation. In this work, we propose Longitudinal Guided Group Sparsity (LGGS), an interpretable deep-learning framework for longitudinally informed MRI reconstruction derived from classical group sparsity. The prior scan is incorporated into the reconstruction regularizer through learned phase-invariant circulant attention that identifies corresponding features across exams. This formulation provides robustness to local in-plane and through-plane misregistration as well as inter-visit phase variation. Across acceleration rates, LGGS achieves state-of-the-art performance on longitudinal T1-weighted multi-coil brain MRI data, outperforming the evaluated unguided and longitudinally informed reconstruction methods, with substantially fewer learned parameters. We further evaluated the contributions of multiple guide slices and phase-invariant similarity and demonstrated that accelerated prior images can also serve as effective guides with minimal degradation in reconstruction quality.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM机器人视觉遥感
2026-10-15

FCTS: Failure-Guided Curriculum Tree Search Over Generated Terrain for Legged Parkour

FCTS: Failure-Guided Curriculum Tree Search Over Generated Terrain for Legged Parkour

Narayanan Palghat Parameswaran、Dong-Ki Kim、Jay Patrikar、Santiago Gutierrez、Harshad Zade 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Quadruped parkour policies handle canonical obstacles well, but their training curricula often miss the irregular, compound geometry of industrial and disaster-response sites, where agile locomotion matters most. Such environments pose a hard generalization problem: even training directly on reconstructed geometry from the target site does not reliably yield policies that traverse it. Hand-designed curricula span only a few terrain families, and recent LLM-generated curricula offer little control over which policy weaknesses they target. We propose FCTS, a failure-conditioned terrain curriculum framework that uses localized traversal failures to automatically generate the terrains a policy should train on next. Each failure's location, termination mode, robot state, and surrounding terrain geometry condition this generation, and Monte Carlo Tree Search explores alternative curriculum trajectories under a fixed training budget. On terrains built from scanned real-world construction sites, FCTS raises success rate from 28.4% to 85.8% on a medium-difficulty course and from 14.9% to 44.7% on a hard course relative to the strongest parkour or LLM-curriculum baselines, and maintains higher progress on extreme terrains where full traversal is rare. Hardware experiments on a quadruped robot confirm that the learned FCTS policies transfer to the real world.

Three LiDARs, Twenty-Five Datasets: Cross-Source Generalization in Tree-Species Recognition

Three LiDARs, Twenty-Five Datasets: Cross-Source Generalization in Tree-Species Recognition

Jae Joong Lee、Bedrich Benes

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

FOR-species20K is the largest public LiDAR benchmark for tree-species classification, pooling 25 independently collected terrestrial, mobile, and airborne laser-scanning datasets across 33 species. We introduce a multi-resolution, multi-view ConvNeXtV2 model that improves official hidden-test accuracy from 79.5% to 85.0%, ranking first at the time of submission. We then examine what this score measures. Because the hidden test is species-balanced, its overall accuracy is more comparable to a macro metric than to accuracy under the public set's long-tailed distribution. Moreover, the official random split places every source dataset in both training and evaluation. A logistic probe recovers source identity from out-of-fold model probabilities with 69% accuracy, while geometric features identify the source above 90% within two widely shared species; 18 of the 33 species occur in only one source. Under a source-species-cell holdout, evaluated on 5,566 trees from eight eligible species that retain the same LiDAR-type training support in other sources, the unweighted species mean falls from 82% to 36%, and tree-weighted accuracy falls from 95% to 60%. Count-matched retraining and experiments with two alternative backbones indicate that this gap is not explained by reduced training support alone, while an external PureForest experiment shows a smaller but consistent grouped-split effect. These results establish the 85% leaderboard score as strong in-distribution performance, but not as an estimate of source-disjoint generalization, motivating balanced metrics and source-aware evaluation. The code is available at: https://github.com/JaeLee18/FOR20K-lidar-tree-species

Stuck on Shuffle: On Attribute-Object Binding in Vision-Language Models

Stuck on Shuffle: On Attribute-Object Binding in Vision-Language Models

Netanel Y. Tamir、Michael Green、Issar Tzachor、Or Shimshi、Dvir Samuel 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Retrieval is a fundamental task in multimodal systems: given an image or text query, the goal is to find the most relevant images or texts in a collection, often using a shared embedding space. Reliable retrieval requires these embeddings to capture not only which concepts are present, but also how they are composed. One key compositional challenge is attribute--object binding: encoding which attributes belong to which objects. Existing benchmarks provide a limited view of this capability, typically covering a subset of modality pairings, simple two-object swaps, or small attribute sets. Consequently, it remains unclear how robustly current models preserve attribute--object bindings across different modality pairings and more complex settings. We therefore introduce SABER, a controlled benchmark for evaluating attribute--object binding across all four image and text modality pairings, spanning diverse attribute types and scenes with up to ten objects. Evaluating models on SABER reveals substantial binding failures in both CLIP-style dual encoders and recent MLLM-based embedders. These findings motivate us to ask how binding can be improved efficiently. While multimodal supervision may be effective, collecting suitable images is difficult and costly. Text, on the other hand, is well suited to binding supervision: it is cheap to generate, supports a broader range of attributes and enables precisely controlled hard negatives. We thus finetune an MLLM-based embedder exclusively on synthetic attribute-binding text. Despite the absence of image supervision, the gains transfer to cross-modal and image--image retrieval while preserving general multimodal embedding performance. Our model achieves SOTA results on SABER while also improving on negation and instance-retrieval benchmarks, demonstrating broader benefits of our binding-focused supervision.

Embodiment-Classifier Guidance: Transferring Frozen VLAs Across Robot Dynamics without Demonstrations

Embodiment-Classifier Guidance: Transferring Frozen VLAs Across Robot Dynamics without Demonstrations

Devin Han、Hershraj Niranjani

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Flow-based policies such as $π_0$ are trained on a fixed set of embodiments, and transferring them to new ones is an open problem. To adapt a frozen $π_0$ to a new embodiment without additional demonstrations or parameter updates, we train a lightweight embodiment classifier to distinguish successful action chunks on the source body from those on the target body, using a few calibration tasks. On transfer tasks that $π_0$ saw on its source body but the classifier was never trained on, we nudge each denoising step of $π_0$ with the classifier's gradient, which estimates the direction from the distribution of successful actions on the source body toward that on the target body. On the LIBERO simulation benchmark with four modified controllers, embodiment-classifier guidance raises $π_0$'s average success on the target bodies from 72% to 80% across 12 suite-controller pairs, with a 31% relative reduction in failures (11 of 12 gain; 5 significant after Holm correction). In simulation, guidance is data-efficient, needing few target-body rollouts per task, which makes it promising for sim-to-real transfer, where rollouts are costly. Guidance is mixed on weak wrists and harmful on a locked wrist, when the classifier lacks data or favors infeasible motions. Collecting more target-body data with guided $π_0$ can fill such gaps: on one weak-wrist arm, guided $π_0$ solved a task on which unguided $π_0$ had zero successes in the original data, and retraining the classifier on the guided successes raised success from 52.5% to 70.7%.

Expand-and-Prune: Test-Time Candidate-Space Adaptation for End-to-End Autonomous Driving

Expand-and-Prune: Test-Time Candidate-Space Adaptation for End-to-End Autonomous Driving

Fabian Schmidt、Karol Fedurko、Markus Enzweiler、Abhinav Valada

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Candidate-based end-to-end driving planners reason over a finite set of trajectory hypotheses. However, for sparse planners, a limited candidate set may provide insufficient support for scene-relevant maneuvers, while fixed downstream candidate budgets do not account for prediction uncertainty. To address these challenges, we introduce Expand-and-Prune, a test-time framework that jointly adapts candidate support and capacity without updating learned parameters. The framework expands the native candidate set using semantic driving intent and temporally propagated predictions and then uses uncertainty in intermediate trajectory scores to adaptively prune the resulting candidates. Across four heterogeneous candidate-based planners and four benchmarks, Expand-and-Prune consistently improves planning performance. It achieves state-of-the-art results on LangAuto, increasing the Driving Score from 68.2 to 75.9, while providing further improvement on Bench2Drive, Fail2Drive, and NAVSIMv2, with particularly strong improvements in out-of-distribution scenarios. Moreover, in its full instantiation, 72.5% of selected trajectories originate from the newly introduced intent-guided or temporal candidates, confirming that candidate-space expansion provides useful hypotheses beyond those generated natively by the planner. The code is available at https://github.com/iis-esslingen/Expand-and-Prune.

2026-10-14

ESC: Escaping 2D Diffusion Ambiguity with Geometric Constraints for Consistent Novel View Synthesis

ESC: Escaping 2D Diffusion Ambiguity with Geometric Constraints for Consistent Novel View Synthesis

Ge Ya Luo、Daniel Severo、Brian Karrer、Christopher Pal、Adriana Romero-Soriano 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Generating 3D-consistent novel views from 2D diffusion models remains a fundamental challenge in computer vision. In this work, we introduce a framework that enhances geometric alignment for monocular view synthesis under drastic camera changes by jointly generating a target view and its corresponding depth map. By conditioning the diffusion pipeline on the single source image, source depth, and target camera rays, our approach forces the model to inherently reason in 3D space even under extreme viewpoint shifts. To guarantee spatial fidelity, we propose a novel per-pixel depth consistency constraint, which verifies and enforces 3D consistency across generated views. Our framework establishes a robust pipeline for geometrically consistent view synthesis, yielding superior visual accuracy and spatial alignment compared to current state-of-the-art methods. With this work, we pave the road towards robust 3D world models capable of understanding complex environments from a single view image.

Synthetic Image Oversampling for Rare-Class Representation Learning in Deep Facial Phenotyping

Synthetic Image Oversampling for Rare-Class Representation Learning in Deep Facial Phenotyping

Miles Liao、Zhanliang Wang、Hongzhuo Chen、Kai Wang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Recent deep facial phenotyping methods can support the recognition of genetic syndromes, but patient images are often scarce and unevenly distributed across disorders. Synthetic facial images offer a way to supplement the training data without collecting additional patient photographs. Their usefulness, however, depends on whether they improve recognition of real patients: generated images may introduce artifacts or repeat features already represented in the training set. It is therefore necessary to examine both how much synthetic augmentation to use and which disorders benefit. We investigate disease-specific synthetic oversampling using images from the Phenotypic Disease Image Database to augment a subset of the GestaltMatcher Database. We compare increasing augmentation levels in an image-based model, GestaltMatcher-Arc, and a multimodal model, GestaltMML, while keeping the real training and patient-disjoint evaluation partitions fixed. Repeated training runs assess overall and disease-specific accuracy, and an exploratory embedding analysis examines changes in class compactness and separation. Augmentation improves mean recognition accuracy in both training setups, although gains remain modest in GestaltMatcher-Arc and continue across the tested levels in GestaltMML. These aggregate improvements are accompanied by uneven disease-level responses, including reduced accuracy for some disorders. In the embedding analysis, disorders with fewer real training images tend to gain more class separation, but geometric changes do not consistently track classification performance. Together, the findings support synthetic oversampling for limited-data facial phenotyping and emphasize the need to assess its amount and benefit for individual disorders within each training setup.

FedRAPP: Federated Test-Time Prompt Tuning for Vision-Language Models with Reliability-Aware Prompt Personalization

FedRAPP: Federated Test-Time Prompt Tuning for Vision-Language Models with Reliability-Aware Prompt Personalization

Syed Md. Ahnaf Hasan、Md Akil Raihan Iftee、Sajib Mistry、Amin Ahsan Ali、AKM Mahbubur Rahman 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Federated prompt learning enables vision-language models like CLIP to collaboratively adapt lightweight soft prompts across distributed clients without sharing raw data. However, existing federated prompt learning methods are designed primarily for supervised settings with labeled client data and assumed relatively stable training distributions. At test time, source-free federated prompt tuning remains largely unexplored. Clients may receive unlabeled target samples with heterogeneous, often non-IID distributions that differ substantially across clients and from the source training distribution, causing locally adapted prompts to drift in different directions and reducing the effectiveness of federated aggregation. In this work, we explore Source-Free Federated Test-Time Prompt Tuning (SF-FTTPT), where distributed clients collaboratively adapt soft prompts on unlabeled test streams without access to source data. Our study reveals that conventional federated aggregation methods struggle under heterogeneous client distributions, as locally adapted prompts become increasingly specialized and difficult to combine effectively. To address this challenge, we propose FedRAPP, a personalized aggregation framework that combines reliability-aware client assessment, server-side exponential moving-average prompt memory, and similarity-guided peer aggregation. FedRAPP selectively incorporates information from reliable and compatible clients while preserving local specialization, enabling more robust adaptation under distribution shift. Extensive experiments across nine CLIP benchmark datasets and ImageNet variants demonstrate that FedRAPP consistently outperforms standard federated aggregation baselines under heterogeneous client distributions while maintaining the efficiency advantages of prompt-only adaptation.

When Plans Change, Remember: Replanning-Guided Visual Memory for Vision-Language-Action Models

When Plans Change, Remember: Replanning-Guided Visual Memory for Vision-Language-Action Models

Victoria Smelova、Daniil Kazachkov、Georgiy Kazantsev、Iuliia Tsvetkova、Egor Cherepanov 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Long-horizon, multi-stage Vision-Language-Action (VLA) policies often require visual information that is no longer present in the current observation. Retaining the complete observation history is computationally expensive and highly redundant, motivating selective visual memory. We introduce RePlanMem-VLA (Replanning-Guided Keyframe Memory for VLA), which turns a policy's own plan revisions into an online memory-write signal. Under receding-horizon execution, consecutive action chunks predict some of the same future actions. RePlanMem-VLA treats unusually large disagreement between these temporally aligned predictions as evidence of a task-relevant event. We first compare 35 disagreement measures across single-task autoregressive decoder-only and Flow-Matching Transformers trained from scratch, then validate the selected signals on pretrained OpenVLA-OFT and $π_{0.5}$ policies over MIKASA-Robo-VLA tasks. The resulting detectors achieve ROC-AUC of 0.981-0.997. We then use the selected signals in RePlanMem-VLA to trigger updates to a bounded visual memory, with a robot-masked write filter suppressing redundant frames. In the evaluated memory-retention experiments, RePlanMem-VLA preserves task-relevant observations until the later decision point in 100% of episodes. Real-robot replay data further show that event selection and redundancy suppression extend beyond simulation. These results establish plan revision as a policy-internal signal for controlling what a VLA remembers. Website: https://vika787.github.io/RePlanMem/.

2026-10-15

Generative AI Produces and Transmits Beauty Bias

Generative AI Produces and Transmits Beauty Bias

AJ Alvero、Deepak Varuvel Dennison、Alexandra Crespin、Heeyon Kim、Alejandra Regla-Vargas 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Societies reward people judged to be attractive by standards intertwined with gender and race. In this paper, we examine if and how generative AI may contribute to these dynamics. We audited 27 text-to-image generators released between 2022 and 2026 using simple prompts that never mention attractiveness or beauty, analyzing 51,300 generated faces and 160,196 photographs of real people across primary and supplementary comparisons. We used three automated measures and collected ratings from 550 human raters on a subset of images. Nearly all generators produced faces scoring above photographed faces under automated measures, and human raters judged the outputs of all 23 generators as significantly more attractive than photographed faces, even after correction for multiple comparisons. When focusing our analysis on gender, we find that 37% of female-prompted generated faces outscored the median professional fashion model. Across racial, ethnic, and national categories, groups associated with darker skin tones tended to receive lower attractiveness scores than groups associated with lighter skin tones. In a preregistered hiring simulation experiment (N = 570), exposure to AI-generated faces selected for high human-rated attractiveness lowered hireability and trustworthiness ratings and reduced interview advancement for an identical test set of candidates paired with photographs of real people; exposure to retouched photographs produced no such effect. Generative AI can reproduce racial hierarchies and gendered patterns of beauty and alter the context in which real people are judged.

目标检测/分割3D视觉机器人视觉
2026-10-15

RTISSLAM-3D: Full 6-DoF SLAM With an In-Air 3D Imaging Sonar

RTISSLAM-3D: Full 6-DoF SLAM With an In-Air 3D Imaging Sonar

Jan Steckel、Vincent Katter、Wouter Jansen

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Echolocating bats navigate dense forests using sonar alone, and in-air 3D imaging sonars now capture the entire frontal hemisphere with every pulse. Sonar SLAM, however, has so far been planar: current systems estimate a 2D trajectory and discard the elevation information in the sonar images, an assumption that does not hold on rough and sloped terrain such as a forest floor. In this paper, we introduce RTISSLAM-3D, a novel 6-DoF SLAM system for a wheeled robot that carries an in-air 3D imaging sonar, an IMU and wheel encoders. A CFAR detector on the sphere reduces every sonar image to a sparse set of 3D detections, which we use for a 3D acoustic odometry that models the shape of the sonar beam, for place recognition with polar submap descriptors, and for the verification of loop closures by 3D registration, all combined in a 6-DoF pose graph with attitude factors. We evaluated RTISSLAM-3D on a 545m drive through a forest, with a 3D lidar SLAM as reference. The loop closures reduced the horizontal error of the acoustic odometry from 1.74m to 0.28m, with a height error of 0.12m, and 94.6% of the resulting 3D sonar map lies within 0.3 m of the lidar map.

TriSAR-3D and PaintFusion: A Novel Trimodal LiDAR-RGB-Thermal Dataset and Method for 3D Person Detection in Search and Rescue

TriSAR-3D and PaintFusion: A Novel Trimodal LiDAR-RGB-Thermal Dataset and Method for 3D Person Detection in Search and Rescue

Markus Kramer、Jan Wachenfeld、Oskar von Stryk

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Reliable 3D person detection is important for rescue robotics to give first responders situational awareness. Yet most multimodal 3D detection research targets automotive scenarios in which pedestrians mostly appear in a narrow range of upright, near-road poses. It is therefore unclear how much of the reported performance transfers to rescue settings, as a person may be lying, partially occluded, or in unstructured indoor and outdoor environments. To investigate this gap, we introduce TriSAR-3D, a multimodal person detection dataset recorded with a high-resolution 128-channel LiDAR, an RGB camera, and a thermal camera. It captures varied poses in indoor as well as outdoor locations at different times of day to study their influence on 3D object detector performance. Furthermore, we propose a novel trimodal 3D detection pipeline, PaintFusion, that fuses PointPainting-based evidence using a simple MLP. We show that the derived method reaches 65.4 AP@50 and thereby outperforms strong LiDAR-only (CenterPoint, 53.4), RGB+LiDAR (BEVFusion, 60.5), and adapted trimodal (Tri-BEVFusion, 60.7) baselines on the dataset. At the same time, it is substantially faster than other fusion-based methods such as BEVFusion, with a per-frame latency of 51.6 ms compared to 101 ms. Additionally, we test how the performance of different 3D object detectors generalizes to unseen pose categories. Removing lying instances from training data collapses the lying-pose AP@50 to near zero across all evaluated detectors. The results indicate that dataset diversity exerts a large effect on 3D object detection performance. The dataset and code are available at https://github.com/MarkusKramer1/3D-Trimodal-People-Detection.

Star Light, Star Bright: Can Multimodal Models Parse an Astronomy Visual Question Answering Dataset Right?

Star Light, Star Bright: Can Multimodal Models Parse an Astronomy Visual Question Answering Dataset Right?

J. P. Naiman

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Figures are essential for scientific communication, and the field of Visual Question Answering (VQA) aims to test the figure understanding capabilities of large multimodal models (LMMs). Here, we investigate the accuracy of several low-cost LMMs on domain-specific VQA tasks for astronomical images of the sky. As instrument for this task we build a domain-specific VQA dataset comprising 2001 partially-synthetic figures divided evenly between contour plots, real telescope images drawn from NASA's SkyView, and "fake" sky images which share the axes and angular scales of the real images but draw their colors from a gaussian mixture model, alongside 47357 questions. Each figure is released as pristine and with realistic page-aging degradations mimicking the historical astronomical corpus. Testing three low-cost proprietary LMMs, we find models frequently fail to answer domain-specific questions at high accuracy. An initial comparison with 104 human-verified, astrometrically tagged figures from historical journal publications shows similar median errors in angular extent. All code is available on GitHub: https://github.com/jnaiman/SkyImagesWASP2026 and dataset is available on HuggingFace: https://huggingface.co/datasets/ReadingTimeMachine/astro_sky_image_vqa.

A Unified Theory of Image Scaling: Point Spread Functions, Discrete Kernels, and Scale-Spaces

A Unified Theory of Image Scaling: Point Spread Functions, Discrete Kernels, and Scale-Spaces

Assaf Shocher

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We derive the optically aligned system of image-scaling operators $\mathbf{K}_s$ that reproduces optical zoom (exact zoom-out, canonical zoom-in), by identifying a Huygens-like consistency principle in which each pixel acts as a secondary source. The system is determined in closed form by the camera's point spread function (PSF), itself the operator $\mathbf{K}_0$. The operators compose, $\mathbf{K}_{s'} \circ \mathbf{K}_s = \mathbf{K}_{ss'}$ (with band projection for down-up), and account for both upscaling and downscaling, which form a Moore-Penrose pseudoinverse pair, $\mathbf{K}_{1/s} = \mathbf{K}_s^\dagger$. We show that image scaling, as routinely practiced, is neither optically faithful nor internally consistent; for example, common anti-aliasing rules generally correspond to no camera, and two resizes by a factor of $1/2$ generally do not equal one resize by a factor of $1/4$. Gaussian scale-space kernels cannot be exact finite-aperture PSFs. Gaussian scale-space is an idealized special case of what emerges here: a generalized scale-space for sampled images, derived entirely from camera optics, bidirectional, and not limited to Gaussian kernels. The system goes beyond scaling and supports composable and invertible subpixel geometric transformations. We empirically validate its optical predictions with real captures and noisy optics simulations, and demonstrate its practical impact on the stability of tasks such as super-resolution and classification.

CF-NODE: Counterfactual Spatial Node Intervention for Test-Time Adaptation Under Domain Shift, Evaluated Across Five Diabetic Retinopathy Datasets

CF-NODE: Counterfactual Spatial Node Intervention for Test-Time Adaptation Under Domain Shift, Evaluated Across Five Diabetic Retinopathy Datasets

Sabarish G、Deepak Parashar

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Automated fundus-based diabetic retinopathy (DR) grading can substantially expand screening capacity, but source-trained models degrade sharply across different cameras, clinics, and patient populations. Existing test-time adaptation methods depend on entropy minimization or target information accumulated from image batches and streams. These mechanisms can reinforce confidently incorrect predictions and break down when clinical images arrive independently. We introduce CF-NODE, a novel counterfactual test-time adaptation framework for cross- domain DR grading. Instead of trusting prediction confidence or estimating a target distribution, CF-NODE asks how each prediction responds under a controlled intervention on the evidence supporting it. CF-NODE ranks internal spatial nodes using model-derived attention, feature magnitude, and node-set similarity, progressively attenuates the highest-ranked nodes, and uses the stability of the resulting expected-grade response to apply a bounded calibration of the predicted grade distribution. The framework is fully frozen and adapts every image independently using only geometric views and a fixed source-derived prior, requiring no target-derived information or model updates. We evaluate CF-NODE on five external DR datasets under repeated-seed evaluation against TENT, EATA, SAR, and T3A. CF-NODE outperforms all four methods with statistically significant improvements over the second-best method, improves AUC on every target domain, and attains the highest average AUC and macro-F1, delivering stronger grade discrimination together with more balanced recognition across DR severity levels. These results demonstrate that counterfactual intervention sensitivity provides consistent and deployment-ready domain robustness for single-image clinical inference.

Adv-Camou: An Emissivity-based Physical Dynamic Adversarial Patch for Infrared Attack

Adv-Camou: An Emissivity-based Physical Dynamic Adversarial Patch for Infrared Attack

Zhuo Chen、Xu Zheng、Runyun He、Tuoyu Liu、Kaixuan Ding 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

With the widespread application of infrared object detection technologies in military domains, intelligent infrared adversarial camouflage capabilities have become critical. Existing physical attack methods predominantly employ static thermal suppression strategies, suffering from limited environmental adaptability, insufficient attack robustness, and inadequate visual concealment. This study proposes Adv-Camou, an emissivity-based physical dynamic adversarial patch that achieves paradigm-shifting advancements from static to dynamic attacks through algorithm-material co-innovation. First, a multi-level loss function based on feature confusion mechanisms is designed to deceive detectors by attenuating target saliency and enforcing background feature alignment. Second, adaptive physical transformations and differentiable binarization optimization are integrated to address physical robustness and realizability constraints. A 6*4 array of reversible electrochromic emissivity device is developed and tested across a range of background scenarios, enabling scene-adaptive dynamic camouflage through independent unit control. Experiments demonstrate that this method achieves 95.59% attack success rate (ASR), reducing average precision (AP) by 60.51%. Physical validations show that static adversarial patches attain 76.55% average ASR under multi-view, multi-range, and multi-spectral conditions, while dynamic patches enhance ASR up to 91.55% in complex environments (wilderness/urban) through adaptive switching. The seamless digital-to-physical transfer via tunable emissivity devices transcends the limitations of traditional static camouflage by establishing a dynamic infrared regulation capability. This advancement enhances the battlefield survivability of military assets and demonstrates transformative potential for adaptive camouflage in practical combat scenarios.

Case-Specific Priors-Guided Multimodal Fusion for Prognosis Prediction in Head and Neck Cancer

Case-Specific Priors-Guided Multimodal Fusion for Prognosis Prediction in Head and Neck Cancer

Yue Lin、Shuchang Ye、Shenghai Wu、Dashun Zheng、Mingyuan Meng 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Accurate prognosis prediction for head and neck cancer typically necessitates integrating highly heterogeneous clinical and pathological evidence by sophisticated multimodal fusion. However, existing fusion techniques are usually grounded in case-generic clinical rationales about complementary synergies across modalities, underestimating case-specific variance. In this study, we propose a prognosis prediction framework that explicitly incorporates Case-specific Priors to guide Multimodal fusion (named CPM). It leverages broad medical knowledge of LLMs to characterize each case's (case-specific) intra-modal contexts and inter-modal relations, as well as case-generic multimodal rationale, as priors, enabling three-stage priors-guided multimodal fusion: (i) Intra-modal Context-guided Calibration (IntraCC) that leverages case-specific intra-modal priors to guide feature calibration within each modality; (ii) Inter-modal Relation-guided Interaction (InterRI) that leverages case-specific inter-modal priors to guide pair-wise feature interaction in modality pairs; and (iii) Multimodal Rationale-guided Aggregation (MultiRA) that leverages case-generic multimodal priors to guide final feature aggregation over all modalities. On the well-established HANCOCK challenge dataset involving four distinct medical modalities, our CPM achieved a mean AUC of 83.98% for predicting 5-year overall survival and 2-year recurrence-free survival, improving upon the baseline by 7.58%, pioneeringly demonstrating the effectiveness of LLM-derived medical priors in guiding multimodal fusion for prognosis prediction. The code is available at https://github.com/yuelin421/CPM.

The code is available at https://github.com/yuelin421/CPM.

napari-TurboBox: Interactive Multi-Viewer Bounding Box Annotation for AI-Guided Volumetric Bioimaging

napari-TurboBox: Interactive Multi-Viewer Bounding Box Annotation for AI-Guided Volumetric Bioimaging

Nils Friederich、Philip Kohle、Maximilian Beichter、Mona Wellhäusser、Marcel Stahl 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Promptable segmentation models turn one 3D bounding box into an instance mask. Box placement therefore limits how fast microscopy volumes with thousands of objects are annotated. Each box must be checked in several synchronized viewers, but napari-bbox shares no state between viewers. An edit must update every viewer and slows down with more boxes. napari-TurboBox instead holds one shared copy of the boxes for all viewers. In a four-viewer layout, napari-TurboBox needs a few milliseconds per mouse move to update the three slice viewers up to $10^4$ boxes. The same update in napari-bbox leaves the 33 ms budget beyond about $10^3$ boxes and takes 159 ms at $10^4$. Boxes stay axis-aligned and export to COCO and YOLO as per-slice 2D boxes. napari-TurboBox is a pure-Python plugin for napari 0.6.6 to 0.9. The code is available at https://github.com/N-Friederich/napari-turbobox under the Apache 2.0 licence, and version 0.1.0 is archived at https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23090828 on Zenodo.

2026-09-29

Memory Bandwidth, Not FLOPs: Profiling and Accelerating Query-Based Segmentation Decoders on Edge GPUs

Memory Bandwidth, Not FLOPs: Profiling and Accelerating Query-Based Segmentation Decoders on Edge GPUs

Oliver Busemann、Torben Töniges、Martin Atzmueller

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Query-based segmentation models achieve strong performance even under real-time inference on workstation GPUs. On edge devices, however, we show their transformer decoders are deeply memory-bandwidth-bound, a bottleneck invisible to standard FLOP analysis that worsens with input resolution and limited DRAM bandwidth. An FP16 roofline analysis with tensor-core GEMMs active and hardware DRAM counters shows that the non-tensor-core aggregates of FastInst and Mask2Former reach only $2.5\%$ to $6\%$ of the CUDA-core ridge yet account for $53\%$ to $70\%$ of decoder time and $61\%$ to $71\%$ of decoder bytes. The largest individual kernels sustain up to $90\%$ of nominal peak DRAM bandwidth, effectively saturating the memory bus. The bottleneck is therefore not primarily low GEMM intensity, but cross-kernel activation traffic. CompactInst addresses this bottleneck using parameter-free spatial pooling at $4{\times}$, which reduces the bytes moved within the transformer decoder, while a two-convolution refinement head recovers spatial detail. Under TensorRT FP16 on Jetson Orin Nano, CompactInst delivers decoder speedups from $2.35{\times}$ to $4.74{\times}$ and model speedups from $1.57{\times}$ to $2.62{\times}$ as input resolution grows, while closely matching accuracy ($-0.4$ AP on COCO val2017, $+0.2$ AP on Cityscapes test). A heavier variant with $2.57{\times}$ more decoder FLOPs still runs its decoder $1.74{\times}$ faster on the most bandwidth-constrained device, confirming at the deployment level that decoder bytes, not FLOPs, govern latency.

VTrajEmbed: Aligning Text with Video Object Trajectories via Spatiotemporal Embeddings

VTrajEmbed: Aligning Text with Video Object Trajectories via Spatiotemporal Embeddings

Ruizhi Chen、Zining Fan、Yudi Guo、Jiang Liu、Zhiyu Lin 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Fine-grained alignment between text and video object trajectories is a core challenge in multimodal understanding. Existing embedding models excel at global retrieval, yet their single global representation entangles multiple objects and their spatiotemporal dynamics, limiting direct object-level grounding. To address this issue, we propose VTrajEmbed, an embedding model that decomposes a video into object trajectories and aligns each trajectory embedding with text for object-level grounding through retrieval. Our main contributions: (1)Slow-Fast Trajectory Encoding, a dual-branch architecture that establishes trajectory representations by densely sampling low-resolution frames for high-frequency motion cues and sparsely selecting high-resolution frames for scene context, aggregated by a shared LLM backbone; (2)Mask-Guided Contrastive Learning, a training objective built upon these representations that couples an intra-group loss and a batch-level bidirectional loss under a relation mask, excluding unverified relations and preventing confirmed matches from becoming false negatives; (3)Text2Traj-Bench, a trajectory-level text-video benchmark with model-assisted construction and human-verified query--trajectory relations. Experimental results show that VTrajEmbed-5B improves text-to-trajectory and trajectory-to-text R@1 over Qwen3-VL-Embedding-8B by 15.48 and 18.05 percentage points, respectively, and achieves 49.48% average R@1 across five global retrieval benchmarks, compared with 42.86% for Qwen3-VL-Embedding-8B.
