2026-10-15

MedSTP: Preserving Subspace and Transition Evidence for Medical VQA Token Pruning

MedSTP: Preserving Subspace and Transition Evidence for Medical VQA Token Pruning

Mengchen Fan、Qizhen Lan、Xi Xiao、Lin Zhao、Liang He 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Medical vision-language models (VLMs) for visual question answering (VQA) often pass long visual-token sequences to the language model, making visual-token pruning attractive but risky for medical images, where answers may depend on localized boundary and texture evidence. We study training free pre-decoding pruning and treat pruning as a subspace stable evidence preservation problem. The retained tokens should remain close to the dominant visual evidence subspace induced by the full token set. To this end, we propose \emph{Subspace-Transition Token Pruning} (\method{}), which estimates each token's spectral support from principal component space and constructs a compact representation subspace. To better reflect medical image structure, \method{} further uses a bounded token-grid transition prior to promote boundary and texture sensitive tokens. We evaluate \method{} on the closed-ended SLAKE and OmniMedVQA benchmarks and on RadImageNet-VQA, which provides both closed-ended and open-ended evaluations, using Lingshu-7B and Qwen3-VL-8B backbones. \method{} delivers consistently strong performance on both the closed-ended and open-ended RadImageNet-VQA evaluations. These results show that \method{} is an effective training free pruning framework for medical domain VQA.

Medical vision-language models (VLMs) for visual question answering (VQA) often pass long visual-token sequences to the language model, making visual-token pruning attractive but risky for medical images, where answers may depend on localized boundary and texture evidence. We study training free pre-decoding pruning and treat pruning as a subspace stable evidence preservation problem. The retained tokens should remain close to the dominant visual evidence subspace induced by the full token set. To this end, we propose \emph{Subspace-Transition Token Pruning} (\method{}), which estimates each token's spectral support from principal component space and constructs a compact representation subspace. These results show that \method{} is an effective training free pruning framework for medical domain VQA.

TransNSNet: A Transformer-Enhanced Multi-Scale Adversarial Framework for CT-to-PET Image Synthesis

TransNSNet: A Transformer-Enhanced Multi-Scale Adversarial Framework for CT-to-PET Image Synthesis

Shaojun Li、Xiaoyu Deng、Ikuo Keshi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

This study investigates CT-to-PET image synthesis using TransNSNet, a Transformer-enhanced multi-scale adversarial framework. A paired CT-PET dataset was curated from the public non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) Radiogenomics collection through patient screening, slice-level matching, preprocessing, and patient-level train-test separation. The proposed framework generates PET-like predictions at multiple spatial scales and applies coarse-to-fine reconstruction supervision, while TransPatchGAN introduces contextual feature interaction into adversarial evaluation. A progressive ablation study compares Pix2Pix, NSNet-Base, NSNet-D, and TransNSNet. Image-level performance was summarized over a fixed convergence window (epochs 80-101) from one training run per model. TransNSNet achieved the best mean value in nine of ten metrics, whereas NSNet-Base achieved the highest visual saliency-induced index. For fixed-fraction high-intensity regions, NSNet-D was marginally higher in slice-level recall and balanced accuracy, but the equal-weight patient-level values were nearly identical and the highest Dice values shifted to TransNSNet. These differences were small relative to between-patient variability. In the separate adaptive patient-level analysis (n=10), no pairwise model difference remained statistically significant after Holm correction. TransNSNet also had a substantially smaller parameter footprint than the original next-scale models. These findings indicate promising image-quality trends, but they do not establish statistical superiority or clinical validity; synthesized PET-like images should not be interpreted as substitutes for clinically acquired PET.

Look, Reason, Draw a Region: Recursive Refinement for Spatial Context Prediction

Look, Reason, Draw a Region: Recursive Refinement for Spatial Context Prediction

Wajeeha Tahir、Anna Tegon、Lionnus Kesting、Gamze İslamoğlu、Kristóf Karacs 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Spatial context prediction requires visual grounding and spatial reasoning to identify vacant regions relative to objects in a scene. Spatial relationships and physical constraints remain challenging for large vision-language models (VLMs). Recursive reasoning has demonstrated strong performance on discrete and abstract reasoning tasks, motivating its use for improving spatial context prediction in natural images. Weight sharing enables higher accuracy with a small trainable parameter count. We introduce Spatial-TRM, a compact recursive model with frozen vision-language features, a convex region decoder, and just 11.2M trainable parameters. This weight-tied reasoner supports six spatial relationships, three reference frames, and open-vocabulary anchor objects. On the RoboSpatial-Home benchmark, Spatial-TRM achieves 29.51% context accuracy, a gain of 9.02 percentage points (44.0% relative) over a separately trained single-step model with the same parameter count. Including the frozen encoders, the model has 877.8M total parameters, approximately $15\times$ fewer than the reported 13B size of RoboPoint. These results show that repeated refinement improves spatial context prediction from pretrained vision-language features at a fixed trainable parameter count on this benchmark.

The Optimal Ranking Tradeoffs Between Two Ranking Scores in Two-Class Classification

The Optimal Ranking Tradeoffs Between Two Ranking Scores in Two-Class Classification

Sébastien Piérard、Adrien Deliège、Marc Van Droogenbroeck

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The accuracy, true negative rate (negative class recall), true positive rate (positive class recall), negative predictive value (negative class precision), positive predictive value (positive class precision), class-specific intersections over unions, class-specific F-scores, Cohen's kappa, and balanced accuracy are scores that one intuitively wants to maximize when developing machine learning methods. However, they lead to different rankings of such methods. So, how can we establish a ranking that is an optimal tradeoff between any pair of these scores? It turns out that all these scores are special cases of ranking scores: a continuum of scores parameterized by the relative importance values assigned to the true negative, false positive, false negative, and true positive outcomes. Since all these scores are known to induce meaningful rankings, we seek the optimal tradeoffs among them. We consider the tradeoff between two scores to be optimal when it minimizes the Fréchet variance regarding the Kendall distance (also known as the bubble-sort distance) between rankings. For any given set of classifiers to rank, the optimal tradeoff lies at the center of the shortest (geodesic) path on the manifold of all rankings induced by the ranking scores. However, in the parameter space of the ranking scores, it is rarely achieved by averaging the importance values corresponding to the initial scores. In this paper, we present an exact analytical solution for determining a ranking score that is an optimal tradeoff for any given pair of initial scores, in the common case where the class priors are the same for all compared performances. We also experimentally investigate what happens when performance follows some distributions with fixed priors.

We also experimentally investigate what happens when performance follows some distributions with fixed priors.

SCALE: Improving Guidance-Free Visual Generation via Self-Contrastive Alignment for Likelihood Estimation

SCALE: Improving Guidance-Free Visual Generation via Self-Contrastive Alignment for Likelihood Estimation

Jiwan Hur、Dong-Jae Lee、Dongyeun Lee、Gyojin Han、Junmo Kim 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Classifier-free guidance (CFG) is essential for improving sample quality in visual generation, but it incurs substantial sampling overhead. Existing guidance-free approaches halve this cost by approximating the CFG with a single model inference, but their performance is bounded by the CFG and inherits its limitations. In this paper, we propose self-contrastive guidance (SCG), which contrasts the model's prediction on real and self-generated samples to obtain a direct, model-based signal that pushes generation toward the true data manifold. Integrating SCG into the standard likelihood objective via reparameterization yields Self-Contrastive Alignment for Likelihood Estimation (SCALE), a guidance-free training framework that produces SCG-enhanced predictions in a single forward pass. Across class-conditional and text-to-image generation on diffusion and autoregressive models, SCALE consistently outperforms CFG-mimicking guidance-free baselines and improves further when combined with DPO, demonstrating that the self-contrastive signal yields gains complementary to both CFG and preference-based fine-tuning.

2026-10-16

Can the Parts Fool the Test? Counterfactual Pairing Cycles for Relational OOD

Can the Parts Fool the Test? Counterfactual Pairing Cycles for Relational OOD

Yibo Zhou、Yirong Xiang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Relational out-of-distribution errors are easy to describe and hard to evaluate cleanly. An image may be natural, a caption may be fluent, and all mentioned objects may be common, yet the caption may describe the wrong relation in the image. Many evaluations intended to test this behavior can be solved without checking the pairing at all, because negatives differ in caption style, template, length, object frequency, or image statistics. We propose counterfactual pairing cycles, a blocked evaluation primitive that reuses the same two images and the same two captions on both sides of the comparison, changing only which image is paired with which caption. This makes image-only, text-only, and additive component shortcuts cancel exactly in finite samples. The same contrast also gives a training loss for relational outlier exposure and a diagnostic for bilinear vision-language scores. Across synthetic controls, GQA, and COCO, cycles separate coarse image-caption matching from directional role-binding failures, while also identifying geometric size relations that remain strongly visible in global features. In a shortcut-contaminated COCO outlier-exposure stress test, a model selected by a near-perfect shortcut validation AUROC retains substantial relational signal but remains below cycle training on exact cycles; cycle training improves CycleAcc from 0.882 to 0.927. The main lesson is simple: to test whether a model understands a pairing, the benchmark should keep the parts fixed, change the pairing, and ask only about coupling. The official implementation is available online.

Knowledge-Guided Distributional Prompt Learning with Visual Hierarchy for Zero-Shot Anomaly Detection

Knowledge-Guided Distributional Prompt Learning with Visual Hierarchy for Zero-Shot Anomaly Detection

Jiyul Ham、Seongho Yoon、Yonggon Jung、Sangjae Lee、Jun-Geol Baek 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Zero-shot anomaly detection (ZSAD) aims to detect anomalies in previously unseen classes using auxiliary images, with applications such as industrial visual inspection and medical imaging. Recent vision-language-model-based approaches improve ZSAD by aligning textual and visual representations, while distributional prompting further models prompt variability for unseen categories. However, existing methods do not explicitly combine normality-referenced distributional modeling with hierarchy-specific prompt specialization, limiting their ability to model anomalous semantics relative to normality while addressing both image-level detection and fine-grained localization. We propose Hierarchical Knowledge-Guided Distributional Prompt Learning (HiDist), a normality-referenced hierarchical distributional prompt framework for ZSAD. HiDist models each prompt token with a diagonal Gaussian distribution and generates stochastic prompt variations through Monte Carlo sampling. Rather than learning normal and anomalous distributions independently, anomalous prompt distributions are parameterized as deviations from learned normal references, explicitly encoding their dependency. The resulting distributions are specialized into global and local representations for image-level detection and pixel-level localization, respectively, while an adapted global visual representation guides hierarchical alignment. Experiments on seven industrial and medical datasets demonstrate strong cross-dataset performance across anomaly detection and localization tasks. Extensive ablation and representation analyses further support the effectiveness of the proposed distributional modeling and hierarchy-specific representation learning.

2026-10-16

Spatiotemporal Adaptive Eigenspectrum Remapping in Transcranial Ultrasound Localization Microscopy

Spatiotemporal Adaptive Eigenspectrum Remapping in Transcranial Ultrasound Localization Microscopy

Stephen A Lee、Isidora Jelenkovic、Maia V. Kokoeva、Jean Provost

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Clutter suppression is a critical preprocessing step in ultrasound blood flow imaging, yet conventional singular value decomposition (SVD) filtering depends on data-specific eigenvalue cutoffs that can remove slow flow along with tissue clutter. Here, we introduce adaptive eigenspectrum remapping (AER), a general framework in which clutter is suppressed by continuously remapping, rather than truncating, the eigenvalue spectrum of the spatiotemporal covariance, and we investigate zero-phase component analysis (ZCA) matrix whitening as an instance of AER for transcranial ultrasound localization microscopy (ULM). Rather than discarding eigenspaces, ZCA-based AER whitens the eigenvalue spectrum, reducing the dominance of coherent clutter, including skull reverberation, while retaining weak flow signals. We evaluate AER on both temporal and spatial covariance matrices in mouse brain datasets acquired with long temporal ensembles. Compared with SVD filtering, ZCA improves microbubble contrast and vascular image quality, increases trajectory density across low- and high-concentration regimes, and reveals more low-velocity trajectories in ULM velocity maps. Spatial AER further maintains reconstruction quality across extended ensembles and enables fuller single capillary reporter (SCaRe) reconstructions, including connected artery-to-capillary-to-vein tracks. These results support AER, here instantiated as ZCA whitening, as a data-adaptive alternative to manually thresholded SVD filtering for high-resolution microvascular imaging.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM生成模型/扩散3D视觉
2026-10-16

TriFold: Folding irregular 3D Gaussians into structured latents for 3D Scene Generation

TriFold: Folding irregular 3D Gaussians into structured latents for 3D Scene Generation

Yun Wang、Chiyu Wang、Fan Jiang、Ziyu Zhu、Zhaoxu Sun 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Recent progress in 3D generation has motivated growing interest in compact generative representations of complex 3D scenes. For native 3D Gaussian scenes, however, learning such representations remains challenging due to widely varying primitive counts, nonuniform spatial density, and differences in geometry and appearance even among spatially neighboring primitives. In this work, we introduce TriFold, a native 3D Gaussian VAE that encodes Gaussian scenes into a fixed-layout triplane latent and jointly reconstructs their geometry and appearance. The key insight is to impose structural regularity on the latent space while preserving the spatial adaptivity of the underlying Gaussians. During encoding, we use cell-wise learned queries to aggregate projected Gaussians into multiple slots per cell, reducing the information loss caused by single-feature pooling. During decoding, occupancy prediction identifies active regions, where query-based set prediction and candidate selection reconstruct local Gaussian sets with spatially varying primitive counts. Building on this representation, we train a latent flow-matching model for conditional scene-level 3DGS generation, enabling point-cloud-conditioned synthesis and single-image-conditioned amodal generation. Experiments demonstrate that TriFold achieves high-fidelity reconstruction and generation from a single image that provides only a partial observation of the scene, validating its effectiveness for native 3D Gaussian representation learning. https://fantasy-amap.github.io/TriFold

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM机器人视觉
2026-10-16

Semantic-Critical Regions for Video-to-Audio Generation

Semantic-Critical Regions for Video-to-Audio Generation

Yun Fang、Wei Guo、Zhuonan Liang、Heng Wang、Weidong Cai 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

In real-world videos, sound events are often associated with localized and fine-grained visual cues, such as object motion, object-part dynamics, contact events, and interactions between objects. Existing video-to-audio (V2A) methods employ implicit conditioning based on global visual representations or explicit control derived from temporal and spatial cues. However, subtle sound-producing and sound-indicative interactions remain insufficiently captured, leaving room for more precise visual conditioning of sound-event semantics and temporal dynamics. Motivated by this observation, we refer to regions containing such sound-relevant visual evidence as Semantic-Critical Regions (SCRs) and introduce SCR-V2A, a V2A generation framework that explicitly localizes and encodes SCRs as fine-grained visual guidance for audio generation. Specifically, we first identify SCRs through sound-related visual localization. We then transform the localized regions into a control representation through adapter-based dual visual encoding, which jointly captures global visual context and fine-grained sound-relevant evidence. The representation is used to condition a downstream audio generation model. Experiments on both in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks demonstrate that SCR-V2A achieves strong performance in distribution matching and audio quality while remaining competitive in cross-modal alignment and requiring few trainable parameters. These results highlight the value of prioritizing fine-grained, sound-relevant visual regions for V2A generation.

How Murky Before a VLM Knows It Cannot See? Accuracy, Confidence and Abstention on a Physical Visibility Axis

How Murky Before a VLM Knows It Cannot See? Accuracy, Confidence and Abstention on a Physical Visibility Axis

Mochamad Rizal Fauzan、Irgi Surya

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robustness studies of vision-language models (VLMs) grade degradation on nominal severity scales ("level 1-5") that carry no physical units, and they rarely ask whether a model's confidence follows the loss of visibility. We measure accuracy, internal (token-probability) confidence, verbalized confidence and an explicit abstain option on a visibility axis in metres: fog follows the Koschmieder model, an underwater arm scales the absorption spectrum of pure water, both are applied with a monocular metric depth map, and the visibility range V is swept over eight log-spaced levels from 250 m to 1.95 m. On 1000 COCO scenes and five small open VLMs (2-5 B parameters), accuracy on a boxed-object identification task falls from near its clean value to chance within a narrow band (half-way visibility V_acc of 12.1-15.9 m in fog and 20.5-38.6 m under water) while, for the four models that are confident on clean images, the confidence stays high: at V=1.95 m in fog their accuracy is at chance but their mean internal confidence is 0.68-0.84 and their verbalized confidence 0.61-1.00, a blind-spot width W=log10(V_acc/V_aware) of at least 0.79 decades, i.e. a factor of 6.2 in visibility. The remaining model is under-confident throughout and its internal confidence follows accuracy. An explicit abstain option follows accuracy when it names the reason (W from -0.21 to -0.06 in fog) but, for one model, lags with a generic "I don't know" (W up to 0.18). ImageNet-C fog severities 1-5 correspond to only 55-110 m of physical visibility, outside the failure band, and leave the accuracy of the models within 5 percentage points of its clean value. Blank-image and shuffled-image controls, a prior-matched answer set and a 200-image pilot that preceded the study are reported. Findings are descriptive: one draw of 1000 images, depth of assumed scale, and models of 2-5 B parameters.

Blank-image and shuffled-image controls, a prior-matched answer set and a 200-image pilot that preceded the study are reported. Findings are descriptive: one draw of 1000 images, depth of assumed scale, and models of 2-5 B parameters.

多模态/VLM
2026-10-16

Query-adaptive Recurrent Tokenization

Query-adaptive Recurrent Tokenization

Bavesh Balaji、Pranav Gopinath、Pouya Sadeghi、Sirisha Rambhatla

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Visual information worth extracting from an image and the token budget it warrants depend on the text query. Yet modern vision and vision-language models largely encode images independently of the query and allocate a fixed visual token budget to every input. We introduce the Query-Adaptive Recurrent Tokenizer (QART), a backbone-agnostic module that makes visual representations both query-steerable and budget-adaptive. QART steers the visual representation toward query-relevant evidence while adapting its token budget to the amount of information required. Its key principle is simple. Additional tokens are allocated until the visual features relevant to the query are represented with sufficient fidelity. Starting from frozen encoder features, QART recurrently constructs a compact representation and uses reconstruction quality over the query-relevant visual support to control token allocation. On Visual7W and VQAv2, QART achieves 81.96% and 76.37% accuracy using only about 29 visual tokens, reducing token counts by 20-25x relative to dense VLM inputs. It further reaches 92.0% P@1 on CORE and 36.3% PR-AUC on PODS, outperforming SteerViT on PODS by 8.4%. Together, these results show that visual representations need not be fixed in either content or capacity and can instead adapt to what each query requires.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM生成模型/扩散机器人视觉
2026-10-16

DICL-Qwen3VL: Decoupled Confidence Calibration for Interpretable Workwear Detection

DICL-Qwen3VL: Decoupled Confidence Calibration for Interpretable Workwear Detection

Meng Wang、Wu Zeng、Mei Li、Chao He

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Poor illumination and occlusion obscure clothing cues in indoor work sites, while diverse clothing styles complicate workwear compliance judgments. Although vision-language models (VLMs) support localization and semantic reasoning, their predictions often lack explicit supporting evidence, and a single confidence score cannot distinguish visual uncertainty from ambiguity in compliance judgments. We propose DICL-Qwen3VL, a decoupled interpretable VLM that integrates structured workwear detection, evidence generation, and separate visual and reasoning confidence estimates. First, low-rank adaptation (LoRA) adapts Qwen3-VL to localize visible upper-body clothing and identify workwear. Second, decoupled calibration separates visual observations from compliance reasoning and assigns a confidence score to each. Token entropy and sensitivity to image perturbations provide supervision for visual confidence, linking confidence estimation to the generated observations and their dependence on image content. Finally, group relative policy optimization (GRPO) jointly optimizes detection accuracy, output validity, and confidence calibration. Experiments on the company-collected Indoor Construction Site Workwear Dataset (ICSWD) and the web-collected Indoor Construction Workwear Dataset (WICWD) yield mAP@50 values of 0.730 and 0.753, respectively, outperforming the evaluated LoRA-adapted VLM baselines, including Qwen3-VL. The framework also provides explicit evidence and separate confidence scores to support manual review in industrial safety monitoring. WICWD is publicly available at https://github.com/DaErwen1/WICWD.

WICWD is publicly available at https://github.com/DaErwen1/WICWD.

From Adult Renal Masses to Pediatric Wilms Tumors: Evaluating Cross-Domain Generalization of Kidney Lesion Segmentation on MRI

From Adult Renal Masses to Pediatric Wilms Tumors: Evaluating Cross-Domain Generalization of Kidney Lesion Segmentation on MRI

Sarah de Boer、Myrthe A. D. Buser、Hartmut Häntze、Jawed Nawabi、Lisa C. Adams 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Deep learning segmentation models for pediatric oncology are often trained on small, single-center cohorts, whereas much larger datasets exist for adult diseases, such as kidney cancer. Whether models trained on adult renal lesions generalize to pediatric Wilms tumors remains unclear. We trained a segmentation model on single-sequence T1- or T2-weighted adult kidney MRI and evaluated it on a held-out adult test set and a pediatric Wilms tumor cohort, where it was compared with a pediatric-specific nnU-Net. Segmentation performance was quantified using the Dice similarity coefficient (DSC) and 95th-percentile Hausdorff distance. On the adult test set, the model achieved a median DSC of 0.86 for renal lesions. On the pediatric cohort, the adult-trained model achieved a median DSC of 0.85 compared with 0.90 for the pediatric-specific model. Despite differences in age, anatomy, and tumor type, the adult-trained model showed promising performance on pediatric Wilms tumors, with only a modest difference in median DSC compared with the pediatric-specific model. However, non-inferiority could not be shown, highlighting the value of pediatric training data. In future work, a fine-tuning strategy could be explored.

LiveSync: Iterative Self-Training for Lip-Sync Generation

LiveSync: Iterative Self-Training for Lip-Sync Generation

Huaishao Luo、Huan Zhang、Guanbo Bao、Yinglu Liu、Youzheng Wu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Audio-driven lip synchronization aims to match target speech through localized mouth and jaw articulation while preserving subject identity, head pose, and surrounding facial context. Naturally recorded videos provide synchronized reconstruction targets but rarely offer pairs that are matched in identity and pose yet differ primarily in speech-related articulation. Mask-based training enables reconstruction from these videos, but random-reference inputs differ from the aligned self-reference used at inference. We present LiveSync, an iterative self-training framework in which a single mask-based model generates pseudo pairs and is refined for deployment. The model is first trained on a curated real-video corpus augmented with externally generated clips covering under-represented visual conditions. A frozen checkpoint then uses independently sampled audio to generate mouth-edited references from real-video frames, copying pixels outside the edit mask from each source frame. After confidence filtering, these references are paired with aligned masked inputs and original real-frame targets. The retained pseudo pairs are mixed with real training data to refine the same model from its current weights, updating training supervision without adding inference stages. Although we instantiate the framework with a single-pass GAN-based model, the self-training strategy could in principle also be adapted to diffusion-based models. Experimental results show that LiveSync achieves leading synchronization performance on HDTF and competitive performance compared with the baselines on the AIGC-LipSync subset, while maintaining low inference latency.

PreciseEdit: Strong Yet Stable Semantic Transport for One-Step Image Editing

PreciseEdit: Strong Yet Stable Semantic Transport for One-Step Image Editing

Jingyi Wang、Xiao Lu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Recent one-step text-to-image (T2I) models enable efficient image synthesis, opening up new possibilities for interactive Web-based image editing. However, existing one-step editing methods often construct a global transport field through simple vector operations between conditional predictions. While such strong transport can effectively drive the image toward the target semantics, it may be released along unreliable directions or over non-edited regions, leading to structural distortions within the editing region and background drift elsewhere. More importantly, stabilizing the editing process through global smoothing or uniformly weakening the transport field often makes large semantic changes overly conservative. In this work, we revisit one-step image editing from the perspective of the tension between semantic strength and transport stability, and identify a key insight: strong semantic transport is not inherently unstable. What matters is whether it is applied along reliable directions and at reliable spatial locations. Motivated by this insight, we introduce PreciseEdit, a model-agnostic, training-free and inversion-free one-step editing framework that strengthens target-semantic alignment while preserving background fidelity and structural consistency. We further propose a dual-noise-scale role decomposition strategy: a higher-noise scale produces the strong target-directed transport used for actual editing, whereas a lower-noise scale jointly serves as a probe for directional agreement and spatial structure. Extensive experiments on PIE-Bench demonstrate that PreciseEdit achieves state-of-the-art performance and effectiveness on various editing tasks.

TriINR: Unsupervised Atmospheric Turbulence Mitigation with Triple Implicit Neural Representations

TriINR: Unsupervised Atmospheric Turbulence Mitigation with Triple Implicit Neural Representations

Yuka Ogino、Rikuto Otsuka、Yuho Shoji、Takuya Ogawa、Takahiro Toizumi 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Atmospheric turbulence degrades image quality in long-range imaging. The degradation is generally modeled with two spatio-temporally varying components, tilt and blur. Recent unsupervised turbulence mitigation methods have demonstrated the effectiveness of representing a sharp image as an implicit neural representation (INR). However, their tilt and blur representations remain frame-dependent, and the number of optimization parameters grows with the number of input frames. To address this limitation, we propose TriINR, an unsupervised turbulence mitigation method that recovers a sharp image from multiple degraded frames. TriINR represents the sharp image, tilt, and blur as three implicit neural representations. By modeling tilt and blur as spatio-temporal INRs, TriINR achieves frame-count-independent degradation representation and memory-efficient optimization. We further incorporate physical and statistical properties of atmospheric turbulence as constraints on the INRs to improve restoration accuracy. Experiments on synthesized turbulence data with three degradation strengths show that TriINR restores images more robustly across varying turbulence strengths than prior TM methods and achieves the highest accuracy among the evaluated unsupervised methods. Our method also demonstrates robustness on real long-range scenes containing additional non-ideal motion.

Our method also demonstrates robustness on real long-range scenes containing additional non-ideal motion.

STC-MGNet: Spatial-Temporal-Channel Attention-Enhanced Multimodal Graph Network for Pedestrian Intention Prediction

STC-MGNet: Spatial-Temporal-Channel Attention-Enhanced Multimodal Graph Network for Pedestrian Intention Prediction

Yuanzhe Li、Meng Wang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Accurate prediction of pedestrian crossing intentions plays a critical role in enhancing the safety of pedestrian-vehicle interactions in autonomous driving. However, effectively capturing subtle spatiotemporal patterns in pedestrian pose sequences and integrating complementary visual and ego-vehicle motion information remain challenging. This paper proposes a spatial-temporal-channel attention-enhanced multimodal graph network (STC-MGNet) for pedestrian crossing intention prediction. STC-MGNet introduces a spatial-temporal-channel (STC) attention-enhanced pose graph encoder to learn discriminative representations from pedestrian pose sequences. Three parallel attention branches selectively emphasize informative skeletal joints, observation frames, and feature channels, with their outputs integrated through learnable fusion weights. To leverage complementary visual and ego-vehicle motion information, a multi-stage cross-modal channel modulation mechanism is introduced to adaptively refine pose representations through modality-specific channel gating at successive graph encoding stages. Experimental results demonstrate that STC-MGNet achieves accuracy rates of 70%, 87%, and 88% on the JAADbeh, JAADall, and PIE datasets, respectively, outperforming several representative baseline methods. Ablation experiments further highlight the complementary contributions of visual and ego-vehicle motion information to prediction performance.

I2V-DETACH: Source Grounding Detachment for Unauthorized Image-to-Video Generation

I2V-DETACH: Source Grounding Detachment for Unauthorized Image-to-Video Generation

Chanhui Lee、Yeonghwan Song、Yewon Kang、Jeany Son

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Recent diffusion models have advanced image-conditioned video generation, but their accessibility raises risks of unauthorized manipulation. Image immunization has emerged as a promising defense for Image-to-Video (I2V) protection, yet existing methods often rely on degrading visual quality or temporal coherence. In this work, we revisit I2V immunization as reducing source-content preservation, shifting the focus from output degradation to weakening source-conditioned content propagation. To this end, we propose I2V-DETACH, an image immunization method that prevents unauthorized I2V generation through source grounding detachment. I2V models preserve source content by propagating source-image cues into video latents during denoising. Accordingly, I2V-DETACH disrupts this process in two complementary ways: it suppresses source-video grounding to reduce source cue propagation, and strengthens intra-video interactions to shift denoising toward video-side dynamics with reduced reliance on the source image. To obtain informative optimization signals, we initialize auxiliary video latents with source-oriented states that remain within the source-conditioned generation regime while deviating from source-preserving trajectories. Extensive experiments across diverse diffusion architectures show that I2V-DETACH substantially reduces source-content preservation, improves over prior immunization methods, and exhibits strong black-box transferability.

Xiaomi-Lens-0 Technical Report

Xiaomi-Lens-0 Technical Report

Pei Fu、Yike Liu、Yue Sun、Hanmo Wang、Xiuwen Xi 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) answer questions about whole images well, yet they still struggle with fine-grained region understanding. A key reason is that text-only supervision never specifies which regions support an answer. To close this gap, we present Xiaomi-Lens-0, a 4B MLLM built on Qwen3.5 that predicts masks from its own visual representations. Specifically, a lightweight decoder combines vision-encoder patch features with visual latents, the language model's mask-token states, so mask supervision reaches the shared backbone without an additional segmentation encoder. Because each latent decodes to a mask, the model can interleave reasoning with region-specific latents and thus think with visual latents, while grounded reasoning traces supervise the regions it visits during chain-of-thought. As a result, across all eight RefCOCO, RefCOCO+, and RefCOCOg splits, Xiaomi-Lens-0 gains 5.6 points on average over Qwen3.5 in referring comprehension and achieves the highest segmentation cIoU among compared models. Moreover, it surpasses Qwen3.5 across all perception benchmarks. Homepage: \href{https://seerray-lab.github.io/Xiaomi-Lens-0}{this https URL}

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM
2026-10-16

RAP: A Dataset for Paper-to-Poster Generation via Declarative Markup from Human-Crafted Exemplars

RAP: A Dataset for Paper-to-Poster Generation via Declarative Markup from Human-Crafted Exemplars

Haonan Han、Yukang Lin、Minliang Lin、Haoxian Tan、Jun-Yan He 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Making a conference poster is visual coding in miniature: an entire paper must be compressed onto a single page, which exercises summarization, visual design, and code implementation at once, using nothing beyond the paper itself. Yet benchmarks for this task in the context of visual coding remain scarce: existing resources are small and annotated only with layout boxes, so evaluation must fall back on VLM-as-judge or human checklists rather than objective render-and-compare, and the generation methods they support design layouts from scratch, leaving human-crafted posters unusable as either a retrievable design library or as verified supervision. We introduce RAP (Reconstruct A Poster), a dataset and benchmark for paper-to-poster generation as a visual coding task. RAP contains 25K+ verified paper-poster pairs from various machine learning conferences held between 2022 and 2026, annotated with a three-level content taxonomy, plus a held-out benchmark of 150 test papers each paired with its human poster. A fully automated derendering pipeline turns each poster into styled HTML/CSS of typed, refillable slots, so every sample is a paper-poster-markup triplet verifiable by rendering against the source poster. The corpus serves both inference and training: PosterRAG, a training-free multi-agent pipeline, retrieves an exemplar and refills its slots without touching layout or styling, while an SFT+RL recipe trains a dedicated model from the same triplets. An online demo is available at https://reconstruct-a-poster.github.io/.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM视频理解
2026-10-16

MLLM Frame Subset Ensembling for Audio-Visual Video QA and MLLM-based Reranking for Ad-hoc Video Search in TRECVID 2025

MLLM Frame Subset Ensembling for Audio-Visual Video QA and MLLM-based Reranking for Ad-hoc Video Search in TRECVID 2025

Andreas Goulas、Damianos Galanopoulos、Ioannis Patras、Vasileios Mezaris

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

This paper presents the overview of the runs related to the Ad-hoc Video Search (AVS) and Video Question Answering (VQA) tracks of TRECVID 2025 on behalf of the CERTH-ITI team. For the AVS track, we introduce a two-stage framework built on foundation models. In the first stage, multiple vision-language models (VLMs) encode both the input query, augmented through LLM-generated rephrasings, and the candidate video shots, producing weighted similarity scores for initial retrieval. In the second stage, we utilize a Multimodal-LLM(MLLM)-based reranking module that evaluates the semantic alignment between each shot among the top-N highest-ranked ones and the original query, generating updated relevance scores for reordering these shots. This MLLM-driven reranking significantly improves contextual matching and produces more accurate final rankings without requiring any model training. Regarding the VQA track, we fine-tune an audio-visual MLLM model on the provided TRECVID training dataset and we implement an inference-time scaling technique to enhance the multimodal understanding capabilities of the MLLM. For the open-ended Answer Generation (AG) task, we aggregate multiple model responses per question via a majority vote. The responses are generated with greedy sampling from different random frame subsets of the video and they are ranked based on the number of votes. For the Multiple-Choice (MC) task, instead of voting, we use mean pooling on the logits assigned by the fine-tuned model to each candidate response. Through the combination of fine-tuning and frame subset ensembling we achieve the highest score across 3 metrics in the VQA AG task and the second highest in the VQA MC task.

The responses are generated with greedy sampling from different random frame subsets of the video and they are ranked based on the number of votes. For the Multiple-Choice (MC) task, instead of voting, we use mean pooling on the logits assigned by the fine-tuned model to each candidate response. Through the combination of fine-tuning and frame subset ensembling we achieve the highest score across 3 metrics in the VQA AG task and the second highest in the VQA MC task.

Seeing Seizures: A Large-Scale Empirical Study of Video and EEG Deep Learning for Automated Rodent Seizure Detection and Severity Grading

Seeing Seizures: A Large-Scale Empirical Study of Video and EEG Deep Learning for Automated Rodent Seizure Detection and Severity Grading

Alloy Das、Shreyan Ganguly、Nyzil Massey、Hung Phi Doan、Zhanhong Jiang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Manual annotation of synchronized video and EEG recordings is a bottleneck in preclinical seizure research. We present an empirical study of seizure detection and behavioral severity grading using 24,131 paired clips from 601 sessions across 20 rats in a soman-induced epilepsy model. Ten video architectures and seven single-channel EEG models are evaluated independently on binary detection and three- and five-class severity tasks. In the session-exclusive reference comparison, R(2+1)D-18 and GRU achieve detection macro-F1 scores of 0.968 and 0.974, respectively; for five-class grading, the corresponding scores are 0.657 and 0.560. Subject-exclusive evaluation reduces five-class macro-F1 to 0.475 for video and 0.404 for EEG, highlighting the difficulty of grading unseen animals. Temporal-sampling experiments demonstrate the value of multiple frames, while detection performance varies relatively little across the evaluated video architectures. A separate video-only binary-detection evaluation on RodEpil achieves a mean seizure-class F1 of 0.979 after uniform re-encoding to reduce identified class-correlated encoding cues. The study characterizes existing models for behavior-defined severity labels under the evaluated protocol, motivating further investigation of expert-assisted annotation workflows. Our code and github page is available here.

Micro-Localization: High-Precision Mobile Robot Localization Using Microscopic Ground Texture

Micro-Localization: High-Precision Mobile Robot Localization Using Microscopic Ground Texture

Aaron Wilhelm、Marina de Albuquerque Marcon、Emith Uyanwatte、Nils Napp

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We demonstrate high-precision global localization using natural ground texture viewed through microscope optics, achieving localization in the tens-of-micrometers regime. For the planar ground texture localization problem considered here, we relate optical magnification and image-space localization uncertainty to physical localization precision. Leveraging this relationship, we then develop a microscope-based localization system that addresses the calibration, camera-modeling, and focus challenges introduced by high-magnification imaging. We evaluate this system across several surface textures using a calibrated precision gantry. Across 1,007 held-out queries per surface, the system localizes every query and achieves translation RMSEs of 28$-$73 $μ$m. Independent photometric registration agrees with the feature-based localization estimates to within approximately 9 $μ$m RMSE. We further demonstrate localization on a low-cost mobile robot using the gantry-collected maps, and also show that the system can resolve micrometer-scale displacement caused by thermal expansion. These results show that microscopic natural surface texture can bring mobile localization into a precision regime typically associated with fixed positioning systems.

CFC26: Building Evaluations for Deployment in Sonar-Based Fish Counting

CFC26: Building Evaluations for Deployment in Sonar-Based Fish Counting

Madison Van Horn、Suzanne Stathatos、Sevan Brodjian、Justin Kay、Kai Van Brunt 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Accurate counts of fish populations are essential for monitoring ecosystems and making decisions for conservation, harvest limits, and river management. In many field deployments, these counts are obtained from sonar videos that experts must inspect manually, making large-scale monitoring slow, expensive, and difficult. Automated counting systems have been proposed as a scalable alternative, but so far this promise has been difficult to realize in deployment. Developing evaluations that better reflect real-world deployment conditions will help close this gap and drive improvements in counting performance in practice. To achieve this, we propose Caltech Fish Counting 2026 (CFC26), a new dataset with improved evaluation protocols that simulate deployment to seen and novel rivers, testing in-distribution and out-of-distribution performance. We expand the CFC22 dataset to eleven deployment locations across nine Pacific salmon river systems from Alaska to Northern California, capturing a broader geographical and ecological range of data. We additionally introduce two new evaluation metrics for counting, nMNE and nMANE, that mirror the operational workflow that experts use and help surface errors that frame-level detection metrics do not capture. Using these metrics, we reveal previously-concealed directional bias in upstream vs downstream counts, which would have direct and significant ecological implications if undetected. The dataset and code are publicly available on CaltechDATA and GitHub.

JAVIS: Joint Audio-Visual Interaction and Streaming for Real-Time Conversational Agents

JAVIS: Joint Audio-Visual Interaction and Streaming for Real-Time Conversational Agents

Junseok Ahn、Jaehyun Park、Jeongsoo Choi、Joon Son Chung、Ji-Hoon Kim 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Human communication is inherently multimodal, conveying intent not only through linguistic and vocal cues but also through visual signals. However, existing conversational systems have mainly focused on text or speech, leaving out the visual feedback that is essential for natural interaction. In this paper, we propose JAVIS, an end-to-end streaming framework for real-time audio-visual conversational agents. Our key idea is to let the voice and the face speak from a single representation. On top of a shared LLM, a Joint Refined Head produces a unified audio-visual representation, which is fed into dedicated streaming branches for speech and face generation. This end-to-end design avoids intermediate bottlenecks, enabling real-time generation while keeping speech and face tightly synchronized. In addition, we present JAVIS-Syn, a 334-hour synthetic corpus designed to address the lack of expressive audio-visual dialogue data, covering emotionally rich multi-turn conversations and assistant-style exchanges. Experiments show that JAVIS achieves approximately 200$\times$ lower initial latency than a strong baseline, while outperforming baselines in lip synchronization and facial expression appropriateness.

Experiments show that JAVIS achieves approximately 200$\times$ lower initial latency than a strong baseline, while outperforming baselines in lip synchronization and facial expression appropriateness.

Has GPT-6-Astra Achieved Embodied Intelligence?

Has GPT-6-Astra Achieved Embodied Intelligence?

SenseNova、Joint Research Team、 :、Zhongang Cai、Ziang Cao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

GPT-6-Astra demonstrates a level of competence in robotic manipulation and navigation that challenges the conventional paradigm of building specialized models for individual tasks and embodiments. In this report, we ask not only how capable GPT-6-Astra is, but what its capabilities imply for the field. First, we probe the limits of standalone GPT-6-Astra. We conduct a large-scale evaluation across 12 representative benchmarks spanning tabletop manipulation, humanoids, mobile platforms, and dexterous hands. Under a unified protocol that allows in-context learning (ICL) from training-set demonstrations and substantially increases the number of execution steps, GPT-6-Astra achieves unprecedented performance (Fig. 1). Future research may focus on improving 1) the generality of ICL 2) cost and execution time, 3) physical-state verification, 4) recovery from irreversible failures, and 5) long-horizon preservation of progress. Second, we study the agentic harness for embodied control. We identify that close-loop coordination between a high-level "System 2" reasoner (i.e., GPT-6-Astra) and a low-level "System 1" controller (e.g., π0.5) is costly but effective. Third, we examine GPT-6-Astra on real-world deployment. Our experiments on 8 robot configurations reveal substantial gaps: 1) robot demonstrations required for ICL are expensive to collect, and 2) it is implausible to trade longer execution for higher success rates with physical constraints. Fourth, we envision the next generation of embodied benchmarks. We develop a prototype that 1) derives tasks from real-world needs, 2) provides no in-domain demonstrations, and 3) judges agents on a world clock that keeps running while they deliberate. Taken together, our results suggest that GPT-6-Astra has largely crossed the threshold of embodied competence, but not yet that of practical embodied intelligence.

Erase the Mark, Preserve the Motion: Temporally Consistent Removal of Video Watermarks

Erase the Mark, Preserve the Motion: Temporally Consistent Removal of Video Watermarks

Yifan Liao、Zhen Sun、Zhicong Huang、Jiaming He、Zongmin Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Recent advances in video generation have increased the demand for reliable watermarking for copyright protection, yet the robustness of video watermarks against removal attacks remains underexplored. Most watermark-removal methods originate from the image domain: signal optimization injects imperceptible pixel-domain perturbations, whereas generative resynthesis produces a visually similar instance through diffusion synthesis. When extended to videos, the former introduces temporally varying noise that causes visible flickering, while the latter regenerates fine-grained structures inconsistently across frames, leading to structural motion inconsistency. To evaluate video watermark robustness, we propose PoseRegen, a pose-consistent reconstruction framework that removes watermarks while preserving the original content and motion. PoseRegen first applies a temporally shared viewpoint transformation to disrupt watermark-bearing spatial patterns and then jointly reconstructs the transformed sequence with a pose-conditioned video model. A frozen video DiT processes the complete sequence with its reference-video latent, and full-sequence Flow Matching supervision keeps identity and background consistent across frames. To further preserve motion structure, we introduce a lightweight Pose-Conditioned Motion Consistency Adapter that adapts the frozen backbone to temporally ordered pose guidance and constrains body-part trajectories throughout reconstruction. Experiments on eleven video watermarking schemes show that PoseRegen reduces the average bit accuracy to 0.505, near random guessing, while outperforming the strongest baseline by 9.34% on motion-related VBench metrics. These results reveal a practical vulnerability of current video watermarks and highlight the need to evaluate robustness jointly with temporal and motion quality.

DriveWorld3D Evidence Constrained 3D World Modeling and Generative Simulation for Real-World Autonomous Driving

DriveWorld3D Evidence Constrained 3D World Modeling and Generative Simulation for Real-World Autonomous Driving

Wei Wang、Yiru Veronika Wang、Yihan Wang、Zihao Li、Xiaohui Liang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Three dimensional modeling for autonomous driving must continuously estimate road geometry, obstacle occupancy, traffic participant behavior, and observational uncertainty under limited computational resources. Existing reconstruction methods can synthesize realistic novel views, but visual fidelity does not sufficiently characterize the geometric reliability required for driving. Generative world models can expand scenarios and predict future observations, yet may introduce content unsupported by measurements. We propose DriveWorld3D, a framework combining temporal video learning, measurement constraints from multiple sensors, and offline generative simulation. Its online component maintains a unified world state comprising road surfaces, volumetric occupancy, dynamic entities, and road relations, while separately representing physical occupancy, observability, and evidence provenance. Action conditioned future prediction and risk sensitive planning connect this representation to driving decisions. Its offline component reconstructs recorded scenes using neural radiance fields or 3D Gaussian Splatting, then expands long tail conditions using generative models while restricting the use of generated content as geometric ground truth. We specify data partitioning, sensor ablations, fault injection, paired closed loop testing, and real to simulation consistency assessment. The central hypothesis is that explicit evidence management can reduce dangerous false free space predictions and occlusion risk within a matched computational budget while retaining acceptable driving efficiency. All performance claims remain subject to experimental validation. Keywords: autonomous driving; 3D occupancy; sensor fusion; uncertainty; generative world models; NeRF; 3D Gaussian Splatting; closed loop simulation

Deep Learning for Forest Plantation Mapping in Godavari Districts of Andhra Pradesh, India

Deep Learning for Forest Plantation Mapping in Godavari Districts of Andhra Pradesh, India

Snehal More、Anuj Karpatne、Valerie A. Thomas、Randolph H. Wynne

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Small-area forest plantations play a vital role in the socioeconomic well-being of farmers in Southeast Asia. Most plantations are established on former agricultural land, often on land less suitable for agriculture. Plantations that are converted from natural forest have adverse impacts on biodiversity. Mapping small-area plantations is thus important to understand the dynamics of forest cover in Southeast Asia and to study the social, economic, and ecological effects of this important land cover and land use change. While the small size of forest plantations makes it difficult to detect them using moderate resolution satellite sensors, the problem is exacerbated by the high degree of mixing between plantations, surrounding vegetation, and other land covers, which often show variegated responses in satellite signals across space and time. In this paper, we study the problem of mapping small-area forest plantations in East and West Godavari districts of Andhra Pradesh, India using deep learning methods. Remotely sensed cloud-free data from the Harmonized Landsat Sentinel-2 S10 product were classified using a pixel-level neural network and training data labeled using a field-based survey in concert with expert aerial photo interpretation. We compare the performance of deep learning methods with a baseline random forest classifier in our study region of 21543 sq. km over a period of 3 years and analyze the differences in the results across land cover classes and seasons.

COMET: Decoupled Distillation, Routing, and Capacity Control for Task-Agnostic Continual Vision--Language Learning

COMET: Decoupled Distillation, Routing, and Capacity Control for Task-Agnostic Continual Vision--Language Learning

Seungyoon Woo、Keonhee Park、Gunhee Kim

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-language models (VLMs) such as CLIP retain zero-shot recognition ability, but their open-world use often requires task-agnostic continual learning: tasks arrive sequentially, past data are unavailable, task identity is unknown, and the backbone cannot be retrained. We argue that this setting fails VLMs through three coupled pressures beyond the stability-plasticity tradeoff: (1) modal-consistency drift, (2) representation-space interference, and (3) per-task capacity shortfall. We propose COMET (Collaborative Mixture-of-Expert Transfer) to resolve these three issues respectively as follows. A frozen teacher provides only a feature reference on a shared image-text pool; Tri-Affinity Distillation preserves image-image, text-text, and image-text geometry; and Student-Aware Cross-Expert Distillation aligns new experts with compatible prior experts so routing errors degrade gracefully. We design COMET to keep capacity and inference separate: a contextual bandit keeps or merges full per-task experts using post-training signals, while a reconstruction router selects sparse experts at test time without teacher semantics. On the X-TAIL benchmark, we show that each proposed component improves continual learning performance. COMET shows that continual vision-language adaptation can preserve multimodal geometry, coordinate accumulated experts, and allocate capacity jointly when feature reference, expert shaping, routing, and capacity control are separated by construction.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM3D视觉视频理解机器人视觉

HAPACT: A Benchmark For Human-Centric Physical Impact Localization in Movies

HAPACT: A Benchmark For Human-Centric Physical Impact Localization in Movies

Youngrae Kim、Yejin Jang、Eunho Kim、Youjin Sung、Kun-Woo Song 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Physical reasoning about humans in videos has largely focused on everyday interactions, where contact, pressure, pose, and motion support embodied perception and contact-aware applications. Still, short and forceful properties like impact events remain relatively underexplored, despite their relevance to broader understanding of how abrupt physical events affect the human body. To fill this gap, we introduce human-centric impact localization, a new task that localizes target physical impacts temporally within a video and spatially on the human body. We further present HAPACT, a HumAn-centric imPACT dataset and benchmark built from movie clips, containing over 2.8K video-query pairs with more than 20K fine-grained human-annotated frames. We built HAPACT around viewer-grounded annotation, text-conditioned localization, and supervision at frame-level temporal and SMPL-X-based spatial granularity. To demonstrate the effectiveness of our benchmark, we conduct extensive evaluations of existing methods, employing video moment retrieval models for temporal localization and human-scene contact models for spatial localization. For the spatial task, we propose a text-conditioned baseline by reformulating localization as query-guided body-region selection. Benchmark results show that physical impact localization remains challenging for existing methods, motivating HAPACT as a dedicated benchmark to support physical impact reasoning in videos.

Measurement-Adaptive Light-Cone Transform for Robust Non-Line-of-Sight Imaging

Measurement-Adaptive Light-Cone Transform for Robust Non-Line-of-Sight Imaging

Lianfang Wang、Xueying Liu、Junnan Dong、Qinghua Zhang、Jun Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Practical non-line-of-sight (NLOS) measurements are affected by noise and deviations from idealized light transport, which can degrade reconstruction with conventional light-cone transform (LCT) methods. We propose the Measurement-Adaptive Light-Cone Transform (MALCT), a physics-guided framework that retains the efficient Fourier-domain inversion structure of LCT. {Within a unified variational formulation, MALCT combines the analytical response with a shared learned response through measurement-conditioned weighting and predicts a measurement-conditioned, frequency-dependent regularization map.} An attention-enhanced spatio-temporal feature extraction module captures long-range temporal dependencies and local spatial correlations to support reconstruction under low-signal conditions. Experiments on synthetic data and real measurements from different NLOS systems demonstrate improved reconstruction quality. Controlled evaluations further assess robustness to temporal-response broadening and reduced acquisition time. MALCT also preserves scene structures with spatial sampling grids down to $32\times32$, supporting its potential for NLOS imaging under limited acquisition budgets.

STRIDE: Evaluating Spatiotemporal Reasoning in Driving Edge Cases

STRIDE: Evaluating Spatiotemporal Reasoning in Driving Edge Cases

Lieqi Liu、Rui gao、Jia-Chen Gu、Wenbo Hu、Zhaobin Mo 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

In autonomous driving, perceiving surrounding objects is not enough: failing to understand how they move and interact over time can lead to dangerous decisions. Evaluating vision-language models (VLMs) therefore requires testing whether they connect past spatial observations to future consequences. Yet existing benchmarks often assess edge cases through basic perception questions or test temporal understanding in simple scenarios, leaving complex spatiotemporal reasoning underexplored. We introduce STRIDE (SpatioTemporal Reasoning In Driving Edge Cases), an object-centric benchmark for long-tail, safety-critical driving scenarios. STRIDE covers six task families: spatial perception, spatial understanding, temporal memory, temporal extrapolation, trajectory prediction, and scene-context awareness. It pairs multiple-choice questions probing individual spatiotemporal judgments with related free-form visual question answering (VQA) requiring detailed scene explanations and justifications of driving decisions. We prioritize scenes with unusual object behaviors and complex traffic interactions, generate reference answers from structured dataset annotations through automated procedures, and use limited language-model assistance and human verification to ensure annotation quality. Across 12 proprietary, open-weight, and driving-specialized models, even the best performer, GPT-6-Astra, achieves only 43.4% average multiple-choice accuracy, versus an approximately 20% random-choice baseline. Further analysis reveals that strong performance in describing driving scenes does not necessarily translate to accurate safety-critical judgments, and that models struggle more with reconstructing object motion than recalling past positions. STRIDE thus exposes spatiotemporal driving limitations that aggregate VQA scores can conceal.

Pedicel-Targeted Strawberry Harvesting via Imitation Learning on a Low-Cost Teleoperated Robotic Arm

Pedicel-Targeted Strawberry Harvesting via Imitation Learning on a Low-Cost Teleoperated Robotic Arm

Nitin Rai、Won Suk Lee、Hongyoung Leon、Heping Zhu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Precise pedicel-targeted grasping in strawberry harvesting remains a critical bottleneck due to the pedicel's small diameter (1.3-3 mm), color similarity with surrounding foliage, and precise force control required for clean abscission zone detachment. As a market-grade crop, strawberry aesthetic value makes bruise-free pedicel detachment essential over direct fruit grasping. Classical approaches struggle to unify vision models, motion planning, and contact manipulation into a robust framework, particularly in occluded canopy environments. Therefore, this work presents a visuomotor imitation learning pipeline for pedicel-targeted strawberry harvesting that unifies perception, motion planning, and contact manipulation from collecting human teleoperation demonstrations, training an Action Chunking with Transformers (ACT) policy with a ResNet-50 visual backbone, and deploying the learned policy autonomously on a Jetson AGX Orin edge platform at 15 Hz. The trained policy achieved consistent loss convergence from 2.84 to 0.091 over 100K gradient steps on 130 demonstrations. We have also established a reproducible open-source pipeline with publicly released dataset and model weights for the agricultural robotics community. Full dataset is available at, https://huggingface.co/datasets/nitindominicrai/strawberry_pedicel_grasp_teleoperation and trained policy at, https://huggingface.co/nitindominicrai/act_strawberry_pedicel. To replicate this work, all code, configurations, and deployment instructions are publicly available at, https://github.com/nitin-dominic/autonomous_strawberry_pedicel_harvesting.

OmniXRef: A Unified Framework for Multimodal Multi-Reference Video Editing

OmniXRef: A Unified Framework for Multimodal Multi-Reference Video Editing

Xiaoyan Cong、Tianyu Wang、Hyeonho Jeong、Zhifei Zhang、Yuqian Zhou 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Creative intent in video editing can be expressed through text instructions (e.g., "remove the people"), image references (e.g., subject appearance), video references (e.g., camera movement), or a combination of the above. We introduce OmniXRef, a unified framework for multimodal multi-reference video editing that allows users to combine variable numbers of text, image, and video references to control diverse attributes of a source video. We first devise a unified input design with a reference-bank interface and introduce $R^2$-RoPE to support generalization to more references. Then our model, OmniXRef-Model, uses a single-stream diffusion transformer to jointly process the source video and multimodal context from an MLLM in an interleaved sequence. To address the scarcity of training data for video-reference editing, we construct OmniXRef-Data using synthesis pipelines for different editing tasks and combinations of references. We use curriculum learning to progressively increase task complexity and resolution. To support systematic evaluation of video as a control modality, we introduce OmniXRef-Bench, a benchmark for video-reference attribute transfer across Camera, Motion, Content, Style, and Visual Effects (VFX). Comprehensive evaluations show that our OmniXRef-Model outperforms task-specific baselines and commercial models.

Comprehensive evaluations show that our OmniXRef-Model outperforms task-specific baselines and commercial models.

Improving OCR Accuracy Through Multi-Classifier Verification of Visually Similar Characters

Improving OCR Accuracy Through Multi-Classifier Verification of Visually Similar Characters

Nuzhat Khan、Ab Al-Hadi Ab Rahman、Mohamad Anuar Kamaruddin、Muhammad Paend Bakht、Sana Fatima 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Distinguishing visually similar characters is a persistent challenge for optical character recognition (OCR) systems, causing errors in critical applications of text digitization. While preprocessing techniques improve image quality and word-level post-processing corrects spelling errors, neither addresses character-level visual ambiguity, an intrinsic property of certain character pairs that limits even state-of-the-art OCR models. We present the Character Verification Model (CVM), a character-level post-processing framework that verifies and corrects ambiguous characters in OCR outputs using specialized binary classifiers trained on visually confusable pairs such as 'O'/'0', 'Z'/'2', and 'S'/'5'. CVM operates independently of dictionary validation, making it suitable for non-lexical data including serial numbers, alphanumeric codes, and license plates. Experiments on three OCR engines (Tesseract, EasyOCR, TrOCR) and ten fonts with low to high complexity demonstrate accuracy improvements of 3.95-14.81 percentage points, with an 87.3% correction rate for visually similar character errors and a false-correction rate of only 2.3%. The modular architecture integrates with existing OCR pipelines without modification to underlying recognition models.

Thinking from Above: Efficient Spatial Reasoning with Bird's-Eye-View Visual Tokens

Thinking from Above: Efficient Spatial Reasoning with Bird's-Eye-View Visual Tokens

Faizan Siddiqui、Nirav Savaliya、Sahith Reddy Chada、Enna Sachdeva

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Existing video multimodal large language models (MLLMs) organize observations primarily in time, but embodied spatial reasoning requires organizing them in space. Consequently, their visual representations can remain locally consistent across nearby frames while lacking a persistent spatial context that is stable across viewpoints and time. We exploit depth and camera pose, which are naturally available to embodied agents, to explicitly establish this spatial correspondence. We introduce Cambrian-BEV, which extends Cambrian-S with a parameter-free geometric tokenization scheme that back-projects visual patch features from each frame into a shared bird's-eye-view (BEV) coordinate frame and averages features that fall within the same spatial cell. Thus, observations of the same physical location, regardless of viewpoint or time step, are aggregated into a common spatial token. In addition to providing a persistent scene representation, this formulation acts as an efficient compression mechanism: 128 frames containing approximately 100K DINOv3 patch features are consolidated into a fixed 27$\times$27 grid of only 729 BEV tokens, cutting LLM prefill time by $2.9\times$ and peak memory by $3.6\times$. Cambrian-BEV requires no architectural modification beyond BEV tokenization and uses a frozen DINOv3 vision encoder with a trainable multimodal projector and LLM. On VSI-Bench, our model reaches 63.48, $+8.5$ points over an identically fine-tuned frame-token control with $12.6\times$ fewer visual tokens, with particularly large gains on room size ($+24.5$) and relative direction ($+34.1$). On ReVSI, it reaches 44.68, $+4.5$ over the corresponding control.

Task Structure and Scalability in Multi-Robot Authoring

Task Structure and Scalability in Multi-Robot Authoring

Christian Marowski、Byung-Cheol Min

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multi-robot authoring can reduce completion time by distributing drawing work, but the resulting speedup depends on task structure and the allocation-routing pipeline. We investigate these relationships by representing drawings as geometric stroke graphs and comparing component-level (single-token) allocation with shared-token (decomposed) allocation, which permits multiple robots to work within a connected component. A simulation study of 385 drawings and teams of one to eight robots shows diminishing speedup gains under single-token allocation and larger mean speedups under the shared-token pipeline, each normalized by its own single-robot completion time. Shared-token execution also exhibits lower idle-time shares at larger team sizes, although it does not reduce completion time for every drawing. Comparisons across structural attribute groups reveal associations between structural descriptors and differences in scaling behavior, with greater separation between several attribute groups at larger team sizes. These findings characterize empirical scaling in the evaluated pipelines and highlight the importance of considering within-component workload and graph structure alongside component count. Additional materials are available at https://sites.google.com/view/multirobotauthoring/home.

Is a Linear Probe Evidence of a Linear Representation?

Is a Linear Probe Evidence of a Linear Representation?

Nadir Kutluozen、Christian Internò、David Klindt

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The linear representation hypothesis holds that pretrained vision models encode generative factors as linearly decodable directions in feature space, and comes with a standard methodology: train a linear probe, declare a factor represented if accuracy is high. We show this test is too permissive. Asymptotically, any sufficiently rich feature map, including a random one, admits a linear readout, so high probe accuracy is consistent with two very different geometries: a true linear world model, where the latent-aligned subspace dominates the representation, or a kernel machine, where the latents lie linearly inside a much larger curved manifold. This distinction matters because the curved structure orthogonal to the probe is relevant for out-of-distribution generalization, robustness, and compositional transfer. We propose three asymmetric diagnostics that distinguish the two cases: reverse predictivity, spectral concentration, and out-of-distribution generalization. We validate them in a fully observed simulation where ground truth is known. Applied to six pretrained vision transformers on SVG-World, our deterministic generative benchmark with paired visual styles, and to two of them on CelebA, they show pretraining produces structure beyond a random kernel but well short of a linear world model. On dSprites, 3D Shapes and MPI3D, forward decodability and latent-aligned variance dissociate. Thus, forward decoding alone does not show that the probed factors align with the dominant geometry of the representation. In summary, our work cautions about the success of linear probes as opposed to truly linear and low dimensional representations.

StopWatch: Goal Grounding for Object-goal Navigation

StopWatch: Goal Grounding for Object-goal Navigation

Xinhao Liu、Yunsheng Tian、Yuheng Qiu、Pieter Abbeel、Xi Chen 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Pretrained VLMs bring rich world knowledge and semantic priors to object-goal navigation, and fine-tuning a VLM into a navigation policy has become a common recipe. Despite this progress, success rates remain low, and it is unclear which capability these fine-tuned policies lack. Our diagnosis identifies goal grounding as the dominant failure. These policies explore well, yet often fail to notice that the goal is already in view or to estimate how far away it is. To address this failure, we ground the goal explicitly. Explicit grounding substantially improves success and reaches state-of-the-art results on six benchmarks across three scene collections. We also correct annotation defects in these benchmarks, so that future methods are evaluated more faithfully. Our analysis on the clean benchmarks shows that, with ground-truth grounding, our policy nearly matches the expert that generated its demonstrations, leaving the headroom in perception rather than policy learning. Our work points to goal grounding as a missing piece of object-goal navigation and offers a simple and effective solution to the task.

MTEdit: Packed Trajectories Beat Rollouts for Multi-Turn Image Editing

MTEdit: Packed Trajectories Beat Rollouts for Multi-Turn Image Editing

Houyuan Chen、Fanzhou Wang、Xiangyu Fan、Songlin Yang、Zhitao Yang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multi-turn image editing aims to modify an image according to a sequence of instructions, making it better aligned with human-AI collaborative creation than single-turn editing. However, constructing high-quality multi-turn training data remains challenging, as conventional sequential rollout accumulates errors and introduces visual-quality drift across editing turns. To address this challenge, we introduce MTEdit, a multi-turn image editing dataset built through a pipeline centered on packed trajectory canvas (PTC) generation. By jointly generating multiple editing states within a unified visual context, this pipeline reduces visual-quality drift across turns. Covering both step-wise and context-aware editing scenarios, MTEdit comprises 500K multi-turn image editing trajectories, with up to 8 turns and an average of 5.38 turns per trajectory, making it one of the most extensive datasets for multi-turn image editing. We further introduce MTEdit-Bench, a benchmark designed to evaluate both scenarios by assessing models' ability to follow editing instructions, maintain content consistency, resist visual-quality drift, and effectively use historical context across turns. Using MTEdit, we fine-tune Qwen-Image-Edit-Causal to obtain Qwen-Image-MTEdit. On MTEdit-Bench, we evaluate a broad range of closed-source and open-source image editing models, including Qwen-Image-MTEdit. Experimental results show that Qwen-Image-MTEdit achieves the best performance among the evaluated open-source models, demonstrating the effectiveness of MTEdit in improving models' multi-turn image editing capabilities.

PCDFusion: Proposal-Context-Detail Bayesian Rendering for Infrared-Visible Image Fusion

PCDFusion: Proposal-Context-Detail Bayesian Rendering for Infrared-Visible Image Fusion

Rui Liu、Lin Gu、Hesong Li、Ying Fu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Infrared-visible image fusion (IVIF) aims to combine visible and thermal infrared observations for human perception and downstream vision tasks. Visible and thermal infrared observations are physically complementary but unreliable in different ways. Existing deep IVIF methods mainly improve feature interaction and reconstruction, but often lack an explicit local rule for deciding when visible appearance should dominate and when thermal responses should contribute. To address this issue, we reformulate IVIF as local Bayesian posterior rendering, where fusion is controlled by posterior action weights rather than direct modality mixing. In this paper, we propose Proposal-Context-Detail Fusion (PCDFusion), a visible-anchored framework that implements this formulation through three rendering stages. PCDFusion preserves potentially useful thermal responses, updates exposure-degraded visible regions, and renders reliable band-limited infrared structures into the final luminance. Extensive experiments on public IVIF benchmarks show that PCDFusion improves fusion quality, target recovery, and downstream detection and segmentation performance under challenging illumination. The code is available at https://github.com/BIT-RuiLiu/PCDFusion.git.

2026-10-16

Diversity Without Labels: Task-Agnostic Coreset Selection Across Perception and Action

Diversity Without Labels: Task-Agnostic Coreset Selection Across Perception and Action

Julia Hindel、Benjamin Ramtoula、Kyuhwan Yeon、Nick Heppert、Paul Newman 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

While data collection for model training is relatively easy, curating high-quality datasets from large collections is inherently challenging. Coreset estimation methods address this problem by identifying the best data subsets to train models on. However, existing approaches often require proxy model training, extensive annotations, or task-specific tailoring. Unsupervised alternatives typically exploit geometric properties in foundation model feature spaces, but are limited to pairwise image-to-image comparisons or comparisons against a fixed subset. We introduce CoreDNA, an unsupervised, task-agnostic coreset estimation approach that pools neuron activations from vision foundation models into per-image and per-dataset distributions. By comparing each image's distribution to the dataset-level distribution, CoreDNA can capture both low-level visual structure and high-level semantic information across layers. We find that high-quality samples concentrate in the tails of the last layer's activation statistics, which we validate by relating them to training-based coreset methods. Building on these findings, we develop a computationally efficient selection mechanism to construct task-agnostic, high-quality coresets for selective annotation and model training. Across image classification, semantic segmentation, depth estimation, and imitation learning for end-to-end driving and mobile manipulation, CoreDNA matches or outperforms the state of the art, demonstrating unprecedented generalization across perception and action tasks. These results highlight the effectiveness of compact activation-based representations for data selection.
