2026-10-16

PONDER: Learning to Generate in the Right Order

PONDER: Learning to Generate in the Right Order

Julius Erbach、Dominik Narnhofer、Bernt Schiele、Jan Eric Lenssen、Konrad Schindler 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We introduce PONDER, a method that learns in which order an image generator should fill in content and which of its earlier predictions to revise. Masked generative models construct a sample by repeatedly filling in subsets of missing variables. The order of these partial predictions determines the context available in subsequent steps and therefore affects the quality of the final sample. Common strategies are to choose the order randomly or based on local confidence estimates. Conditioned on the partially generated sample and the generator's features, our policy chooses which positions to fill next and/or which previously filled positions to mask for later regeneration, a mechanism we call rethinking. In this way, the generation process is able to adapt to the evolving context and also to revise earlier predictions. We optimize the policy using group-relative policy gradients, with rewards that measure constraint satisfaction, human preference, or distribution quality. For the latter, we introduce FIDgrad, an image-level reward derived from the (first-order) change in Fréchet Inception Distance caused by adding a generated sample to the current feature distribution. With the generators frozen, learned ordering reduces MAR-B ImageNet FID from 4.09 to 2.78 and raises the accuracy on hard visual Sudokus from 3.9\% to 56.6\%, surpassing uncertainty-based greedy ordering (38.7\%). Rethinking further improves these results to 2.70 FID and 96.7\% accuracy, while increasing the rethinking budget of a fixed policy yields continued improvements at inference time.

We introduce PONDER, a method that learns in which order an image generator should fill in content and which of its earlier predictions to revise. Masked generative models construct a sample by repeatedly filling in subsets of missing variables. The order of these partial predictions determines the context available in subsequent steps and therefore affects the quality of the final sample. Common strategies are to choose the order randomly or based on local confidence estimates. Conditioned on the partially generated sample and the generator's features, our policy chooses which positions to fill next and/or which previously filled positions to mask for later regeneration, a mechanism we call rethinking. In this way, the generation process is able to adapt to the evolving context and also to revise earlier predictions. We optimize the policy using group-relative policy gradients, with rewards that measure constraint satisfaction, human preference, or distribution quality. For the latter, we introduce FIDgrad, an image-level reward derived from the (first-order) change in Fréchet Inception Distance caused by adding a generated sample to the current feature distribution. With the generators frozen, learned ordering reduces MAR-B ImageNet FID from 4.09 to 2.78 and raises the accuracy on hard visual Sudokus from 3.9\% to 56.6\%, surpassing uncertainty-based greedy ordering (38.7\%). Rethinking further improves these results to 2.70 FID and 96.7\% accuracy, while increasing the rethinking budget of a fixed policy yields continued improvements at inference time.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM机器人视觉遥感
2026-10-16

GeoReason: Bridging Logical Reasoning and Spatial Fidelity in Remote Sensing Segmentation

GeoReason: Bridging Logical Reasoning and Spatial Fidelity in Remote Sensing Segmentation

Cheng Fan、Han Wu、Tingzhang Luo、Minjing Dong、Yehui Tang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have significantly advanced instruction-driven perception through the "Embedding-as-Mask" paradigm. However, extending this capability to Remote Sensing (RS) reasoning segmentation remains exceptionally challenging under cluttered geographical contexts and extreme scale variations, often leading to Spatial Fidelity Degradation and Target Misidentification. We identify a critical yet underexplored bottleneck: the sequential alienation of the segmentation token [SEG] from its initial visual grounding within the autoregressive generation paradigm. Produced only at the end of long reasoning chains, [SEG] relies on visual evidence that has been progressively diluted by linguistic abstraction and autoregressive noise. This information decay is particularly catastrophic for RS targets with minuscule pixel footprints, where even marginal fidelity loss leads to localization failure. To address these bottlenecks, we propose GeoReason, a novel framework designed to fortify logical deduction and preserve spatial integrity. First, we introduce an Anticipatory Prior mechanism that injects the [SEG] token directly into the initial user query, shifting it from a terminal output to a primary condition. This paradigm shift transforms the token into a persistent spatial anchor, preventing information decay during long-form linguistic inference. Second, we enhance visual reasoning via a Location-aware Chain-of-Thought (CoT) that enforces coarse-to-fine localization using bounding boxes, coupled with a Latent Patch-to-Token Alignment (PTA) Loss that explicitly aligns the latent segmentation token with physical RS textures. Extensive experiments demonstrate that GeoReason consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods on the Earthreason and RRSIS-D benchmarks. Code is available at https://github.com/Fanc0126/GeoReason.

MOSS-Image Technical Report

MOSS-Image Technical Report

MOSI. AI

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Recent closed-source image generation systems can produce diverse visual content from simple compositions to information-dense graphics, yet open-weight text-to-image models still struggle with dense text and intricate layouts. To narrow this gap, we introduce MOSS-Image, an open generation system that combines high-fidelity visual synthesis with intent-aware composition. First, we pretrain a 5B single-stream diffusion transformer (DiT) from scratch with a carefully designed recipe, enabling bilingual long-form text rendering and precise layout control. Second, we develop an intention-aware agentic prompt enhancement (PE) module that translates diverse user requests into detailed content and layout specifications. Together, they enable MOSS-Image to generate text-rich and layout-coherent images. We further introduce LongLongTextBench (LLTB) for evaluating long-form English and Chinese text rendering, and RealLayoutArena (RLA) for end-to-end layout-rich generation. MOSS-Image achieves state-of-the-art performance among open-weight models on LLTB, with an average text coverage of 96.3%. On RLA, MOSS-Image ranks second in the end-to-end system comparison, while its backbone ranks first among generators evaluated with shared enhanced prompts. We will release the model, training recipe, and evaluation framework to support further research and promote the text-to-image community.

UniPolar: Joint Image and Polarimetric Restoration under Compound Degradations via Physical Consistency Learning

UniPolar: Joint Image and Polarimetric Restoration under Compound Degradations via Physical Consistency Learning

Yang Jin、Guangyu Guo、Linghao Shen、Binglu Wang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Polarimetric image restoration aims to recover polarization angle images and reliable polarimetric quantities from degraded observations. Beyond visual fidelity, it also requires preserving the physical relationships underlying reliable polarimetric estimation. However, existing methods typically address either device- or environment-induced degradations in isolation, limiting their applicability to color polarization filter array (CPFA) imaging, where both often coexist. Such compound degradations jointly perturb image intensities and cross angle polarization differences. Consequently, improved image fidelity alone does not necessarily ensure accurate Stokes, DoLP, and AoLP recovery. To address this challenge, we propose UniPolar, a coupled Image Stokes framework for joint image polarimetric restoration from compound-degraded CPFA observations. Specifically, UniPolar employs two complementary streams to reconstruct four polarization-angle images and directly predict Stokes difference components. To coordinate these streams, Degradation Conditioned Cross Domain Interaction combines Dual Domain Frequency Interaction for frequency selective evidence exchange with Degradation Conditioned Modulation for condition adaptive feature correction. Furthermore, at the output level, Image Stokes Physical Consistency Learning aligns image derived and directly predicted Stokes components while regularizing angular fidelity and the physical feasibility of DoLP. On the compound-degradation benchmark, UniPolar achieves average image and DoLP PSNR of 31.522 dB and 24.097 dB, respectively, with an average AoLP error of 16.833 degree. These results underscore the importance of jointly considering visual fidelity and polarimetric reliability, offering a physically grounded perspective on learning based restoration under compound degradations.

SemPurify: Semantics-Aware Data Purification for Black-box Backdoor Defense

SemPurify: Semantics-Aware Data Purification for Black-box Backdoor Defense

Jiawei Yu、Syed Irfan Ali Meerza、Yi Wu、Amir Sadovnik、Jian Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Backdoor attacks have emerged as a critical security threat in machine learning, where adversaries embed triggers within poisoned training samples to manipulate a model's predictions on targeted inputs while leaving its general behavior unaffected. This stealthy nature makes backdoor attacks difficult to detect, as the model functions normally until activated by a specific malicious trigger. Current purification-based defenses use image transformations (e.g., blurring) to disrupt trigger patterns but often degrade image quality. Even with advanced restoration techniques like diffusion models, these approaches can still alter visual or semantic information, reducing utility for downstream tasks. To address this, we propose the first semantics-aware data purification framework for black-box backdoor defense, SemPurify, which explicitly incorporates multi-level semantics (i.e., low-level semantic embeddings and high-level image tags) within a text-to-image (T2I) framework to guide image restoration during purification. Using this semantic information, the T2I model subsequently refines the transformed samples, helping preserve visual details and semantic information during restoration. Extensive experiments spanning multiple backdoor scenarios, i.e., classification models, CLIP-style encoders, and generative models, demonstrate that SemPurify reliably removes backdoors while effectively preserving semantic information for downstream tasks.

Stabilizing Illumination Conditioning in SSM-Based Low-Light Image Enhancement with Bottleneck Prompt Sidecars

Stabilizing Illumination Conditioning in SSM-Based Low-Light Image Enhancement with Bottleneck Prompt Sidecars

Wei Chen Ng、Qiyi Xie、Jian Ding Tan、Yan Kang Lee、Abdulrab Hakim Qaid Abdullah 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Low-light image enhancement (LLIE) requires more than brightness amplification because real low-light images often exhibit spatially varying illumination, amplified noise, color distortion, and local detail loss. Recent SSM- and Mamba-based restorers provide efficient long-range modeling, yet the route for introducing illumination priors into such backbones remains insufficiently studied. This study addresses safe illumination conditioning for SSM-based LLIE through a bottleneck-side PromptAdapter. A lightweight illumination estimator first predicts a single-channel illumination map, which is then converted into prompt tokens and injected through a residual sidecar adapter at the bottleneck, thereby avoiding direct modification of the state-space core. Under a locked three-seed evaluation protocol on LOL-v1, LOLv2-Real, and LOLv2-Synthetic, the PromptAdapter improves over the Base model by 0.94 dB, 1.24 dB, and 0.93 dB in PSNR, respectively, while also improving SSIM, LPIPS, and masked dark-region metrics. The clearest real-data evidence appears on LOLv2-Real. A Learned-Fixed control on LOL-v1 indicates that the present results support the utility of the sidecar conditioning pathway more strongly than a strict claim of illumination-conditioned superiority, and the LOLv2-Synthetic result remains stability-conditioned. These findings suggest that, in SSM-based LLIE, conditioning design can be as important as the conditioning signal itself.

Image Moment Estimation for Crowded Datasets

Image Moment Estimation for Crowded Datasets

Andy Zhang、Oscar Mickelin、Amit Singer

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Single-particle cryogenic electron microscopy (cryo-EM) is an imaging technique capable of reconstructing high-resolution three-dimensional (3D) structure of biological macromolecules from a set of noisy projection images taken at random viewing directions. The mathematical model for cryo-EM images typically assumes that each image contains exactly one particle, which is centered at the origin. However, many experimental datasets contain particle images which include multiple particles: one central particle and a few crowding particles. This deviation from the model leads to inaccurate estimation of image statistics, such as the low-order image moments. To account for this, we propose a method to estimate image moments while correcting for the effect of crowding particles. This method can be run directly from a set of cryo-EM micrographs with picked particles, and we demonstrate its efficacy on a wide array of synthetic data.

Alignment of visual brain encoding models with individual perceptual similarity judgments

Alignment of visual brain encoding models with individual perceptual similarity judgments

Daniel Chong、Haomiao Chen、Keith W Jamison、Raja Marjieh、Ilia Sucholutsky 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Encoding models that predict brain activation responses to images have traditionally been trained to maximize accuracy when predicting observed brain data. However, their internal representations may not reflect individual human perception. In this work, we investigate whether we can fine-tune brain encoding models to better align their representations with individuals' similarity judgments. Using similarity judgments of 100 images from 8 participants in the Natural Scenes Dataset (NSD), we adapt a generic human similarity judgment model (DreamSim) separately for each participant to obtain personalized perceptual similarity models. These models are then used to obtain subject-specific similarity structure across images in their encoding model training sets. We fine-tune each participant's baseline encoder in two conditions, aligning its representations with either generic or personalized similarity judgments, with the same initialization and objective. We find that baseline brain encoders reflect within-subject perceptual similarity more strongly than measured fMRI responses and preserve intersubject correlations present in measured fMRI, but fail to reflect those of human perceptual similarity judgments. Fine-tuning with generic, population-level perceptual similarity information results in better alignment with human judgments than baseline encoders, but these models still fail to capture intersubject correlations of human judgments. However, fine-tuning with personalized perceptual similarity information results in models that are the most aligned with human perceptual similarity judgments and also reflect intersubject correlations of human judgments. Our framework provides an approach for bootstrapping human similarity judgment data into more individualized, human-aligned brain encoding models of vision.

Scaling Online Exploration for Robot Agents

Scaling Online Exploration for Robot Agents

Runhan Huang、Xiangcheng Zhang、Haoyu Chen、Yitong Li、Yen-Jen Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robot learning has advanced by scaling demonstration datasets and model capacity; however, deploying a new robot embodiment or task typically necessitates a dedicated teleoperation corpus. The resulting policies remain bounded by their imitation distributions, limiting generalization and continual self-improvement. We introduce RAX (Robot Agent eXploration), an open-source framework for robot agents centered on scaling exploration: enabling agents to improve by acquiring online experience and synthesizing executable programs. In RAX, a general-purpose coding agent generates robot control code, operating without action demonstration data, hand-engineered action primitives, or value-function guidance. Through online exploration, the agent identifies decision sequences that achieve task goals and compiles them into modular programs encapsulating the full perception-planning-control loop. Using RAX, we present a systematic multi-embodiment study on scaling online exploration across precision and long-horizon manipulation tasks using single-arm, bimanual, dexterous, mobile, and humanoid systems in simulation and on real robots. Generalization performance on unseen environments improves consistently as exploration scales. In simulation, RAX outperforms top baseline models by +37.4% on average. On real robots, RAX autonomously learns memory-dependent, articulated, high-precision, deformable, and long-horizon mobile manipulation tasks without human demonstrations, continually improving performance by composing verified skills from prior exploration sessions. As exploration scales, we observe emergent capabilities including active perception, error recovery, safety guardrails, and human-robot collaboration. We open-source RAX as a generalist robot-agent platform in which new robot embodiments are integrated through their APIs alone, without teleoperated data collection or policy training.

We open-source RAX as a generalist robot-agent platform in which new robot embodiments are integrated through their APIs alone, without teleoperated data collection or policy training.

Mothership: A Two Tier Autonomous Refuse Collection System for Pedestrian Campuses

Mothership: A Two Tier Autonomous Refuse Collection System for Pedestrian Campuses

Wei Wang、Yiru Veronika Wang、Yihan Wang、Zihao Li、Xiaohui Liang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Refuse collection in pedestrian campuses, parks and mixed-use districts remains manual because compaction trucks cannot reach bins located on walkways, courtyards and building service doors. This paper presents Mothership, a two-tier autonomous refuse collection system in which an electric compaction truck (the mothership) carries two low-speed sidewalk robots (RoboCarts) that fetch standard two-wheel bins, deliver them to the truck, return the empty bins to their original positions and re-dock on the truck for charging. The architecture separates responsibilities by scale: the truck performs road driving, compaction, lifting and energy supply, while the carts perform only sidewalk transport and bin grasping. This split removes the lift-and-tip mechanism from the sidewalk vehicle, keeping it light and low, and relaxes cart-to-truck docking tolerance to the +/-100 mm capture window of a standard automated lifter. We describe the system architecture, the bin-handling and docking interfaces, and a mission-planning formulation that decomposes a route into truck stop points and cart tasks. A discrete-event simulation over synthetic campus layouts with 2-16 bins/ha quantifies throughput as a function of bin density, cart count and stop-point radius. A two-cart configuration serves 20-29 bins/h. Throughput is governed by an interaction between cart count and stop-point radius: a single cart performs best with many close stops, whereas three or four carts need wide stops (80 m) to stay busy, reaching 35-49 bins/h at moderate-to-high densities. The best stop radius therefore has to be chosen together with the fleet size, not fixed in advance.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM视频理解机器人视觉

Event-STVG: Spatio-Temporal Video Grounding for Long-Form Event Reasoning

Event-STVG: Spatio-Temporal Video Grounding for Long-Form Event Reasoning

Yizhe Zhang、Xujia Wang、Songqi Tang、Xujie Tang、Leo Jiahao Jiang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Spatio-Temporal Video Grounding (STVG) aims to jointly localize a target in space and time given a natural-language query. Despite continued advances, existing STVG benchmarks remain largely centered on localizing targets through instance-specific descriptions, leaving them largely decoupled from long-form reasoning increasingly emphasized in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). In this paper, we introduce Event-STVG, a task that shifts STVG from locating what is described toward event-level reasoning by unifying long-form context, repeated-event reasoning, event localization, role inference, and multi-target grounding. To facilitate research on Event-STVG, we construct a large-scale benchmark comprising 4,016 annotated event occurrences across soccer and seven additional domains. Experiments with state-of-the-art MLLMs reveal substantial difficulty, with the strongest model achieving only 5.4% m-vIoU at the 10-minute horizon. Across domains, we consistently observe confusion among repeated same-type events, spatial grounding bottlenecks, and rare success in jointly grounding all required roles. Event-STVG establishes a new challenge for visual understanding in MLLMs, calling for future models that can jointly reason about complex events across space and time. Code and annotations are available at https://github.com/THU-KEG/EventSTVG

End-to-End Learned Image Coding: From Rate-Distortion Optimization to Practical Deployment

End-to-End Learned Image Coding: From Rate-Distortion Optimization to Practical Deployment

Ming Lu、Junqi Shi、Zhan Ma

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

End-to-end learned image coding (LIC) has achieved substantial improvements in rate--distortion (R--D) performance, yet practical deployment requires addressing a broader set of system-level constraints. This article provides a deployment-oriented review of LIC, with particular attention to the transition from high-performance neural compression models to practical codecs. We first summarize the basic end-to-end coding framework, including learned transforms, quantization, entropy modeling, and R--D optimization. We then review practical extensions that are increasingly important for deployment, including variable-rate coding and rate control, integer-precision inference and cross-platform consistency, robustness and communication resilience, lightweight and hardware-aware implementation, and standardization and interoperability. To study these issues under a common setting, we further introduce TinyLIC as an open benchmark and reproducible case study for rate--distortion--complexity analysis. Experimental results illustrate the trade-offs among compression efficiency, computational complexity, numerical consistency, and robustness. In particular, TinyLIC with approximately 10 KMACs/pixel decoding complexity substantially outperforms the H.265/HEVC image-profile anchor on Kodak, while the 50 KMACs/pixel configuration achieves performance comparable to the H.266/VVC image-profile anchor on several test sets. The review highlights practical constraints and design trade-offs that remain central to the deployment and interoperability of learned image codecs. Materials are accessible at https://njuvision.github.io/TinyLIC.

Shape-Aware Neural Time Fields for Rigid-Body Motion and Manipulation Planning

Shape-Aware Neural Time Fields for Rigid-Body Motion and Manipulation Planning

Yanming Zhou、Yuchen Liu、Ahmed H. Qureshi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Rigid-body motion planning is fundamental to robotic transport and manipulation, where both object position and orientation determine whether a body can move through cluttered environments. This coupling makes planning chal- lenging because translation and rotation can be constrained very differently by surrounding geometry. We propose Shape- Aware Neural Time Fields, a physics-informed neural motion planning framework that learns a reusable travel-time, or cost- to-go, representation for rigid-body motion while explicitly accounting for these distinct geometric restrictions. The learned field captures local motion difficulty in translation and rotation and is paired with a sampling-based planner that balances progress toward the goal with the cost of reaching nearby poses. We evaluate the framework across 2D and 3D rigid- body planning tasks and non-prehensile manipulation through object pushing in diverse environments. Results show substantial improvements in planning success and inference efficiency over physics-informed neural planning baselines, while remaining competitive with classical sampling-based planners at significantly lower planning times. Ablation studies further validate the contributions of both the learned representation and the proposed planning strategy. Finally, closed-loop pushing experiments under execution deviations and external disturbances demonstrate that fast replanning enables reactive and adaptive task execution.

Cross-Person Identity Change Under Source-Coupled Multi-Human Generation: A Controlled UniPortrait Study

Cross-Person Identity Change Under Source-Coupled Multi-Human Generation: A Controlled UniPortrait Study

Edrees Al-Edreesi、Ghaleb Al-Gaphari、Muneer Alsurori、Mohammed Edrees、Fouad Al-Hamdani 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multi-human personalization must account for identity changes in people outside the nominal target of a generative operation. We characterize this behavior in UniPortrait, without introducing a new preservation algorithm. Two sequential studies use 119 accepted two-person baseline scenes and three fixed output seeds, measuring continuous reference-based facial similarity with InsightFace \buffalo. In Study~1, source-coupled single-ID target-only generation (A1) produces negative mean transitions for both the protected person ($-0.567904$) and target ($-0.236904$), indicating broad perturbation rather than established successful repair with collateral loss. In Study~2, native dual-ID generation (A2) yields more positive paired contrasts: protected $+0.512918$ (95\% case-cluster CI $[0.471434,0.550259]$) and target $+0.158853$ ($[0.118903,0.197523]$), across 287 jointly measurable pairs from 111 cases. Shared baseline similarity cancels algebraically in these paired contrasts, leaving a matched POST comparison. Validation designed after the primary result freeze finds directional agreement with an independent identity evaluator and blinded human judgments; within-\buffalo\ nuisance models provide supportive sensitivity evidence. The findings characterize intact native regimes, whose references, routing, adapters, and token counts co-vary. They do not establish component causality, categorical identity preservation, successful localized editing, or generalization beyond the evaluated implementation and measurable cohort.

Spatial Memory by Agents, for Agents

Spatial Memory by Agents, for Agents

Qianwei Wang、Ce Zhang、Zhanpeng Luo、Pengpeng Su、Shuo Yang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Spatial memory enables robots to reuse past observations for localization, navigation, and question answering. Designing such memory involves jointly deciding what to retain and how to retrieve it for diverse semantic, metric, and temporal needs. We investigate whether coding agents can automate this design process through iterative implementation and evaluation. Under a fixed development budget, a Designer Agent writes and revises code for memory construction and retrieval using feedback from a fixed Query Agent. The resulting implementations are frozen before building memories from held-out observation histories, without access to downstream questions, and evaluated across five complementary task tracks using the same Query Agent. Across three independent design searches, Codex / GPT-6 Astra averages 44.23 on the equally weighted five-track score, versus 29.32 for ReMEmbR, the strongest evaluated baseline supporting all five tracks, a 50.9% relative improvement. Qualitative analysis relates performance differences to evidence retention and retrieval, while identifying grounding and evidence-use failures. These results demonstrate the potential of coding agents to automate spatial memory design, while revealing task-dependent trade-offs in performance and computational cost. Project page: https://ba-fa-3344.github.io/.

EgoP$^{2}$C: Internalizing Plan-Perceive-Compose for Spatial Reasoning in Egocentric Video

EgoP$^{2}$C: Internalizing Plan-Perceive-Compose for Spatial Reasoning in Egocentric Video

Chaoyu Li、Arka Sadhu、Yuliang Li、Hyo Jin Kim、Yuning Chai 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Answering complex spatial reasoning questions from egocentric video requires determining what evidence is needed, acquiring targeted observations, and combining them through multiple reasoning steps. Existing vision-language models (VLMs) struggle to perform this process reliably. Agentic systems address this challenge by querying external vision and geometry tools, but training their reasoning leaves these tools unchanged, so inaccurate observations can still lead to incorrect answers. We introduce EgoP$^{2}$C, a framework that internalizes a plan-perceive-compose process within a single VLM. To this end, we first train specialized Planner and Perception teachers using separate adapters (Dual-LoRA), then fine-tune the corresponding experts in a mixture-of-experts (MoE) student under their supervision. We subsequently train the student on trajectories generated by its current policy using a segment-level extension of multi-teacher on-policy distillation (MOPD), assigning different trajectory segments to different supervisors. The Planner teacher supervises which evidence-seeking questions to ask, when to stop gathering evidence, and how to compose the final answer. The Perception teacher supervises the video-grounded answers to those questions. At inference, the student executes this adaptive workflow end-to-end, without external spatial tools. Across three spatial reasoning benchmarks, EgoP$^{2}$C students built from Qwen3-VL-8B and SenseNova-SI-1.3-8B outperform their base models by an average of 9.2 and 7.5 percentage points, respectively. Across both backbones, EgoP$^{2}$C reduces inference FLOPs by 65.8-66.3% and achieves $1.29$-$1.49\times$ speedups over their shared Dual-LoRA teacher.

ProactGen: Learning Proactive Clarification for Video Generation

ProactGen: Learning Proactive Clarification for Video Generation

Zhixiao Wang、Yang Chen、Yifan Xu、Chenbin Li、Yue Chang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Recent advances in video generation let users create high-quality videos from natural language descriptions, yet obtaining results that match user intent remains time-consuming: individual generations are computationally expensive, and underspecified prompts force repeated cycles of generation, inspection, and revision. Existing video prompt optimization methods improve generation through automatic rewriting, but cannot supply the ideas and preferences that were not in the initial description. We propose ProactGen, a video generation framework that learns proactive clarification, eliciting user intent through questions grounded in generated video drafts. Specifically, we train a vision-language model as a questioning policy with reinforcement learning: observing the initial prompt and a video draft, it learns to ask the questions most valuable to downstream generation, rewarded by the alignment between the final generated video and a target reference. We further find that effective questioning does not require drafts of final-output quality, and exploit this property by generating drafts with fewer denoising steps, keeping the added cost of clarification low. Experiments show that ProactGen improves target alignment over direct generation by at least 1.8x the gain of the strongest baselines. Moreover, a single round of ProactGen already exceeds the alignment that the strongest baseline reaches after four refinement rounds, in 29% of its time.

BRICE: Brain-inspired Recurrent Illusory Contour Encoder for Point Cloud Completion

BRICE: Brain-inspired Recurrent Illusory Contour Encoder for Point Cloud Completion

Yunlei Wang、Dawei Li、Xue-song Tang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Mainstream point cloud completion methods are unanimously based on deep learning architectures. On one hand, these methods tend to repetitively stacking attention modules to enhance network performance, which drifts away from the neat design of unified and interpret-able networks; on the other hand, nearly all existing architectures rely solely on feed-forward connections to propagate information, neglecting feedback from deeper layers. The recurrent neural architecture, formed by feedforward-feedback loops, have shown its ability to generate Illusory Contours (ICs) in the V1 brain region of mammals; and ICs are essentially a completion mechanism of the brain for visual information. Motivated by this, we design a Brain-inspired Recurrent Illusory Contour Encoder (BRICE) to "imagine" what is missing in the incomplete 3D point clouds. Building upon the foundation model of Predictive Coding (PC), the plug-and-play nature enables BRICE to be seamlessly integrated into mainstream point cloud completion baselines. Experimental results show that BRICE can enhance various completion baselines on different 3D datasets such as MVP2K, PCN, and PlantPCom. Our code: Https://github.com/wangyunlei-wyl/BRICE.

2026-10-16

A100: A Hybrid Vision Foundation Model for Agricultural Plant Image Analysis

A100: A Hybrid Vision Foundation Model for Agricultural Plant Image Analysis

Mayur Pratim Das、Ajai Dubey、Aksshay Mathew P、Sana Afreen、Rishi Kumar Soni 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The use of accurate vision models for crop monitoring, disease diagnosis, and plant phenotyping in agriculture is hindered by the lack of expertly annotated data, especially in developing countries. We propose A100, an agricultural vision foundation model that is pre-trained using self-supervised learning on 3 million unlabeled images from fields covering more than 100 crop species. A100 uses a hybrid CNN-Transformer backbone that fuses MBConv stages for local feature extraction with Transformer stages for global contextual representation. A100 uses pre-training with the integration of DINO self-distillation, iBOT masked patch reconstruction, and KoLeo uniformity regularization, resulting in features that are better than ImageNet-12K supervised representations for agricultural applications without any fine-tuning. By utilizing a frozen backbone, the accuracy for crop classification of A100 is 95.37%, which is +2.56% better than ImageNet-12K, and reduces the leaf count error by 10.4% on the GroMo25 phenotyping benchmark, proving that the same backbone can be used for classification and regression tasks. After full fine-tuning, the performance of A100 is 99.90% on 32-class crop identification and 99.02% on 44-class disease detection. On the difficult PlantDoc dataset, A100 outperforms all baselines, such as CvT-13 (64.89% against 63.56%). Remarkably, A100 obtains 98.01% accuracy on plant classification using just 1% of the labelled data (about 284 images per category), showing great label efficiency for use cases with limited labeling. Under an identical protocol, A100 also outperforms the publicly released agricultural foundation models FoMo4Wheat and LeafVision by more than 14 points at 1% labels. We plan to publicly release our pretrained model to allow agricultural professionals to adapt A100 for various plant analysis applications using limited annotations.

2026-10-16

UniField++: A Unified Agentic Framework with Loop Reasoning for MRI Field-Strength Enhancement

UniField++: A Unified Agentic Framework with Loop Reasoning for MRI Field-Strength Enhancement

Yiyang Lin、Chenhui Wang、Tianliang Yao、Shuaibo Li、Zijun Sun 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

MRI field-strength enhancement seeks to combine the accessibility of low-field scanners with the image quality of high-field scanners. Yet, existing methods remain prone to anatomical hallucinations, as uniform processing leaves difficult brain regions insufficiently examined and scarce paired data limit their learning of how tissue contrast and anatomical visibility change across field strengths. To address these challenges, we propose UniField++, a two-agent framework that unifies enhancement tasks across multiple field-strength transitions, including 64mT-to-3T and 3T-to-7T. Unified learning allows both the VLM-based planner and the flow-matching-based enhancer to draw on field-dependent image characteristics observed across tasks, rather than relying on a single transition. Trained with radiologists' annotations of hallucination-prone regions and corresponding correction plans, the planner predicts regional risk and formulates targeted restoration strategies. Guided by these plans, the enhancer performs loop reasoning along the flow-matching trajectory, repeatedly processing high-risk regions for additional refinement. Together, cross-transition learning and risk-guided refinement support anatomically faithful enhancement across field strengths. Across the evaluated tasks, UniField++ improves PSNR by an average of 2.74 dB and SSIM by 14.05 percentage points over comparison methods, with corresponding gains of 3.97 dB and 13.92 percentage points over the ablation baseline. It also maintains enhancement quality on an external cohort with an unseen field strength and improves downstream whole-brain segmentation Macro Dice by an average of 0.1457 over the original low-field images. Code is available at https://github.com/CUHK-AIM-Group/UniField_plusplus.

ANCHOR: Audio-Visually Grounded Chain-of-Thought Reasoning Benchmark

ANCHOR: Audio-Visually Grounded Chain-of-Thought Reasoning Benchmark

Joel Julin、Souraja Kundu、Liza Dahiya、George Z. Wei、Liuyue Xie 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) perform well on audio-visual question answering, but correct answers do not necessarily imply that their stated reasoning is grounded in relevant perceptual evidence. Existing benchmarks mainly evaluate final-answer accuracy, offering limited insight into whether model-generated rationales correctly identify visual regions, temporal intervals, and audio events supporting predictions. We introduce ANCHOR, a benchmark and evaluation framework for measuring grounded-rationale alignment in multimodal reasoning, comprising 2,319 videos and 8,272 question-answer pairs with structured reasoning traces, temporal evidence, spatial bounding boxes, and salient audio cues, including a human-refined test set of 224 videos and 975 question-answer pairs. We separately measure answer correctness, hallucination, logical divergence, and conciseness, with direct spatial and temporal grounding measurements on a focused subset. Across the full benchmark, twelve contemporary MLLMs show a substantial alignment gap: models with comparable answer performance can differ markedly in whether their explanations are supported by annotated evidence. On a matched subset of 100 videos and 446 question-answer pairs, rankings remain highly stable across human-refined, Gemini-generated, and GPT-generated references (mean Pearson r=0.99), providing evidence against model-reference circularity within this subset. Grounding-cue ablations of three MLLMs on the full human-refined test set reveal a consistent modality hierarchy: removing video reduces accuracy by 38.4-41.8 points, while removing bounding boxes (3.2-7.7 points) and audio (4.2-5.6 points) causes smaller, complementary, model-dependent drops. These findings show answer-only evaluation obscures important grounding failures and position ANCHOR as a testbed for evaluating accurate and evidence-aligned audio-visual reasoning.

2026-10-16

MambaAct: Efficient Mamba Action Experts for Flow-Matching Vision-Language-Action Models

MambaAct: Efficient Mamba Action Experts for Flow-Matching Vision-Language-Action Models

Khuyen Pham、Tran Nguyen Le、Edoardo Fazzari、Cesare Stefanini

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Vision-language-action (VLA) models have shown strong promise for generalist robotic manipulation, but their inference cost remains a major bottleneck for real-time control. This limitation is especially pronounced in flow-matching VLAs such as $π_{0.5}$, where the action expert is evaluated repeatedly during iterative denoising. We introduce MambaAct, an efficient Mamba action expert for flow-matching VLA policies. MambaAct retains the pretrained visual-language prefix encoder of $π_{0.5}$, while replacing its transformer-based action expert with a compact Mamba-based action expert. Within this expert, Mamba serves as the action mixer that models dependencies across the noisy continuous-action sequence. To preserve visual-language conditioning without repeated token-level cross-attention, MambaAct uses a lightweight Context Compressor to map Vision-language model (VLM) prefix tokens into a compact conditioning signal that gates and modulates each Mamba action block. MambaAct uses a $10\times$ smaller action expert and reduces flow-matching denoising latency by $64.8\%$ on LIBERO and $58.8\%$ in real-world deployment, while improving mean LIBERO success by $1.08$ percentage points across three independent fine-tuning seeds and maintaining gains on LIBERO-Plus and real-world benchmarks. Our code and model weights are publicly available at https://github.com/giakhuyendihoc/MambaAct.

Vision-language-action (VLA) models have shown strong promise for generalist robotic manipulation, but their inference cost remains a major bottleneck for real-time control. This limitation is especially pronounced in flow-matching VLAs such as $π_{0.5}$, where the action expert is evaluated repeatedly during iterative denoising. We introduce MambaAct, an efficient Mamba action expert for flow-matching VLA policies. MambaAct retains the pretrained visual-language prefix encoder of $π_{0.5}$, while replacing its transformer-based action expert with a compact Mamba-based action expert. Within this expert, Mamba serves as the action mixer that models dependencies across the noisy continuous-action sequence. To preserve visual-language conditioning without repeated token-level cross-attention, MambaAct uses a lightweight Context Compressor to map Vision-language model (VLM) prefix tokens into a compact conditioning signal that gates and modulates each Mamba action block. MambaAct uses a $10\times$ smaller action expert and reduces flow-matching denoising latency by $64.8\%$ on LIBERO and $58.8\%$ in real-world deployment, while improving mean LIBERO success by $1.08$ percentage points across three independent fine-tuning seeds and maintaining gains on LIBERO-Plus and real-world benchmarks. Our code and model weights are publicly available at https://github.com/giakhuyendihoc/MambaAct.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM
2026-10-16

CatchUp: Depth-Aware Re-Admission of Visual Tokens for Efficient Large Vision-Language Models

CatchUp: Depth-Aware Re-Admission of Visual Tokens for Efficient Large Vision-Language Models

Eunju Park、Uiwon Hwang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) repeatedly process hundreds of visual tokens across decoder layers, incurring substantial computation and memory costs. Existing inference-time visual token pruning methods often irreversibly remove low-ranked tokens, although the set of visual tokens receiving high query-conditioned attention can change across decoder depth. Recoverable routing preserves skipped tokens for later reconsideration, but re-admits them with stale representations after bypassing intermediate transformations. We further find that replacing stale re-admitted states with their current-depth oracle states from a full-token reference trajectory substantially improves grounding performance. We therefore propose CatchUp, a training-free framework for depth-aware visual-token re-admission. CatchUp repeatedly re-evaluates query-conditioned visual relevance, processes only selected visual tokens through each decoder interval, and preserves deferred tokens for later re-admission. Upon re-admission, CatchUp approximates missed representation evolution using reference-to-current transition signals from semantically and spatially related visual tokens. Across three referring-expression grounding datasets and six multimodal benchmarks, CatchUp achieves the highest average relative retention in both benchmark groups at 64-, 128-, and 192-token budgets. At a 64-token budget with 88.9% fewer active visual tokens, CatchUp retains 92.17% of the full model's performance on average across six multimodal benchmarks while reducing end-to-end latency by 8.0%. Code is available at https://github.com/pej0918/CatchUp

LonGaussian: Tracking Foundation Priors for Long-Context Feedforward Gaussian Reconstruction

LonGaussian: Tracking Foundation Priors for Long-Context Feedforward Gaussian Reconstruction

Zhoufeng Xie、Guanjun Wu、Shuai Zhang、Xinggang Wang、Wenyu Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Reconstructing high-quality, renderable 3D scenes from long videos is essential for robotic perception and embodied intelligence. Many feed-forward Gaussian reconstruction methods rapidly predict 3D scenes from a small set of images, but extending them to longer sequences presents two bottlenecks: jointly processing all views increases GPU memory requirements, while accumulating pixel-aligned predictions redundantly represents the same surfaces and increases storage and rendering costs. We present LonGaussian, a streaming feed-forward framework that reconstructs compact, globally renderable Gaussian scenes from long image sequences with known intrinsics and unknown camera poses. Built on a pretrained streaming 3D reconstruction backbone, LonGaussian decodes long-context geometric features into dense Gaussians. Within each chunk, multi-frame point tracks link repeated observations of the same surface and guide a learned merger to consolidate the corresponding Gaussian predictions. Rendered-depth consistency filtering further suppresses Gaussians from other chunks that incorrectly occlude locally reconstructed surfaces. On DL3DV and RealEstate10K benchmarks, LonGaussian improves novel-view rendering quality while using substantially fewer Gaussians than the evaluated feed-forward baselines.

From GAN-Based Models to GPT-5: A Taxonomy-Guided Study of Human Performance in Detecting Deepfake Face Images

From GAN-Based Models to GPT-5: A Taxonomy-Guided Study of Human Performance in Detecting Deepfake Face Images

Zhiqing Guo、Lixin Jia、Luning Sun、Xiaowei Chen、Guofeng Yu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Generative models for deepfakes have rapidly evolved from GAN-based architectures to diffusion models and multimodal large language models (MLLMs). However, empirical research on human performance in detecting deepfake face images has not kept pace. It remains unclear if (and how) detection performance varies across generative models and whether model-specific training improves detection more effectively than general-purpose training. In this paper, we propose the \emph{Deepfake Face Artifact Taxonomy} (DFAT), a cross-model taxonomy of human-recognizable visual artifacts in deepfake face images, based on which we build both general-purpose and model-specific training materials. Through an online experiment ($n = 524$), we examined human performance in detecting deepfake face images and evaluated training efficacy across five representative generative models: three GAN-based, one diffusion-based, and one MLLM (GPT-5). Our results show that participants' detection accuracy was strongly model-dependent and that images generated by more recent and advanced models were not necessarily harder to detect. After training, participants achieved significant performance gains across most conditions, and their improvement varied by model. Notably, model-specific training generally yielded greater numerical gains than general-purpose training. In a five-month follow-up test ($n = 342$), we observed no significant decline in detection accuracy and no significant increase in perceived stress. Overall, our findings provide actionable insights for designing effective, sustainable, and targeted training programs to counter the growing threat posed by deepfakes.

Video Diffusion Avatars: Taming Video Diffusion Models into Controllable 3D Avatars

Video Diffusion Avatars: Taming Video Diffusion Models into Controllable 3D Avatars

Simon Giebenhain、Tobias Kirschstein、Liam Schoneveld、Davide Davoli、Zhe Chen 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Video diffusion models (VDMs) trained on internet-scale data encode a rich prior on appearance, motion, and physics that is difficult to replicate with explicit 3D rendering and costly 3D data capture. We introduce Video Diffusion Avatars (VDAs), a method that creates photo-realistic, multi-view consistent avatars from a single image by fine-tuning a VDM for precise control over camera viewpoint, facial expression, and upper-body pose. Departing from 3D morphable models (3DMMs), VDAs use a learned expression space that lifts the expressiveness ceiling of 3DMMs, e.g., for the tongue and mouth interior. However, learned expression codes exhibit heavy viewpoint bias, as our DISTRACT benchmark reveals. We show that this bias can be removed through disentanglement, enabling their use as driving signals for multi-view consistent avatars. Camera and upper-body pose are controlled jointly via proxy-mesh renderings, giving independent control over expression, pose, and viewpoint. A camera-triplet training strategy makes the approach efficient: our models train for 34 hours on a single A100 using only public multi-view data. VDAs outperform state-of-the-art single-image 3D avatars on novel-view reenactment, reducing face LPIPS by 29% and improving perceptual quality (JOD) by 66% on BecomingLit. A static 3DGS reconstruction probe further confirms that our generations are the most multi-view consistent. Built on a video prior, VDAs produce effects beyond the reach of explicit renderers, including physically plausible hair dynamics, shadows, reflections, and refractions.

ADA 2025: A Multimodal Dataset for Water-Deficit Stress Detection in Sugar Beets

ADA 2025: A Multimodal Dataset for Water-Deficit Stress Detection in Sugar Beets

Piper Powell、Robert Franz、Amos Smith、Isaak Ihorst、Joachim Hertzberg 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Research and development into artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning (ML) applications in crop management are hindered by a lack of suitable public datasets. Whereas applications in disease and pest detection benefit from a comparatively higher amount of available data, applications like water-deficit stress detection often have very few if any public datasets available. Moreover, most datasets that are available are single-modality and frequently RGB-only, leaving the early detection and enhanced performance offered by other methods, and particularly by multimodal approaches, out of reach. Datasets with accurate metadata, higher numbers of plants, and smaller intervals between data points are also scarce. To address this, we publicly release ADA 2025, a >500 GB comprehensive multimodal dataset of young sugar beet plants under healthy and water-deficit conditions combining thermal, hyperspectral, RGB, and depth imagery along with relevant environmental and metadata and both relative water content (RWC) and leaf thickness as ground truth sources. Exemplary results showcase the dataset's validity in terms of differences detectable between stressed and healthy plants.

Exemplary results showcase the dataset's validity in terms of differences detectable between stressed and healthy plants.

Execution Before Memory: Diagnosing Memory Failures in Video World Models

Execution Before Memory: Diagnosing Memory Failures in Video World Models

Weiyang Jin、Jiwen Yu、Sihan Xu、Yuwei Niu、Xihui Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

A memory test for a video world model should say whether the scene was set, whether the asked change was carried out, and whether the last frame shows the result. Current tests fold these into one number, so a low score is easy to read as forgetting. When the change is not carried out, the hidden state transition cannot appear, so the test is not really a test of memory. We introduce MIST, an evaluation that separates the change from the memory it should leave behind. The model first sets the scene while the change stays hidden, and only later is asked to show it, with leakage before that point kept under control. This separation locates the bottleneck: the model sets the scene but does not carry out the instructed change. The same gap appears in general memory evaluations. On that basis we propose M3-Agent (Make the Missing Move), which recursively revises the details of the input instruction so that the model successfully carries out the requested instruction. Relative to the baseline, it more than triples the MIST reveal score. On the benchmarks, WRBench's score T rises by 26.1% and MBench-T's score U by 52.7%. The method meets the problem from the execution side: it shows what the frozen model can do once the move is stated, and it sets a stricter baseline.

LithoWorld: An Endpoint World Model for Lithography Process-Window Recovery

LithoWorld: An Endpoint World Model for Lithography Process-Window Recovery

Botong Zhao、Fang Yu、Xinyuan Chen、Yue Lu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Lithography process drift can move a fabricated pattern outside its process window, producing unqualified scanning electron microscopy (SEM) morphology. We present LithoWorld, an endpoint world model for SEM-guided parameter recovery. Interior representative targets jointly determine qualified reference appearance and correction supervision. A causal predictive branch aligns currentimage features with endpoint representations, while a conditional flow policy generates a two-dimensional correction as an action chunk. The model learns from 173 groups of measured SEMs organized as focus-exposure maps, preserving unmeasured cells as unknown. On 200 test pairs from the 73-group subset, LithoWorld achieves 88.5% qualified-region success and 46.0% interior success, exceeding the strongest comparison method by 2.5 and 6.0 percentage points, respectively. Ablations separate the effects of target selection, endpoint alignment, and output parameterization. Qualitative SEM retrieval illustrates recovery to qualified interiors without requiring exact reference-coordinate matching.

VISTA: A Multimodal Egocentric Benchmark for Goal-Oriented Perception and Assistance to Blind and Low-Vision Users

VISTA: A Multimodal Egocentric Benchmark for Goal-Oriented Perception and Assistance to Blind and Low-Vision Users

Noé Mosca、Mohamed Rayan Barhdadi、Muhammad Hamza、Joshua Yeh、Suryodaya Bikram Shahi 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Blindness and low vision limit many people's ability to complete daily tasks, motivating AI assistants that provide useful guidance. Yet, existing benchmarks do not adequately test whether vision models can provide effective everyday assistance. In particular, i) existing benchmarks primarily rely on visual and textual inputs, with limited support for other modalities that are increasingly available in egocentric wearable devices such as gaze, inertial measurements, spatial trajectories, and others; and ii) existing evaluations emphasize scene understanding and question answering, with limited coverage of goal-conditioned guidance for blind and low-vision users across everyday tasks. To bridge this gap, we introduce VISTA (Visually Impaired Scene and Task Annotations), a multimodal egocentric benchmark for goal-oriented assistance for blind and low-vision users. VISTA contains $\sim$1,000 egocentric recordings captured with Project Aria glasses; each recording provides synchronized sensor streams, six of which serve as model inputs, and three different types of annotation, covering ten task categories (TC), from object localization to hazard detection. We evaluate 14 representative vision-language models zero-shot on VISTA and design a lightweight multimodal model baseline that integrates all six modalities. Experimental results show that, although existing models can often describe egocentric scenes, they remain limited in generating goal-directed assistive guidance. VISTA thus provides a benchmark for evaluating multimodal models that could improve everyday accessibility.

Input- and Feature-Space Correction for Cross-Hospital IHC Grading

Input- and Feature-Space Correction for Cross-Hospital IHC Grading

Jongwoon Lee、Jhair Gallardo、Christopher Kanan

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Pathology foundation models (FMs) are trained predominantly on hematoxylin and eosin (H&E) images but are increasingly used as frozen feature extractors for immunohistochemistry (IHC), where cross-hospital differences can impair grading. However, systematic comparisons of correction methods for cross-hospital IHC grading remain limited. To address this gap, we benchmark label-free transformations applied to input images before frozen-FM encoding and to features after frozen-FM encoding using UNI and Virchow2, three IHC markers, and six directed cross-hospital transfers, yielding 12 settings. Among the evaluated input-space methods, our proposed method FIX achieved the largest macro-F1 improvement over no correction, outperforming well-known stain normalization techniques. Among feature-space methods, a simple feature-wise standardization achieved the best improvement over no correction, outperforming CORAL. Under patient-averaged tile-level evaluation, combining FIX with feature-wise standardization improved macro-F1 and quadratic weighted kappa (QWK) by 0.181 and 0.208, respectively, and reduced ordinal error by 0.209 relative to no correction. These results show that input- and feature-space transformations can provide complementary benefits for cross-hospital IHC grading with frozen pathology FMs.

2026-10-16

A Language-Informed Loss for Visual Generation

A Language-Informed Loss for Visual Generation

Suchir Agarwal、Keshigeyan Chandrasegaran、Ziyu Chen、Kyle Sargent、Juan Carlos Niebles 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

End-to-end visual generation with pixel-space loss has recently achieved competitive performance without the need for learned image tokenizers. In this work, we study whether language-informed supervision can provide a richer training signal than pixel loss for visual generation. We introduce LILIE (pronounced "lily"), a Language-Informed and Lossless Image Encoder. LILIE is an invertible image transform trained such that a vision-language model can recover captions from noised features. The encoder uses a normalizing flow transformer trained jointly with a vision-language model via next token prediction. We then use the encoder to extract a simple language-informed loss for text-to-image generation. We train JiT models with LILIE loss, reducing gFD by 14 points (66.84 $\to$ 53.01) against a pixel-only baseline on the GPIC dataset. Our code, encoders, and generative models are available at https://suchiragarwal.com/lilie.

FlashControl: One-Step Controllable Generator via Distillation-Friendly Single-Stream Teachers

FlashControl: One-Step Controllable Generator via Distillation-Friendly Single-Stream Teachers

Ngan Nguyen、Dung Nguyen、Quan Dao、Dimitris N. Metaxas、Minh Hoai 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Diffusion models have become a leading approach in visual generative modeling, particularly effective in text-to-image and controllable generation. Their main drawback is slow inference as it requires many sampling steps. While recent distillation methods enable one-step text-to-image generation, extending these techniques to controllable generation is still challenging. Existing controllable models, such as ControlNet, rely on dual-branch design that make distillation difficult. In this work, we adopt a simpler strategy. Instead of adding extra conditional branches, we finetune a selected subset of layers in the original text-to-image diffusion model to directly enable controllable generation. This results in DeltaControl, a single-flow, multi-step diffusion model capable of supporting spatial-condition generation while matching the performance of dual-branch approaches. Building on this teacher model, we further apply step-distillation to obtain FlashControl, a one-step model capable of spatial controllable generation with a single forward pass.

Learning the Shared Latent Structure of Cryo-EM Inverse Problems

Learning the Shared Latent Structure of Cryo-EM Inverse Problems

Nithin Chalapathi、Theo Sternlieb、Ethan Han、Sonya M. Hanson、Pilar Cossio 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Understanding biomolecular function requires characterizing three-dimensional structure and conformational variability, for which cryogenic electron microscopy (cryo-EM) is a powerful tool. Cryo-EM produces noisy two-dimensional particle images in which molecular structure, particle pose, and imaging conditions are jointly encoded. Recovering biological information from these observations spans inverse problems including pose estimation, heterogeneity analysis, and reconstruction. These tasks are usually addressed independently despite shared protein structure and image formation, motivating a common representation across inverse problems. Since biologically meaningful differences may be spatially localized, we study self-distillation with complementary global and masked patch objectives. Because experimental dataset discrimination can reflect acquisition-specific cues, we develop controlled synthetic and experimental evaluations and find that structural discrimination benefits from dense spatial features. We introduce the EMber family of vision transformers, pre-trained on >50 million experimental particle images from 214 EMPIAR datasets together with simulated projections from 268,000 molecular assemblies. EMber improves upon existing methods at conformational and compositional discrimination by 14.85 and 14.26 percentage points, estimates conformational populations to within 1.59pp error with a linear probe, and produces particle assignments yielding reference-quality reconstructions in fewer epochs. We demonstrate how to learn representations that capture biological information for use across multiple cryo-EM inverse problems.

Understanding biomolecular function requires characterizing three-dimensional structure and conformational variability, for which cryogenic electron microscopy (cryo-EM) is a powerful tool. Cryo-EM produces noisy two-dimensional particle images in which molecular structure, particle pose, and imaging conditions are jointly encoded. Recovering biological information from these observations spans inverse problems including pose estimation, heterogeneity analysis, and reconstruction. These tasks are usually addressed independently despite shared protein structure and image formation, motivating a common representation across inverse problems. Since biologically meaningful differences may be spatially localized, we study self-distillation with complementary global and masked patch objectives. Because experimental dataset discrimination can reflect acquisition-specific cues, we develop controlled synthetic and experimental evaluations and find that structural discrimination benefits from dense spatial features. We introduce the EMber family of vision transformers, pre-trained on >50 million experimental particle images from 214 EMPIAR datasets together with simulated projections from 268,000 molecular assemblies. EMber improves upon existing methods at conformational and compositional discrimination by 14.85 and 14.26 percentage points, estimates conformational populations to within 1.59pp error with a linear probe, and produces particle assignments yielding reference-quality reconstructions in fewer epochs. We demonstrate how to learn representations that capture biological information for use across multiple cryo-EM inverse problems.

2026-10-16

SpikeQ: Spike-Guided Post-Training Quantization for Spiking Neural Networks

SpikeQ: Spike-Guided Post-Training Quantization for Spiking Neural Networks

Kai Sun、Peibo Duan、Zhanglu Yan、Guowei Zhang、Yongsheng Huang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

On neuromorphic chips, spiking neural networks (SNNs) consume far less energy than artificial neural networks (ANNs), but the limited memory of these chips requires low-bit synaptic weights and membrane potentials. Existing post-training quantization (PTQ) methods provide them without retraining and, like those for ANNs, minimize numerical errors such as the mean squared error (MSE). In an SNN, however, a quantization error reaches later layers only through the spikes it flips. We find that errors of equal MSE can cause very different accuracy losses, whereas errors that flip the same fraction of spikes cause the same loss. We therefore propose SpikeQ, a spike-guided PTQ framework. For weights, we select the level of each weight by simulating the downstream neurons and minimizing their spike errors. For membrane potentials, most existing methods quantize the state after the reset, whereas neuromorphic chips compare the threshold with the membrane potential stored at a fixed bit width. We target this pre-fire quantization and build a reset-aware grid that places the threshold on a rounding boundary, so rounding never flips the spike at the current time step. We evaluate SpikeQ on seven benchmarks of image classification, semantic segmentation, and object detection. With 2-bit pre-fire membrane potentials, every existing grid collapses, whereas SpikeQ retains most of the full-precision accuracy. With both at 4 bits, the models stay within 2.59 points of full precision, shrink their memory footprint 7.8--7.9 times, and use 6.4--6.9 times less energy.

Sink-Sparse Hybrid Attention for Efficient Inference in T2I/T2V Diffusion Transformers

Sink-Sparse Hybrid Attention for Efficient Inference in T2I/T2V Diffusion Transformers

Bingxin Xing、Yu Zhang、Jiatong Ding、Lei Lu、Ping Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) have become a common backbone for high-quality text-to-image and text-to-video generation, but repeated dense self-attention remains a major inference bottleneck as token sequences grow and the same operator is invoked across many denoising steps. Our study suggests that dense attention in pretrained DiTs contains reusable layer/head-specific high-mass structure that can be identified from a small offline calibration set and reused across future generations. This leads to Sink-Sparse Hybrid Attention, a train-free attention replacement that reuses static sink masks and supplements them with a lightweight pooled-KV branch, while leaving model weights and sampling schedules unchanged. On Wan2.1-T2V-1.3B, the method attains 1.54$\times$ end-to-end speedup while maintaining generation quality. On Z-Image-Turbo, it attains 1.57$\times$ end-to-end speedup with competitive GenEval2 performance. These results indicate that reusable importance-aware sparse support can provide an effective train-free acceleration path for both T2V and T2I DiTs.

These results indicate that reusable importance-aware sparse support can provide an effective train-free acceleration path for both T2V and T2I DiTs.

ODSS-Net: Orthogonally Decoupled Shared-Specific Representations for Multi-Modal Object Re-Identification

ODSS-Net: Orthogonally Decoupled Shared-Specific Representations for Multi-Modal Object Re-Identification

Zhifeng Ma、Yuhong Feng、Chongyi Huang、Mingyang Zhou、Minwen Deng 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multi-modal object re-identification (ReID) leverages complementary information from heterogeneous sensors. Cross-modal alignment and fusion remain challenging. Existing methods mainly reduce modality gaps via feature alignment and fusion. However, alignment may entangle modality-shared structural information with modality-specific cues, while conventional fusion may not fully exploit complementary information. To address these issues, we propose ODSS-Net, a framework for orthogonally decoupled shared-specific representation learning and adaptive fusion of complementary modality-specific features. First, a Shared Structural Feature Construction (SSFC) module suppresses modality-dependent appearance variations and extracts modality-shared structural information. Then, an Orthogonal Specific Feature Learning (OSFL) module aggregates identity-relevant local information and removes the shared-aligned component via geometric orthogonal projection. Finally, a Sample Adaptive Mixture of Experts (SAMoE) module uses the shared structure as routing context to adaptively refine and fuse modality-specific features. Experiments on three benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of ODSS-Net for multi-modal object ReID.

Experiments on three benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of ODSS-Net for multi-modal object ReID.

MaskMerge: Structure-aware Token Merging for Efficient Instance Segmentation

MaskMerge: Structure-aware Token Merging for Efficient Instance Segmentation

Bavesh Balaji、Letian Jiang、Alexander Wong、David Clausi、Sirisha Rambhatla 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Token merging improves the efficiency of Vision Transformers by combining redundant tokens, but existing methods largely rely on feature similarity to determine which tokens to merge. This criterion is effective for semantic segmentation, yet can fail in instance segmentation, where adjacent objects of the same class often have highly similar features. We identify this failure mode as cross-instance token fusion. We introduce MaskMerge, an instance-aware token merging method that uses coarse intermediate mask predictions to constrain feature-based merging and preserve instance boundaries. We evaluate MaskMerge on two diverse instance segmentation domains characterized by high inter-instance visual similarity: PanNuke and iSAID. Against the SOTA token merging method ALGM, MaskMerge improves mAP from 30.1 to 32.1 on PanNuke while reducing computation from 229 to 153 GFLOPs and running 1.18x faster, and from 33.1 to 36.2 on iSAID while reducing computation from 198 to 182 GFLOPs and running 1.23x faster. Additionally, we also test it on Cityscapes and match ALGM's performance while using fewer FLOPs. The gains also transfer to an MAE pretrained backbone, demonstrating that instance-aware merging enables more aggressive compression when appearance similarity alone cannot reliably preserve object identity.

Variational and Decomposed method for Mitigating Bimodal Bias in Robust Visual Question Answering

Variational and Decomposed method for Mitigating Bimodal Bias in Robust Visual Question Answering

Yihan Zhao、Yuncheng Jiang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Visual question answering (VQA) models are known to suffer from the bias issue, which refers to overfitting the regularities of frequent patterns. Previously, researchers only consider the language bias. Recently, the multimodal bias is noticed. However, what multimodal bias is remains unclear. In this paper, we investigate the multimodal bias in counting and non-counting questions. We find that they have distinct underlying mechanism, so their biases come from distinct reasons. We provide valuable interpretations of VQA counting mechanism: \textbf{quantity information stems from the magnitude of the multimodal features in specific dimensions.} Thus, counting bias stems from the vision side and propagates to multimodal features through modality fusion, while the non-counting bias stems from primarily language bias and slightly vision bias. Thus, we propose a method to deal with them differently: Variational and Decomposed method for mitigating Bimodal Bias (VDB). It extracts bimodal bias affected by different proportion of vision and language sides. We introduce variational bias models utilizing the principle of Variational Auto-Encoder to learn mainly language bias with slight vision effect, and a bimodal bias model for bimodal bias combining language and vision sides. Extensive experiments on VQACPv2, VQACPv1, and GQAOOD datasets demonstrate the rationality of our claim and the effectiveness of our method. We also provide explanation for the compromised performance on VQAv2.

DefFlow: Goal-Conditioned Deformable Object Manipulation using 3D Point Flows

DefFlow: Goal-Conditioned Deformable Object Manipulation using 3D Point Flows

Gaurav Singh、Sergio Orozco、Ryan Duong、Mete Tuluhan Akbulut、George Konidaris 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Manipulating deformable objects toward desired 3D configurations is a fundamental capability for robots operating in unstructured physical environments. However, as the range of possible initial and target configurations grows, goal-conditioned deformable manipulation requires a robot to infer increasingly diverse manipulation behaviors in addition to precise continuous actions. We introduce DefFlow, a framework for goal-conditioned manipulation of deformable objects toward a broad range of 3D configurations. DefFlow learns from demonstrations by jointly predicting the goal-conditioned 3D point flow of the robot and deformable object. We show that this formulation better captures both the manipulation decisions, such as when and where to grasp, release, or re-grasp, and the precise continuous motions required for goal reaching, rather than predicting only the robot flow. Across rope, granular-media, and cloth manipulation settings with diverse initial and target configurations, DefFlow substantially outperforms goal-conditioned action-prediction and robot-flow-only baselines on success rates and goal-distance metrics. We further demonstrate deployment of our framework on a real robot using real point-cloud observations and unseen goal configurations.

PASS-VLN: Passability-Aware Local-Command Vision-and-Language Navigation

PASS-VLN: Passability-Aware Local-Command Vision-and-Language Navigation

Cong Zou、Xiangsheng Shi、Xuanhui Wang、Fubin Wang、Guangjun Ge 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Reliable vision-and-language navigation (VLN) requires not only understanding instructions but also determining whether and how a robot can physically execute them. However, existing VLN benchmarks typically assume executable instructions and focus on long-horizon tasks. This leaves body-dependent local execution, including passability and collision-free execution, largely unexamined. Evaluating these factors within long-horizon tasks would entangle local physical execution with task planning and long-term spatial memory, making navigation failures difficult to attribute. We therefore focus on grounding local navigation commands in executable motion under environmental and robot body constraints. We present PASS-VLN, a passability-aware local-command VLN benchmark and model. The benchmark holds the scene, instruction, start pose, and reference route fixed while varying robot body width, requiring target completion when the instructed passage is feasible and stopping before blockage otherwise. Our model encodes body width through obstacle inflation in a depth-derived local bird's-eye view, fuses geometry with visual and linguistic context, and predicts discrete latent action codes for continuous closed-loop execution. Experiments show that PASS-VLN improves collision-free success by approximately 12 percentage points over a strong VLN model fine-tuned on our benchmark, while adapting reach-versus-stop behavior to body constraints and generalizing to unseen body widths. When integrated with a high-level planner, PASS-VLN achieves higher success with fewer local commands, while real-world deployment further demonstrates its effectiveness as a low-level executor. The benchmark and model will be released.

The benchmark and model will be released.

Replay Pruning and Few-Bit Integer Inference for Hardware-Efficient Speed-Sign Recognition

Replay Pruning and Few-Bit Integer Inference for Hardware-Efficient Speed-Sign Recognition

Se Un Park、Kyungkeon Chung、Huigeol Kim、Junyoung Park

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Speed-limit sign recognition on an automotive camera system-on-chip (SoC) must fit a detector and a classifier (the reader) into the memory, arithmetic, and energy budgets of the device. In our two-stage recognizer, the detector, a YOLO-family network with ReLU and hard-sigmoid operators, is compressed by channel replay: an iterative pruning cascade identifies the channels to remove, the recorded deletions are replayed on the original task-trained network, the parent, and one continuous knowledge-distillation recovery follows. On a YOLOv5n, replay removes 60.3% of the parameters at the accuracy of the parent and, in validation mAP50-95 over five matched seeds, outperforms iterative pruning and training from scratch, and its per-step variant (per-step replay) is the most accurate pruning method. On a YOLOv8n, replay and per-step replay outperform every other method and the unpruned parent. The reader, a five-layer CNN with 18k parameters, and the post-processing of the YOLOv5n and YOLOv8n heads use integer arithmetic only. With 4-bit weights and 8-bit activations (W4A8), the reader matches FP32 accuracy in 12.1 KB of on-chip memory, and an executable streaming model reproduces the INT8 class predictions of its runtime on all 9,850 test crops. On a Jetson AGX Orin, INT8 is faster than FP16 only in the implicit formulation of TensorRT, and the replay detector runs fastest on the deep-learning accelerator (DLA). Trained on public data only, the combined system of 0.72 M parameters recognizes 95.2% of the signs of a held-out automotive camera at 90% precision and 94 frames per second.
