Kingfisher: Fast Personalized Visual Autoregressive Generation via Scale-Aware Prompt-Conditioned Scale Decomposition

Kingfisher: Fast Personalized Visual Autoregressive Generation via Scale-Aware Prompt-Conditioned Scale Decomposition

Dinh-Khoi Vo、Akihiro Sugimoto、Minh-Triet Tran、Trung-Nghia Le

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Personalized image generation has been extensively studied in diffusion models, often relying on subject-specific optimization of model weights, textual embeddings, LoRA modules, or adapters. In this work, we explore a different direction for fast personalization in visual autoregressive models. Our key observation is that a short prefix of early autoregressive scale codes is sufficient to strongly determine both object structure and global appearance, while later scales primarily refine the established generation trajectory. Based on this insight, we introduce Kingfisher, a model-tuning-free framework that decomposes early residual scale codes into content- and style-related components through prompt-conditioned scale-code subspaces. Given two real images, Kingfisher first decomposes their early scale codes, then recomposes the content components of one image with the style components of the other into a compact semantic prefix. The frozen generator subsequently predicts the remaining scales through bitwise self-correction for coherent refinement. Experiments on CSD-100 show that Kingfisher achieves strong content preservation and the highest style and text alignment among the evaluated methods, while reducing pair-specific preparation time by approximately 54x compared with diffusion-based methods and 7.5x compared with VAR-based alternatives. These results demonstrate that effective personalization can be achieved by directly manipulating the native scale-space representation of a frozen visual autoregressive generator.

X-Splat: Unified Gaussian Splatting for Joint RGB and X-ray 3D Reconstruction

X-Splat: Unified Gaussian Splatting for Joint RGB and X-ray 3D Reconstruction

Wangze Xu、Chu Zhou、Mae Yamaguchi、Linrui Dai、I-Chao Shen 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Jointly reconstructing RGB and X-ray provides new insights by connecting external appearance with internal structure, enabling a more complete understanding of real-world objects than single modality. Yet existing methods train separate models per modality and suffer from redundant geometry, alignment artifacts, and loose coupling that limits cross-modal insight discovery. We propose a unified representation in which all Gaussian primitives share a single set of geometric parameters, with only surface-proximal primitives additionally carrying appearance attributes. We perform cross-modal registration through clean multi-view stereo and X-ray geometric proxies instead of aligning two sets of optimized Gaussians, improving alignment stability. We then jointly optimize the unified representation via supervision from both modalities, complemented by a surface-depth consistency loss derived from X-ray geometry to guide RGB reconstruction. Experiments on the InsideOut dataset and our Flower & Insect dataset show that our framework achieves more robust cross-modal alignment, higher RGB rendering quality without compromising X-ray reconstruction, and a more compact representation than maintaining two independent geometries.

Scene-Designer: Learning Design Intelligence for Agentic 3D Scene Generation

Scene-Designer: Learning Design Intelligence for Agentic 3D Scene Generation

Xuanyu Liu、Hongyan Feng、Miao Pan、Sunlai Chen、Lizhou Liang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Generating complete, coherent, and usable 3D indoor scenes from natural language requires strong \textbf{design intelligence}: the ability to design high-quality scenes and critique their quality. However, existing approaches either rely on general-purpose models lacking domain-specific design experience or learn from static scenes. In this work, we introduce \textbf{Scene-Designer}, an agentic framework that learns design intelligence through complementary design and critique experiences. We construct two datasets: \textbf{Scene-OTA-35K}, which provides Observation--Thought--Action supervision for learning scene design and efficient error correction, and \textbf{Scene-OTC-15K}, which provides Observation--Thought--Critic supervision for learning scene quality critique across physical validity, functional correctness, and layout coherence. Based on these experiences, we propose \textbf{Scene-GRPO}, a Critic-guided GRPO framework that improves design decisions and reduces unnecessary trial-and-error corrections. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Scene-Designer achieves superior performance in physical validity, functional correctness, and layout coherence, while the learned Scene-Critic significantly outperforms general-purpose models in scene critique. Moreover, test-time Critic guidance further improves scene generation, showing that the Critic can provide useful guidance beyond training. Additional analysis suggests a trend of mutual enhancement between scene design and critique abilities.

Domain-specific language alignment of multimodal embeddings towards explainable predictions

Domain-specific language alignment of multimodal embeddings towards explainable predictions

Gabrielė Tijūnaitytė、Thijs L. van der Plas、Mohamed-Bachir Belaid、Rob Knapen、Maciej J. Soja 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multimodal data are commonly fused into latent representations that improve downstream task performance but are not interpretable, limiting their application in domains that require explainable model decisions. Vision-language alignment has made image embeddings interpretable through language, but requires vast image-caption training data. However, captioned data are typically not available for multimodal embeddings, as relevant features cannot directly be observed and annotated from embeddings alone. Here, we propose a framework to language-align any embedding space that can be linked to interpretable auxiliary data. Captioning functions convert auxiliary data into partial captions; a soft contrastive loss weights pairs by the similarity of their properties that each caption describes; and a rank-based concept retrieval task evaluates alignment against the auxiliary data rather than the captions. We apply this framework to multimodal Earth embeddings to facilitate explainable predictions for two species distribution modelling tasks: S2BMS and SatBird. AlphaEarth embeddings beat previous state-of-the-art models on 6/7 metrics, while our framework language-aligns domain-specific concepts of the embedding space, outperforming previous Earth observation vision-language models on average concept retrieval for both datasets. Finally, aligned concepts recover species-habitat associations ($ρ$=0.44) and relate habitat descriptions to species predictions, showing that abstract embeddings can be interrogated in the language of domain experts.

Agentic Image Generation in the Wild

Agentic Image Generation in the Wild

Yifan Li、Bowen Qin、Kun Zhou、Haochen Zhao、Zikang Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Agentic image generation pairs an image generator with a search agent that retrieves references from the web, extending generation beyond the generator's training data. Real-world requests, however, often concern entities that appeared only days ago. We call this setting Image Generation in the Wild. In this setting, the web offers limited visual evidence, search results are crowded with easily confused entities, and the target is rarely shown on its own. To study this setting, we build an automated pipeline that synthesizes prompts from recent headlines and keeps only those whose real search results are highly confusing, yielding requests that are hard to solve yet natural to ask. With it, we construct Savanna, a benchmark of 500 prompts across 16 subcategories. On Savanna, even harness frameworks built on the frontier models retrieve a correct reference for only about half of the prompts. We then propose Locate-Corroborate-Isolate, a paradigm in which the agent first pins down the target's identity through text search, then verifies candidate images against multiple sources, and finally crops a clean reference for the generator. Trained on 15K trajectories that follow this paradigm, Ranger-8B achieves a search score of 67.2, outperforming harnesses equipped with frontier models. It also achieves the best generation quality with every generator we test, and surpasses prior agentic methods on other image generation benchmarks. We release the pipeline, benchmark, training data and model at https://github.com/RUCAIBox/Agentic-ImageGen-Wild.

Learning to Correct Geometry in Generated Videos

Learning to Correct Geometry in Generated Videos

Weijie Lyu、Tiancheng Shen、Jason Li、Yujing Wang、Xiaoyuan Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Generated videos often lose geometric consistency under camera motion: structures warp under viewpoint change, parallax becomes inconsistent, and smooth camera movement may collapse into shot-like transitions. We argue that this failure is largely a data problem. In web-scale video corpora, most visible motion comes from dynamic objects captured by static or weakly moving cameras, leaving only sparse supervision for viewpoint-induced appearance changes. To address this, we propose Video Geometry Corrector (VGC), a post-hoc video-to-video model trained on synthetic paired supervision. Starting from real videos with diverse camera motion and pose annotations, we construct VG-Pairs with a synthetic paired-supervision data generation pipeline. Each video clip is treated as a geometry-consistent target and paired with a distorted counterpart synthesized by a pretrained image-to-video inpainting model using trajectory-adaptive keyframes. VGC learns to map the distorted input to the geometry-consistent target without modifying the source generator, and uses optical flow as an auxiliary cue to help disentangle camera motion from object motion. Experiments on pose-conditioned and prompt-driven benchmarks show that VGC improves geometric consistency across multiple source generators, and scaling to a larger backbone further extends the framework toward more general video refinement.

Rethinking the design space of Vanilla Pixel Diffusion Transformers

Rethinking the design space of Vanilla Pixel Diffusion Transformers

Soumik Mukhopadhyay、Long Mai、Abhinav Shrivastava、Aniruddha Mahapatra

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Pixel-space diffusion models have recently re-emerged as a compelling alternative to latent diffusion, avoiding the lossy latent bottleneck and separately trained autoencoder. JiT shows that a vanilla diffusion transformer can generate directly in pixel space by replacing the commonly used velocity prediction ($\boldsymbol{u}$-prediction) with clean image prediction ($\boldsymbol{x_0}$-prediction). In this work, we revisit this choice and introduce MFlow, an adaptive parameterization that learns to interpolate between $\boldsymbol{x_0}$- and $\boldsymbol{u}$-prediction across principal directions of the data, learned end-to-end with diffusion training. We find that the preferred parameterization varies systematically with the data representation and model capacity -- notably, increasing model capacity shifts preference toward velocity prediction. We evaluate MFlow across ImageNet, UCF-101 video, and large-scale text-to-image generation, demonstrating competitive or improved generative performance across diverse settings. In addition, we find that vanilla pixel diffusion transformers, irrespective of parameterization, retain a persistent high-frequency deficit relative to real images. To address this, we introduce ConvRefiner, a lightweight convolutional module trained post-hoc with an adversarial loss while keeping the diffusion backbone frozen. With only a small amount of additional training, ConvRefiner restores the high-frequency statistics of generated images while preserving FID. Project webpage: https://soumik-kanad.github.io/mflow-diffusion/.

The Collection of a Real-World, Large-Scale Dataset for Automated Skill Evaluation in Central Line Dressing Change

The Collection of a Real-World, Large-Scale Dataset for Automated Skill Evaluation in Central Line Dressing Change

Ummey Tanin、Shannan K. Hamlin、Hsin-Mei Chen、 Peizhu、 Qian 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Central line-associated bloodstream infections (CLABSIs) remain a major source of preventable morbidity and mortality in healthcare settings. Proper execution of the central line dressing change (CLDC) procedure is essential for infection prevention, yet current approaches to competency assessment rely primarily on manual ob- servation by clinical educators, making evaluation time-intensive, difficult to scale, and limited in consistency and granularity. These challenges highlight the need for AI-assisted skill evaluation methods that can support more objective, reproducible, and scalable assessment of clinical performance. However, progress in this area has been constrained by the lack of broad-scale, clinically grounded datasets for developing and evaluating such methods. In this work, we present the collection process of a large-scale video dataset for CLDC skill evaluation, comprising 365 recordings of registered nurses collected during the 2026 annual competency exam at Houston Methodist Hospital. Each video is paired with subtask-level temporal annotations and clinical performance assessments spanning 17 subtasks of the procedure. We describe the data collection and curation pipeline, including pilot testing of record- ing configurations, annotation protocol design, and post-collection processing. We further establish the feasibility of video-based skill assessment through benchmark experiments with spatiotemporal deep learning models. This dataset provides a foundation for future research on automated clinical skill assessment, feedback generation, and AI-assisted training in nursing education.

2026-10-16

An Efficient Approach to Beetle Damage Detection of Banana Using Deep Learning

An Efficient Approach to Beetle Damage Detection of Banana Using Deep Learning

Md. Mehedi Hasan Naeem、Ishtiyak Ahmmad Araf、Md. Arefin Haque Mahir、Tushar Kanti Saha

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Visible surface damage of banana fruits by Basilepta subcostata (the banana fruit scarring beetle) can reduce their market value. Here, manual inspection of Banana fruits is subjective and difficult to scale. This study presents a lightweight framework based on YOLOv8n deep learning model for locating individual bananas and classifying their visible surface condition as healthy or unhealthy. The term beetle damage in the paper title is used operationally for the locally observed scar-like surface condition; the model does not identify a specific beetle species or biological cause. A custom dataset of 2,473 images containing 5,827 annotated banana instances was prepared from local-market imagery in Bangladesh. The reported experiment used an 80/20 protocol with 1,982 training images and 491 test images. A pretrained YOLOv8n model was trained at 640x640 resolution for 50 epochs with a batch size of 4. On the test partition, it achieved 81.36% precision, 74.30% recall, 77.67% F1-score, 87.04% mAP@0.50 and 59.33% mAP@0.50:0.95. A YOLOv8s comparison gave slightly lower F1 and mAP while using a substantially larger checkpoint. The YOLOv8n checkpoint occupied 5.96 MB and required 6.54 ms/image for inference on an NVIDIA Tesla T4. The results support YOLOv8n as a compact baseline for instance-level banana surface inspection within the studied acquisition conditions.

FlexPart: Text-Controlled Semantics-Aware 3D Part Segmentation at Flexible Granularity

FlexPart: Text-Controlled Semantics-Aware 3D Part Segmentation at Flexible Granularity

Yiqun Zhao、Jiachen Xu、Yihao Zhi、Yunfei Zhao、Zibo Zhao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

A 3D asset admits multiple valid part decompositions, while semantics-aware segmentation should reflect both the desired granularity and the parts a user wants to extract. Ideally, a practical segmentation model should strive for a simple principle: what you ask for is what you get. However, realizing this goal is hindered by two core obstacles: the lack of multi-granularity training data and the absence of a holistic control mechanism. We propose FLEXPART, a unified framework for text-controlled 3D mesh segmentation. To establish flexible control, we introduce a holistic control prompt comprising semantic part names with location and shape clauses. To support this mechanism, we utilize an automated Vision-Language Model (VLM) pipeline to construct a large-scale dataset of multi-granularity partitions and their corresponding prompts. Driven by this data, FLEXPART generates a view-independent part-identity field in native 3D space, directly assigning discrete part IDs to mesh faces. To compel the model to respect semantic conditions rather than rely on geometric priors, we train the model using randomized background dropping. Furthermore, an inference-time VLM planner can automatically complete descriptive clauses from 2D renderings, allowing users to simply name the desired parts in ordinary words. FLEXPART with planner-completed prompts outperforms the strongest evaluated baseline by 15% in mIoU, even when that baseline receives ground-truth guidance. On our held-out FLEXPARTBENCH, FLEXPART leads at each granularity and improves average mIoU by 22% over the strongest baseline. With only the requested part names supplied by the user, it also improves IoU by 35% over the strongest click-driven baseline on selective segmentation.

2026-10-16

Stereo LWIR-RGB Automatic Labeling for Low-Light Camera-Based VRU Detection

Stereo LWIR-RGB Automatic Labeling for Low-Light Camera-Based VRU Detection

Dimitrios Bouzoulas、Pyry Jäppinen、Pejman Habibiroudkenar、Eerik Alamikkotervo、Risto Ojala 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

VRU detection in low-light conditions is a key component in ensuring VRU safety during the most collision prone time of the day. Currently, advances in low-light detection are limited due to the lack of large scale datasets and automated labeling methods. Most autolabeling methods use RGB detections as their labels source, which cannot be replicated in low-light conditions. To fill this gap in this paper we present an automated pipeline for low-light VRU labeling. We use LWIR-RGB image pairs and source our labels from LWIR as VRUs differentiate from the background due to their body temperature. Unlike our previous work that relied on a complex hardware setup to align the two modalities, we perform this experiment using a LWIR-RGB stereo setup, lowering hardware complexity and cost, making the pipeline easier to reproduce. We evaluate method end-to-end on a dataset collected in the capital region of Finland. Models trained on our generated labels beat both off-the-shelf baselines and models fine tuned on a hand labeled subset on F1, mAP@50 and LAMR metrics when tested on a manually labeled dataset, and the result holds for two different LWIR detectors. This shows that low-light VRU autolabeling can be made both accessible and effective without specialized alignment hardware.

Shift-Reliance Alignment: Ranking Vision-Language Models by the Cues They Trust

Shift-Reliance Alignment: Ranking Vision-Language Models by the Cues They Trust

Faisal Mohammad、Mohamed Younis

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-language models (VLMs) are usually chosen from leaderboards, which give one ranking for every application. On questions about objects that a scene suggests but does not contain, however, the model with the lowest error on the POPE hallucination benchmark is not the one with the lowest error on such questions. For yes/no questions about object presence, we show that the best model for a target domain can be predicted without running any candidate on that domain, given 650 to 1,200 labeled target items that calibrate a detector. We measure each model's reliance on a cue, a feature such as scene context or text in the image, by editing 100 labeled source images so that the cue suggests the wrong answer, and comparing with a matched control edit. Reliance predicts which VLMs are misled by physical sticker attacks (Spearman .93 on nine models not used to design the score) and which hallucinate on unedited COCO photographs, beyond what their tendency to answer yes explains. Shift-Reliance Alignment (SRA) scores a model by its source error on the labeled source questions plus its reliance multiplied by the shift. The shift is the proportion of target questions on which the cue misleads, and a detector estimates it on the unlabeled target images. SRA selects the model with the lowest score. In pre-registered tests on new images where most questions mislead, it lowered the error of the selected model from 30.4% under selection by source error to 18.8% on Objects365 and OpenImages but, against its prediction, not on COCO. On CueDial, a benchmark we release that varies how often a cue misleads, selection by model confidence falls more than 40 percentage points behind the best model when overlaid text denies a visible object, and SRA stays within 3 points.

2026-10-15

Really Need Recursion? Revisiting One-pass Registration with Multi-level Multi-space Contextual Guidance

Really Need Recursion? Revisiting One-pass Registration with Multi-level Multi-space Contextual Guidance

Yue Lin、Shenghai Wu、Mingyuan Meng

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Deformable image registration is a fundamental challenge in medical image analysis. Recent deep learning-based methods typically adopt either one-pass architectures that complete registration in a single network iteration, or recursive architectures that refine registration through multiple passes. While recursive methods often improve accuracy, they incur substantial computational overhead and slower inference speed. In this paper, we challenge the necessity of recursion for high-performance deformable registration and propose M2CG, a one-pass registration network with Multi-level Multi-space Contextual Guidance. It performs multi-step progressive pyramid registration within a single iteration, guided at each step by contextual cues from three levels (previous, current, and subsequent steps) and four spaces (image space, image feature space, segmentation space, and segmentation feature space). Our M2CG supports both unsupervised (without any labels) and semi-supervised (with segmentation labels for partial training data) registration, and has achieved state-of-the-art performance across two well-benchmarked inter-/intra-patient medical registration tasks with eight public datasets. Our findings suggest that the current reliance on recursion may stem from the absence of sufficient contextual cues that comprehensively characterize the remaining distance to the target alignment.

CRUX: Clinical-Text Anchoring for Cross-Institutional CT-Pathology Fusion in Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Survival Prediction

CRUX: Clinical-Text Anchoring for Cross-Institutional CT-Pathology Fusion in Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Survival Prediction

Kyungwon Kim、Dosik Hwang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Accurate prognosis in non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) benefits from integrating computed tomography (CT) and histopathological whole-slide images (WSIs), which capture complementary macroscopic and microscopic disease characteristics. Fusing these modalities is difficult: their features come from different pretrained foundation-model encoders and lack spatial correspondence, and both are sensitive to institution-specific acquisition and staining, causing multimodal models to overfit site-specific artifacts and generalize poorly. We present CRUX (CRoss-modal Unification via teXt anchoring), a fusion framework that uses clinical text---encoded by a biomedical language model---as an institution-invariant semantic anchor. Because clinical variables follow standardized classification systems, their embeddings occupy a representation space that is consistent across institutions, providing a stable reference frame for aligning heterogeneous imaging features. CRUX introduces a text-mediated cross-modal bridge in which CT- and pathology-derived text-query summaries exchange information within a compact text semantic space through a learnable hub token, avoiding direct image-space cross-attention that would propagate domain-specific artifacts. We evaluate on 2,157 NSCLC patients from five hospitals, holding out one hospital (N=446) entirely for external validation. CRUX attains an external concordance index (C-index) of 0.761, the highest among all unimodal, bimodal, and trimodal configurations. Notably, direct CT-WSI fusion degrades under distribution shift, whereas text anchoring restores and improves cross-institutional generalization. These results indicate that language-model-encoded clinical text is an effective structured prior for robust, generalizable multimodal cancer prognosis.

CRUX introduces a text-mediated cross-modal bridge in which CT- and pathology-derived text-query summaries exchange information within a compact text semantic space through a learnable hub token, avoiding direct image-space cross-attention that would propagate domain-specific artifacts. We evaluate on 2,157 NSCLC patients from five hospitals, holding out one hospital (N=446) entirely for external validation. CRUX attains an external concordance index (C-index) of 0.761, the highest among all unimodal, bimodal, and trimodal configurations. Notably, direct CT-WSI fusion degrades under distribution shift, whereas text anchoring restores and improves cross-institutional generalization. These results indicate that language-model-encoded clinical text is an effective structured prior for robust, generalizable multimodal cancer prognosis.

多模态/VLM医学图像
2026-10-16

Enhancing RAG-based Medical VQA with Gated Multimodal Representations

Enhancing RAG-based Medical VQA with Gated Multimodal Representations

Daniela Jimenez-Pano、Hugo Jair Escalante、Delia Irazú Hernández-Farías

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Medical Vision-Language Models (Med-LVLMs) have shown strong performance in Medical Visual Question Answering (MVQA), yet they may still produce factually inconsistent responses. Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) can mitigate this issue by incorporating external clinical evidence; however, retrieval is often guided by a single modality, potentially overlooking the joint semantics of medical images and clinical questions. We introduce a multimodal RAG framework that learns a joint query representation through a Gated Multimodal Unit, enabling evidence retrieval to leverage information from both modalities while keeping the underlying Med-LVLM frozen. We evaluate the proposed approach on benchmark datasets. Results show that the multimodal query representation consistently outperforms a reference Med-LVLM and remains competitive with state-of-the-art methods. Our findings demonstrate the potential of multimodal query representations for more effective evidence retrieval in Medical VQA.

2026-10-16

CLEAR: Cooperative Latent Flow for Visual Counterfactual Explanations with Accurate Region Preservation

CLEAR: Cooperative Latent Flow for Visual Counterfactual Explanations with Accurate Region Preservation

Yan Zeng、Changlu Guo、Oskar Kristoffersen、Anders Nymark Christensen、Morten Rieger Hannemose 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Visual counterfactual explanations (VCEs) should alter a classifier's prediction while remaining realistic and preserving decision-irrelevant content. Image-space methods provide high-quality counterfactuals but are computationally costly, whereas latent-space methods are more efficient but often less faithful. We identify two sources responsible for this gap. First, latent generation recovers a natural sample from a perturbed representation, whereas classifier guidance pushes it toward a target class, so their update directions may oppose. Second, VAE reconstruction can alter regions that should remain unchanged. We introduce CLEAR, a latent Flow Matching framework for VCEs. CLEAR uses Flow Matching's explicit instantaneous velocity as a reference for the generative direction to decompose classifier guidance into cooperative and conflicting components. It preserves the cooperative component and adaptively attenuates only the conflicting one according to conflict strength. We further analyze conflict frequency and strength throughout generation to reveal how classifier guidance interacts with the generative process. CLEAR also dynamically identifies editable regions, constrains latent modifications, and directly preserves factual content outside them to reduce VAE-induced changes. Across five bidirectional tasks on two datasets, CLEAR retains latent-space efficiency while achieving counterfactual quality comparable to or better than strong image-space baselines, with a 2.5x speedup over the strongest baseline. Project page: https://yan-zen9.github.io/CLEAR/

PanoBench: A Panoptic Occupancy Prediction Benchmark for Autonomous Driving

PanoBench: A Panoptic Occupancy Prediction Benchmark for Autonomous Driving

Yi Feng、Zizhan Guo、Junwu E、Yu Ma、Mengtan Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Panoptic occupancy unifies 3D geometry, semantics, and instance identities, but its development is limited by scarce instance-aware annotations. Existing benchmarks mainly provide semantic labels reconstructed from incomplete sensor observations, while panoptic datasets have limited domain coverage or annotation completeness. We introduce PanoBench, a camera-centric panoptic occupancy prediction benchmark spanning synthetic and real-world driving scenes. It combines PanoBench-CarlaOcc, PanoBench-nuScenes, and PanoBench-KITTI360 through canonical data interfaces and unified evaluation protocols, providing multi-resolution annotations with persistent instance identities. The synthetic branch derives complete panoptic geometry from simulated meshes. For real-world data, a new pipeline reconstructs backgrounds using geometric priors and topology constraints, generates foreground mesh assets from multi-frame multi-view images, and fuses them into sparse panoptic voxels. A unified valid-mask protocol evaluates complete thing instances supported by temporal observations while scoring stuff regions at the voxel level. Analyses of observation fidelity, geometric completeness, spatial continuity, and temporal consistency demonstrate the benefits of the resulting annotations over existing occupancy benchmarks. We benchmark representative methods on semantic occupancy prediction, panoptic occupancy prediction, and 4D occupancy forecasting, and study voxel resolution, synthetic-to-real transfer, and storage efficiency. These results reveal complementary strengths of volumetric and ray-based metrics and persistent challenges in real-world instance-aware prediction. PanoBench is available at https://mias.group/PanoBench.

PanoBench is available at https://mias.group/PanoBench.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM生成模型/扩散3D视觉
2026-10-16

ReVision: Reprogramming Image Generators for Dense Visual Perception

ReVision: Reprogramming Image Generators for Dense Visual Perception

Yitong Yang、Yinglin Wang、Xuexin Liu、Wei Chen、Zhaoyang Xu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Recent image generators exhibit rich visual priors that can support dense visual perception. However, existing approaches typically encode multiple perception tasks into a shared parameterization, making joint training sensitive to the mixture and weighting of heterogeneous data and prone to cross-task interference. To address this issue, we introduce ReVision, an instruction-conditioned parameter generation framework built on a frozen multimodal understanding model and a frozen image generator. Given an image and a free-form instruction, the understanding model jointly encodes them into a multimodal image--instruction representation, which a trainable parameter generator maps to transient low-rank updates for the generator. Each image--instruction pair induces a distinct set of transient prediction parameters, enabling instance-specific adaptation while both pretrained backbones remain unchanged. ReVision requires no task-specific heads, explicit task identifiers, or stored adapters. A shared RGB output interface unifies segmentation, depth estimation, and surface-normal prediction. Across diverse dense prediction benchmarks, ReVision achieves competitive performance with state-of-the-art methods while supporting flexible task specification through natural-language instructions. Our analysis further reveals substantial gradient disagreement across tasks, highlighting the conflict introduced when heterogeneous objectives share a single parameterization. Meanwhile, the generated updates exhibit structured task- and input-dependent organization in weight space, further validating instance-level parameter generation for multi-task perception.

2026-10-16

Momentum: Persistent Dynamics for Video World Models

Momentum: Persistent Dynamics for Video World Models

Nedko Savov、Stefan Maria Ailuro、Danda Pani Paudel、Yilun Du、Xi Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Video world models must capture how a scene evolves beyond the current camera view to generate consistent observations when its content reappears. Existing approaches rely on observation history, static point clouds, or image caches constructed from past frames. These representations are often expensive and, more importantly, cannot update dynamic scene content outside the field of view: generated vehicles and pedestrians may be forgotten after leaving the field of view or reappear in physically inaccurate states. We represent an evolving scene through synchronized videos from reference-camera rigs, each comprising eight cameras that jointly cover the surrounding scene. We connect these rigs in a graph and propagate video generation between neighboring rigs to coordinate scene dynamics across locations and time. We introduce Momentum, a two-stage pipeline: WorldUpdate jointly generates reference-camera videos as a shared memory of scene dynamics, and WorldInteract grounds camera-controlled video generation in this evolving memory, allowing moving content to remain represented outside the target view and reappear consistently. To support training and evaluation, we develop a CARLA pipeline that captures synchronized videos from reference camera rigs and a moving target camera, together with an evaluation framework measuring consistency within the generated world state and the target observations.Experiments on generated street scenes show that grounding target videos in scene dynamics generated by Momentum improves dynamic scene consistency by 126.2% in unseen scenes over static image conditioning, while also improving visual fidelity. Our models and source code will be made publicly available.

Can We Use Saliency Maps for the Evaluation of Generative Image Models?

Can We Use Saliency Maps for the Evaluation of Generative Image Models?

Eleonora Ristori、Luca Bindini、Marco Lippi、Paolo Frasconi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Evaluating generative image models remains challenging, as commonly used metrics depend on fixed pretrained feature extractors optimized for different tasks that do not always align with human realism perception across datasets and model families. To address this limitation, we investigate a human-inspired evaluation framework that avoids representations learned for unrelated tasks. Specifically, we train a generator-specific classifier to distinguish real images from samples produced by a given model and analyze its decisions using saliency maps. Across diverse benchmarks, we observe that these classifiers learn generator-specific artifacts rather than a universal notion of "fakeness", exhibiting strong accuracy and limited cross-generator transfer. This makes saliency analysis particularly informative: the resulting attribution maps highlight visual cues and structural irregularities characteristic of each model's outputs. By combining classification accuracy and saliency information, we obtain a composite score aligned with human judgments, without relying on large pretrained backbones and using only real and generated samples from the evaluated model.

FloodVLM-Pix: Language-Guided Pixel-Level Flood Understanding

FloodVLM-Pix: Language-Guided Pixel-Level Flood Understanding

Chengyang Zhang、Zhihao Wang、Zhiyu Long、Xueqian Wang、Quanming Yao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

As climate change intensifies, floods are becoming more frequent and destructive, making accurate flood understanding an urgent priority. However, existing vision-language models perform poorly on flood-related tasks, suffering from attentional bias and inaccurate descriptions of flood magnitude. To bridge this gap, we construct the first large-scale remote sensing flood multi-modal benchmark that supports language-level flood understanding and pixel-level flood segmentation. The benchmark contains 131,200 flood image patches and corresponding 443,100 textual descriptions for flood severity and evolution. Based on this benchmark, we propose FloodVLM-Pix, a language-guided pixel-level flood understanding model that includes a flood prior module and a multi-temporal flood pixel decoder to achieve accurate flood understanding. The flood prior module automatically distills sub-region flood spatial priors and inputs priors into FloodVLM-Pix, solving the attentional bias problem in flood language understanding. The flood pixel decoder introduces a novel general-specific feature fusion mechanism to address performance degradation caused by interference (e.g., clouds or mountain shadows) during flood segmentation. Experiments on FloodVL-Pix demonstrate that FloodVLM-Pix improves both flood description quality and pixel-level flood segmentation performance compared with state-of-the-art VLMs and flood segmentation models. Our code and data will be released at https://github.com/Haomiaoyanmeng/FloodPix.

Our code and data will be released at https://github.com/Haomiaoyanmeng/FloodPix.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM机器人视觉
2026-10-16

CoEvolve: Self-Evolving Code Policies from Skills to Strategies for Humanoid Robots

CoEvolve: Self-Evolving Code Policies from Skills to Strategies for Humanoid Robots

Zicheng Liu、Haochen Wu、Jiawei He、Suyun Zhao、Huan Yang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Recent advances in Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models and World Action Models (WAMs) have expanded the manipulation and whole body capabilities of individual robots. Yet a single robot remains limited by its embodiment, and many real world tasks require capabilities that can only emerge through cooperation. Existing multi robot frameworks largely emphasize task decomposition, allocation, and replanning, while providing limited support for adapting executable coordination when physical execution deviates from the intended behavior. We present CoEvolve, a framework that represents team coordination as executable code over fixed robot motor skills. CoEvolve introduces Verifier Guided Coordination Evolution, where a multimodal foundation model instantiates coordination strategies through motion primitives, semantic objectives, temporary robot roles, and bounded execution parameters. Physical verification evaluates each executed transition, rejected behaviors trigger structured coordination revision from an accepted checkpoint, and verified experience is retained to guide subsequent decisions. We evaluate CoEvolve with two simulated Unitree G1 humanoids transporting a freely supported rigid payload through constrained corridors. Across 15 base task runs with three multimodal backbone families, CoEvolve completes 8 tasks, with every backbone achieving end to end success. Additional evaluations yield verified placement on two additional corridor layouts and after both same-backbone and cross-backbone experience initialization, while exposing persistent limitations in motion precision and contact stability. These results support executable coordination as a practical interface for adapting long horizon multi robot collaboration under shared physical constraints.

GRASP: Global-Local Representation Alignment and Spatial-Physical Grounding for Bimanual Robotic Manipulation

GRASP: Global-Local Representation Alignment and Spatial-Physical Grounding for Bimanual Robotic Manipulation

Zeyuan Ren、Guanxun Zhao、Qi Li、Hao Wang、Wenlong Liao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models that directly regress joint-space actions avoid the deployment-time cost of inverse kinematics. However, their training objectives remain misaligned with task success, as joint-space losses ignore the unequal physical consequences of joint errors and visual conditioning lacks explicit focus on hand-object interaction regions. To address these issues, we propose a training-phase prior injection framework for fine-grained bimanual manipulation. First, a physics-grounded flow-matching loss maps the implicit clean-action estimate at every noise level through differentiable forward kinematics (FK) and supervises the resulting Tool Center Point (TCP) pose and inter-arm relative pose, with a bounded noise-level weighting that compensates gradient decay. Second, we introduce a global prediction query group distilled from a frozen DINOv3 teacher through future-frame feature prediction, together with a local interaction query supervised by Grounding DINO at the attention level. Both teachers are removed at inference, and the deployment pipeline is that of a standard joint-space flow policy, adding only the learned query pathway. Simulation and real-robot experiments on long-horizon bimanual tasks show that the two priors are complementary across different deployment settings. The full model excels in in-distribution deployment, while the distilled query branch is preferable under strong visual randomization.

InstAgent: A Recursively Self-Improving Agent Operating System for Heterogeneous Robot Collaboration

InstAgent: A Recursively Self-Improving Agent Operating System for Heterogeneous Robot Collaboration

Haoyang Luo、Junhao Qin、Jingyan Cao、Yi Huang、Guangjie Yang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Heterogeneous robot teams enable complex, long-horizon tasks by combining complementary sensing, mobility, and manipulation capabilities across multiple embodiments. Yet existing systems struggle to retain execution-time corrections as reusable experience, maintain task-relevant context memory across space and time for reliable handoffs, and explicitly represent the inter-robot dependencies required for structured collaboration. In order to address these challenges, we present InstAgent, a recursively self-improving (RSI) Agent Operating System for heterogeneous robot collaboration. InstAgent organizes team behaviors as agentic behavior trees (BTs) that explicitly model capability assignment, inter-robot dependencies and handoff checks. The RSI mechanism updates these collaboration programs from execution feedback and preserves successful revisions for reuse in future tasks, while shared multimodal spatio-temporal memory provides context for planning and handoff validation. Experiments on 20 real-world multi-robot tasks with three high-level models show consistent gains in task success, first-attempt completion, and execution efficiency.

GR-VIP: Geometric representation conditioning for precise visuomotor policy

GR-VIP: Geometric representation conditioning for precise visuomotor policy

Chanhwi Lee、Jiwon Seong、Eunje Choi、Hohyung Lee、Jongsoo Lee 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Achieving precise terminal position and heading alignment remains a significant challenge for learning-based mobile robot control. Recent vision-based learning methods have shown promise for precise target-relative control and generalization to unseen environments with pretrained visual encoders, but general-purpose visual representations are not specifically designed to capture the fine-grained spatial differences between current and goal views required for precise control, and real-world demonstrations combining millimeter-scale terminal accuracy with zero-shot transfer across environments remain limited. To address these challenges, we propose Geometric Representation Conditioning for Precise Visuomotor Policy (GR-VIP), a goal-conditioned visuomotor policy that leverages a visual encoder pretrained for relative camera pose estimation. Rather than using the final relative-pose estimate, GR-VIP conditions action prediction on the dense relational features produced before pose regression, preserving fine-grained cross-view information for learning corrective actions from demonstrations. We evaluate GR-VIP on a real-world docking task and demonstrate high docking success rates and millimeter-scale terminal accuracy in both the training and unseen environments, without additional training for transfer. Furthermore, supplementary experiments illustrate the policy's ability to align with non-docking targets and complete docking under a tested low-light condition.

Beyond Success Rate: Interventional Evaluation of Memory in Vision-Language-Action Systems

Beyond Success Rate: Interventional Evaluation of Memory in Vision-Language-Action Systems

Keyuan Luo、Yi Xie、Wanyun Ling、Yuanzhe Li、Ziyue Li 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

When we say that a robot uses memory, does that memory actually play a role? Success-rate ablations alone cannot establish the use of task-relevant facts: they change information and computation together, while aggregate rates can hide offsetting gains and losses. We propose an Interventional Memory Evaluation Protocol (IMEP) for vision-language-action (VLA) systems to address four questions: (1) Does memory provide a net benefit? (2) Under what conditions does memory help? (3) Do changes to memory alter task outcomes? (4) Are these effects specific to task-relevant facts? At the retrieval interface, we compare target-fact substitutions with fact-preserving replacements, minimal numerical perturbations, and exact replicas, while assessing decision correctness separately from motor execution success. We evaluate within-episode, cross-episode, and agentic memory. In cross-episode learned-bank experiments, a one-unit-in-the-last-place (ULP) intervention, including its installation procedure, changes 11.9% of seed-paired outcomes, while changing key facts produces no clear additional effect over fact-preserving replacements. To examine when memory helps, we introduce ChainBench, where correct decisions require historical information unavailable in current observations. The full memory configuration raises task success from 39.1% to 74.3%, matching the oracle-decision condition under shared recorded executions. These results show that correct history can support robot decisions, but establishing whether a memory system realizes this benefit requires fact-specific evidence beyond aggregate success rates.

When we say that a robot uses memory, does that memory actually play a role? Success-rate ablations alone cannot establish the use of task-relevant facts: they change information and computation together, while aggregate rates can hide offsetting gains and losses. We propose an Interventional Memory Evaluation Protocol (IMEP) for vision-language-action (VLA) systems to address four questions: (1) Does memory provide a net benefit? (2) Under what conditions does memory help? (3) Do changes to memory alter task outcomes? (4) Are these effects specific to task-relevant facts? At the retrieval interface, we compare target-fact substitutions with fact-preserving replacements, minimal numerical perturbations, and exact replicas, while assessing decision correctness separately from motor execution success. We evaluate within-episode, cross-episode, and agentic memory. In cross-episode learned-bank experiments, a one-unit-in-the-last-place (ULP) intervention, including its installation procedure, changes 11.9% of seed-paired outcomes, while changing key facts produces no clear additional effect over fact-preserving replacements. To examine when memory helps, we introduce ChainBench, where correct decisions require historical information unavailable in current observations. The full memory configuration raises task success from 39.1% to 74.3%, matching the oracle-decision condition under shared recorded executions. These results show that correct history can support robot decisions, but establishing whether a memory system realizes this benefit requires fact-specific evidence beyond aggregate success rates.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM机器人视觉
2026-10-16

MOSS: Multimodal Origami Swamp Robot System

MOSS: Multimodal Origami Swamp Robot System

T. Lan、A. Babgei、L. Romanello、M. Kovac、S. F. Armanini 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robotic systems for environmental monitoring and ecological surveying in wetlands and swamps must operate across diverse terrestrial and aquatic conditions. Conventional fixed-geometry wheels cannot adapt their contact geometry to these varying conditions. This work presents MOSS (Multimodal Origami Swamp Robot System), a compact amphibious robotic platform with 3D-printed origami wheels. The compact crank mechanism switches the wheel between contracted and expanded configurations, changing its width, body diameter, and fin orientation simultaneously. The contracted configuration is better suited to efficient rolling on firmer ground, whereas the expanded configuration provides greater contact and terrain engagement on softer surfaces. MOSS was evaluated on soil, sand, mud with varying water content, and water, in addition to in-motion morphing and terrestrial-aquatic transition tests. The more energy-efficient wheel configuration depended on terrain. The contracted configuration achieved a lower cost of transport on firmer soil and mud, whereas the expanded configuration performed better on wetter mud, sand, and water, reducing the cost of transport by up to 29%. Expanded wheels also reduced slip in wet mud and reduced both slip and hub sinkage in sand. MOSS successfully completed configuration changes in $1.95 \pm 0.11$ s and demonstrated continuous locomotion across soil-mud-water transitions. Outdoor trials further demonstrated operation across natural deformable terrain and water. These results show that adaptive wheel geometry provides terrain-dependent locomotion benefits and enables a single compact platform to traverse substantially different terrestrial and aquatic conditions.

What Makes a Good Symbol for Hybrid Discrete-Continuous Planning?

What Makes a Good Symbol for Hybrid Discrete-Continuous Planning?

Yiyuan Pan、Changliu Liu、Jiayuan Mao

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The physical world has continuous state and action spaces, yet robot planning often benefits from hybrid discrete-continuous formulations that explicitly expose combinatorial structure, such as contact modes or discrete primitive actions. Existing methods typically rely on human-defined symbol sets or symbols derived from language supervision, without asking what makes a symbol computationally useful. In this paper, we take an inference-algorithm--centric view to define usefulness: a good symbol should expose the discrete commitments that simplify solving the original planning problem. We first introduce the concept of solver-sufficient symbolization by studying finite-horizon piecewise-affine systems, for which solver sufficiency can be precisely characterized, and develop a counterexample-guided algorithm for symbol invention. We then extend this principle to invent symbols for optimization objectives parameterized by general neural networks as energy functions, showing that solver-sufficient symbols can nevertheless be induced from their continuous optimization landscapes. Experiments on simulated and real-robot tasks show that the resulting symbols reduce solving cost while preserving solution quality, while also improving interpretability and steerability. Furthermore, our theoretical and algorithmic insights help explain why certain human-defined symbols are computationally useful.

Ego2Contact: Predicting Touch from Egocentric Human Video for Contact-Rich Robot Manipulation

Ego2Contact: Predicting Touch from Egocentric Human Video for Contact-Rich Robot Manipulation

Qianqian Yang、Dayou Li、Bangya Liu、Zihao Zhu、Nuo Chen 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Contact locations and interaction forces are critical for contact-rich robot manipulation but are often ambiguous from vision alone. Measuring them on robots requires specialized tactile hardware and platform-specific data collection, which keeps robot tactile data scarce. Human egocentric video is far easier to scale but lacks touch. Recent predictors trained on egocentric recordings paired with tactile-glove measurements can predict this missing signal from video alone. These predictors, however, remain confined to human hands, which differ from robot hands in appearance and tactile layout. We present Ego2Contact, which learns a visual-tactile prior from human interactions and transfers it to robot hands. Its predictor fuses temporal scene context with hand-local visual features to infer bimanual tactile fields from the RGB video. Anatomical region correspondences align varying robot tactile layouts with the predictor's canonical grid, turning robot measurements into fine-tuning targets and predictions into policy inputs. Ego2Contact outperforms prior tactile predictors on human data, including unseen objects, and on robot data after adaptation. The adapted predictor provides tactile inputs for both simulated reinforcement learning and real-robot policy post-training. In simulation, policies using predicted touch outperform non-tactile baselines and recover part of the benefit of ground-truth tactile sensing. These results show that human-learned tactile priors can supply touch when direct tactile sensing is limited.

2026-10-16

LAW: Unifying Language, Action, and World Modeling for Agentic Physical Intelligence

LAW: Unifying Language, Action, and World Modeling for Agentic Physical Intelligence

Jiajun Cao、Kai Tang、Bohua Zhang、Zezhong Qian、Ying Li 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

World-action models jointly predict actions and future world states, but long-horizon manipulation further requires language reasoning to plan subtask objectives, assess execution progress, and guide action selection. We present LAW, a closed-loop VLM--WAM framework that unifies language reasoning, action generation, and world modeling through semantic subtask progress. Within the LAW agent, a hybrid-expert vision-language model plans the remaining subtasks, estimates the values of and fuses actions proposed by a world-action model, and asynchronously assesses execution status to support continued execution and subtask transitions. Within the world-action model, Mixture-of-Transformer Experts integrates modality-specific and shared computation for joint video--action modeling. Dual-stream hierarchical visual memory maintains temporal context for both models, while subtask-local value learning aligns action evaluation with semantic objectives and preserves supervision from successful prefixes of failed trajectories. LAW achieves 94.21% average success across 50 RoboTwin tasks. Replacing the WAM backbone with OpenWAM-$alpha$ further raises the average success rate to 95.56%, establishing a new open-source state of the art on RoboTwin. Despite having only 2B parameters, our VLM also outperforms variants using Embodied-R1.5 and GPT-5.5 High. On three long-horizon real-robot tasks, LAW achieves 65% average success, compared with 28.33% for $pi_{0.5}$ and 25% for Fast-WAM.

2026-10-16

SimpleVLN: Towards Simple and Frontier Vision-Language Navigation

SimpleVLN: Towards Simple and Frontier Vision-Language Navigation

Lingyi Hong、Weiren Cai、Jiayu Chen、Xinyu Zhou、Bailin He 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-and-Language Navigation (VLN) requires embodied agents to ground language in visual observations and make long-horizon decisions. Existing methods often address these demands through task-specific architectures. As multimodal large language models (MLLMs) become increasingly capable, an important question remains: Have we truly unlocked the potential of native MLLMs for VLN before introducing task-specific architecture? We present SimpleVLN, an architecture-preserving framework developed through studies of navigation formulation, data construction, and optimization. At the formulation level, SimpleVLN combines single-turn decision inputs with fixed-budget global-local memory to efficiently use navigation history without unbounded context growth, while action chunks enable efficient output of multiple primitive actions per model invocation. For data construction, we combine expert demonstrations with policy-dependent DAgger experience and develop a scalable synthesis pipeline that generates large-scale tasks for reinforcement learning. At the optimization level, a progressive IL-DAgger-RL pipeline advances from action imitation to task-level outcome optimization. Comprehensive experiments validate the effectiveness of these three aspects. We instantiate SimpleVLN across multiple MLLM backbones and model sizes, demonstrating its generality. SimpleVLN achieves 76.7% and 80.1% success rates on the R2R-CE and RxR-CE splits, respectively, substantially outperforming existing methods. These results show that the navigation capabilities of native MLLMs can be effectively unlocked without task-specific architectural designs.

These results show that the navigation capabilities of native MLLMs can be effectively unlocked without task-specific architectural designs.

ORD-WM: Learning Object-Centric Rigid Dynamics for 3D World Modeling in Robotic Manipulation

ORD-WM: Learning Object-Centric Rigid Dynamics for 3D World Modeling in Robotic Manipulation

Yueyang Weng、Xiaopeng Zhang、Yuxi Ou、Yongjin Mu、Chengzhi Zhao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

World models provide robots with a predictive mechanism for reasoning about how actions change object states in manipulation tasks. However, existing image-based and dense 3D world models do not explicitly impose object-level rigid motion, leaving rigid-object geometry preservation to be learned implicitly and making multi-step rollouts susceptible to shape drift or object-level motion inconsistency. To address this, we propose ORD-WM, a 3D world model that learns object-centric rigid dynamics for robotic manipulation. ORD-WM learns a vector-neuron variational autoencoder supervised by signed distance fields, which encodes each object point cloud as a geometry-aware latent representation composed of 3D vector features, and uses an action-conditioned dynamics model to predict object-wise SE(3) motion increments. Rather than generating unconstrained next latent states, ORD-WM analytically applies predicted object-wise rigid transformations to the current latent states, separating persistent object geometry from pose changes. Experiments show that ORD-WM enables stable, geometry-preserving multi-step prediction and supports efficient real-time replanning for closed-loop model predictive control in simulated and real-world manipulation scenarios.

SAMS: A Structured Graph Framework for Securing Additive Manufacturing Systems

SAMS: A Structured Graph Framework for Securing Additive Manufacturing Systems

Sarah Roscoe、Lachlan Hovany、Kamila Maciocha、Witawas Srisa-an、Jitender S. Deogun 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Additive manufacturing, also known as 3D printing, is significant for its use in industries such as aerospace, robotics, medicine, and construction. However, digital mesh files remain vulnerable to geometry defects which can be introduced through malicious tampering or designer error. This can lead to hampered structural integrity and impede its use due to unexpected degradation of the part. To address these challenges, we propose SAMS, a multi-layered hierarchical graph framework designed to analyze meshes to enhance the Security of Additive Manufacturing Systems. SAMS captures geometric and topological information across mesh, facet, and surface levels, providing an adaptable architecture for targeted mesh processing tasks. We evaluate SAMS's accuracy against existing mesh processing tools. The graph construction results in rich structural representation and has the potential to significantly reduce downstream analytical complexity when detecting vulnerabilities. This paper presents SAMS, its formal graph-theoretic properties, and releases an open-source Python 3.12 implementation on GitHub (https://anonymous.4open.science/r/SAMS-graph-classes/) to facilitate future research in secure additive manufacturing systems software.

Online Constraint Optimization-Based Indirect-Adaptive Robust Control for Autonomous Vehicles with Unknown Friction

Online Constraint Optimization-Based Indirect-Adaptive Robust Control for Autonomous Vehicles with Unknown Friction

Yupeng Zhou、I-Chia Chang、Ziran Wang、Bin Yao

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Friction uncertainty is a significant challenge for autonomous vehicle control, undermining feasibility and performance. This paper proposes a novel two-layer control framework to achieve vehicle motion control under large friction uncertainty by combining optimization-based planning with indirect adaptive robust control (IARC). The upper layer solves a constrained optimal control problem to generate a feasible trajectory that satisfies all the constraints in a time-optimal manner along the desired path. The lower layer, IARC, adapts the unknown parameters related to friction uncertainty and combines model compensation with robust feedback to ensure transient and steady-state tracking performance. An estimation monitor is designed to prevent poor learning when data indicate a model mismatch, while a smooth parameter reset reduces the estimated tire capacity and limits abrupt tightening of constraints. The updated parameters are fed back into the model and constraints in the planner, enabling trajectory replanning as friction conditions change. Simulation results indicate that the proposed method achieves accurate tracking of the desired trajectory and online friction adaptation under large friction uncertainty while ensuring time-optimal vehicle motion. The simulation results demos are available at https://github.com/TedZhou0603/CO-IARC.

Blind Precise Assembly: Proprioceptive Sim-to-Real Peg-in-Hole for Diverse Parts

Blind Precise Assembly: Proprioceptive Sim-to-Real Peg-in-Hole for Diverse Parts

Antonia Bronars、Younghyo Park、Kushal Kedia、Pulkit Agrawal

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Every robot already carries high-resolution contact sensors: its own joints. Legged robots and humanoids exploit them to walk, run, and track whole-body motions without vision, but there the contacts are large and forceful. Is joint sensing also enough for precise, contact-rich assembly, where the robot must find a millimeter-scale opening, mesh gear teeth, or catch a thread through light contact? We study this question by training blind assembly policies that observe only a short history of joint positions, velocities, and their own actions, without visual, force, torque, or tactile sensing. The poses of both the fixture and the part are unknown to the policy, and the grasp is randomized substantially. The policy must therefore assemble the parts through contact, sensed exclusively through its own proprioception. Trained with reinforcement learning entirely in simulation over randomized parts, grasps, and contacts, such policies learn peg insertion, gear meshing, and nut threading. The peg insertion policy transfers zero-shot to the real world and inserts pegs of different shapes and sizes in 73% of trials. Controlled comparisons with matched visual policies in simulation show that blind policies generalize far better to unseen part shapes and sizes, though they can be slower. Training such policies needs thousands of different assemblies, each simulated with its clearance intact, so we also release an open-source generator that builds them procedurally from exact convex pieces for MuJoCo; it is more accurate and faster than convex decomposition or MuJoCo's signed-distance collision. Videos and interactive simulations are at https://younghyopark.me/blind-proprioceptive-assembly/.

SparseMax-dSTL: Sound Robustness with Horizon-Independent Gradient Allocation for Temporal Logic Trajectory Optimization

SparseMax-dSTL: Sound Robustness with Horizon-Independent Gradient Allocation for Temporal Logic Trajectory Optimization

Sai Sandeep Damera、Ryan Matheu、John S. Baras、Calin Belta

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

A gradient-based solver that enforces a Signal Temporal Logic (STL) specification uses a smooth approximation of its quantitative semantics, the robustness. In safety-critical trajectory optimization, this approximation must have two properties. First, it must be sound, that is, nonnegative only on trajectories that satisfy the specification. Second, at each temporal operator, the weight of its gradient on the violating samples must stay bounded away from zero, however long the horizon. The approximations in use, including the log-sum-exp, its sound variant, and the generalized-mean robustness, do not have both. We introduce lower approximations of the minimum and the maximum whose gradients are sparsemax projections, and we prove that the robustness built from them has both. It lies below the exact robustness and is therefore sound, and its error has a bound that is independent of the number of samples and known before optimization. The gradient of each approximation is exactly zero on every sample beyond a data-dependent threshold, so the weight on violating samples a gap beyond the others has a positive lower bound at every horizon. On a planar unicycle and on a seven-joint manipulator beside a person in simulation, the log-sum-exp approximation is positive on a violating trajectory. As the horizon of the manipulator's task grows from 10 to 80 s, the gradient weight of the sound variant on the violating samples falls from 0.22 to 0.04, while that of sparsemax stays at 1. In a constrained trajectory optimization of the unicycle, the constraint on the log-sum-exp robustness returns a violating trajectory, whereas the constraint on the sparsemax robustness returns a trajectory that satisfies the specification with the required margin.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM机器人视觉遥感
2026-10-16

Foundation-Model-Driven 3D Scene Graphs for Mobile Manipulation

Foundation-Model-Driven 3D Scene Graphs for Mobile Manipulation

Dominic Maggio、Lorenzo Shaikewitz、Aryan Naveen、Kris Hauser、Luca Carlone 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Geometric and Semantic Foundation models are becoming increasingly popular tools for generating spatial maps for robots. Recent work (e.g., FOUND-IT) has leveraged these tools to construct real-time, task-driven, and open-set 3D scene graphs from a single uncalibrated camera. However, while classical robot mapping techniques have undergone extensive system demonstrations, limited work has been done to develop both the fundamental and the systems wide advancements needed to integrate these new types of robot spatial memory into a full, useable autonomy pipeline. In this paper, we bridge this gap, and in doing so we also evaluate the effectiveness and limitations of this type of 3D memory. Towards this end, we integrate FOUND-IT into a mobile manipulator platform and demonstrate it is well-suited to support multiple objectives such as object change detection, support for multiple mapping sessions, determination of navigable paths, and directly support object grasping for the task of scene re-arrangement. Furthermore, we provide nuanced insights into the effectiveness and limitations of a monocular, foundation-model-driven 3D scene graph construction system using observations from rigorous on-robot experiments. Code is available at https://github. com/MIT-SPARK/FOUND-IT.

However, while classical robot mapping techniques have undergone extensive system demonstrations, limited work has been done to develop both the fundamental and the systems wide advancements needed to integrate these new types of robot spatial memory into a full, useable autonomy pipeline. In this paper, we bridge this gap, and in doing so we also evaluate the effectiveness and limitations of this type of 3D memory. Towards this end, we integrate FOUND-IT into a mobile manipulator platform and demonstrate it is well-suited to support multiple objectives such as object change detection, support for multiple mapping sessions, determination of navigable paths, and directly support object grasping for the task of scene re-arrangement. Furthermore, we provide nuanced insights into the effectiveness and limitations of a monocular, foundation-model-driven 3D scene graph construction system using observations from rigorous on-robot experiments. Code is available at https://github. com/MIT-SPARK/FOUND-IT.

多模态/VLM

Reason-guided Human Reward Modeling for Preference-based Reinforcement Learning

Reason-guided Human Reward Modeling for Preference-based Reinforcement Learning

Anshul Nayak、Shahil Shaik、Jonathan Smereka、Yue Wang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Reinforcement learning (RL) policies optimized for task rewards may produce behaviors that are efficient but misaligned with human expectations, safety preferences, or intent. This paper presents a reason-guided preference-based reinforcement learning (PbRL) framework that learns a human reward model from trajectory-level feedback. In addition to preference labels, the framework incorporates reason tags explaining why a behavior is preferred, enabling the learned reward to capture both human choices and their underlying rationale. Compared with preference-only reward learning, the reason-guided formulation provides additional supervision for learning human-aligned rewards and improves the interpretability of the resulting policy. The learned human reward is combined with the environment reward during policy optimization, allowing the agent to improve task performance while producing more interpretable, human- aligned, and preference-sensitive behavior

HexaShell: A Thin Harness for Exploring the Limits of VLM Agents as Robot Coding Agents

HexaShell: A Thin Harness for Exploring the Limits of VLM Agents as Robot Coding Agents

Hongcheng Gao、Alex Zhou、Jay Zhu、Zelin Zheng、Shijia Ge 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Agents built on vision language models (VLMs) now compete with learned policies on robot manipulation leaderboards. We ask how far a coding agent can go when it controls a robot instead of a computer, and where it stops. Our system, HexaShell, keeps the harness thin: it only turns the agent's commands into robot motion. The agent drives a robot with two arms through one command line. It sees three RGB cameras and knows their calibration. After each failed attempt, the agent reads the log, finds the cause, and writes or fixes small programs called tools. In this way it keeps what it learns as code, as a coding agent does when it fixes a failing test. The tools may use only what the robot observes. They use triangulation to find where objects are, relative to each other and to the gripper. If two cameras see the same point, the point lies where the two camera rays meet. If a point lies on a known plane, such as the table or the top of a block, it lies where its ray meets that plane. When the head camera is not enough, the robot moves a wrist camera to get a second view. We build the tools on only ten layouts of one seed per task, then freeze them and test them on ten new layouts of the same seed. We use Codex with GPT-6-Astra at medium reasoning effort. On all 42 RoboDojo tasks, HexaShell reaches a success rate of 39.7% and a progress score of 48.0, averaged in the same way as the leaderboard. This is a new state of the art on RoboDojo. The limit is contact, not reasoning: pushing reaches 92.5% and memory tasks 65.0%, but tight insertion only 8.3%. When the agent fails, it usually sees the failure but cannot make the motion work. We open source the tools evolved so far at https://github.com/HexaFuture/HexaShell.

No Eyes, No Problem: What Does Touch Contribute to Blind Baoding Ball Manipulation?

No Eyes, No Problem: What Does Touch Contribute to Blind Baoding Ball Manipulation?

Ayush Deshmukh、Nalin Agarwal、Elle Miller、Sethu Vijayakumar

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Humans manipulate objects within their hands through touch alone with remarkable ease, but for robots, achieving sustained real-world manipulation without vision remains an open challenge. Although reinforcement learning (RL) can generate sophisticated blind manipulation policies in simulation, transferring them to a real dexterous hand remains hindered by continuously changing contacts, motion-induced tactile information drift, and the gap between simulated and physical dynamics. We study this problem through Baoding ball rotation on the Shadow Dexterous Hand Lite, using only proprioception and binary tactile feedback as observations. We achieve successful zero-shot transfer through a combination of a sensor-aligned, coupling-aware embodiment in simulation, tactile calibration, and two novel episodic domain randomisations: slew-rate action limits and STuck-AT Taxels. On the physical hand, the policy achieves sustained blind Baoding rotation, completing 225 consecutive $\mathbf{180^\circ}$ rotations (112.5 full rotations) at $\sim0.36 \;\text{rot/s}$ in a single continuous trial without any vision or object-state information. Furthermore, we evaluate the contribution of tactile sensing relative to proprioception alone and identify the hand regions where tactile information is most important. This is the strongest reported hardware result for Baoding ball manipulation in both rotation count and speed, irrespective of the sensing modality used. By effectively bridging the sim-to-real gap, our framework sets a new benchmark for sustained tactile-driven dexterous manipulation on physical hardware.

AWAM: Active Trajectory-Conditioned View Imagination for Robust Robot Manipulation

AWAM: Active Trajectory-Conditioned View Imagination for Robust Robot Manipulation

Jiawei Geng、Zhenyang Liu、Yuzheng Wu、Yanwei Fu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Rapid progress in embodied intelligence has enabled increasingly capable robots to follow language instructions and manipulate diverse objects. However, strong results in structured environments do not automatically transfer to open-world conditions, where clutter, occlusion, and changing viewpoints can obscure the spatial relationships required for action. To address this perceptual gap, a virtual active-perception paradigm is introduced. AWAM, an Active World-Action Model actively plans where to observe and imagines what will be seen along the planned trajectory before acting. Task-related virtual camera motion is predicted and used as a structured query into a geometry-conditioned world model, producing a reusable spatial prior for manipulation, which is later compressed into a fixed memory and fused with live observations through cross-attention for diffusion-based action prediction. AWAM achieves competitive success rates with 75.9% on LIBERO-Occ, 62.4% on LIBERO-Plus, 76.5% on RLBench-OG Occlusion1, and 94.4% in real-robot trials. These results support improved robustness to the evaluated occlusions and distribution shifts, addressing part of the gap between structured manipulation and less controlled environments.

2026-10-16

LY-GWM-RoboCasa-Human300: Prediction-Guided Action Selection with a Graph Dynamics Model

LY-GWM-RoboCasa-Human300: Prediction-Guided Action Selection with a Graph Dynamics Model

TianLiang Ma、Ting Ma、ZhongYuan Ma

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We present LY-GWM-RoboCasa-Human300, a policy that combines a Human300-fine-tuned GR00T-N1.5 action generator, an action-conditioned graph dynamics model, and a learned binary state-reward scorer. At inference, the policy samples four action chunks, predicts the future latent features and robot state for each, and executes the candidate with the highest predicted-state reward logit. Predictions therefore influence the actions sent to the environment. The evaluated system uses a 16-step prediction horizon and keeps all three components frozen during evaluation. In the local evaluation on 50 RoboCasa 1.0.1 tasks in the pretrain kitchen split, with 50 episodes per task, the system records 207/2,500 successes (8.28%), compared with 181/2,500 (7.24%) in the historical self-trained GR00T-N1.5 baseline. The observed difference is 26 successes, or 1.04 percentage points. The atomic group improves from 141/900 to 150/900. Separately, the RoboCasa maintainers re-evaluated the submission and reported 354/1,500 successes (23.6%) across 50 tasks, with leaderboard rates of 48.3%, 16.3%, and 3.1% for Atomic-Seen, Composite-Seen, and Composite-Unseen. The local and official results are reported separately; their difference is not attributed to a model improvement. Recorded local audits identify 212,700 changed environment-input chunks, including 28,223 in atomic tasks. We describe the actual training stages, implemented inference mechanism, evaluation provenance, and public reproduction entrypoints. These results document an active prediction-and-selection system and a positive historical difference.

2026-10-16

Structural Affinity: An Algorithm for Coherent Cage-Based Shape Design

Structural Affinity: An Algorithm for Coherent Cage-Based Shape Design

Aviraj Newatia、Shengan Yang、Hantao Zhong、Rika Antonova

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Cage-based deformation (CBD) parametrises 3D geometry through a small set of control handles, effectively enabling a variety of tasks from animation and interactive modelling to tool-based co-design, neural deformation transfer, and aerodynamic shape optimization. Many optimization approaches relying on CBD employ regularisation strategies to reduce the distortion of the deformed mesh by encouraging smooth, regular cage deformations. These techniques offer soft guidance to an optimization algorithm towards desirable deformations, but compete with the task objective rather than changing the deformation parameterization itself. In this work we introduce Structural Affinity for CBD, an explicit regularisation method which enables smooth deformations through control cages by exploiting a two-cage structure to couple cage-handle motion directly. Structural Affinity precomputes an affinity matrix using mean-value coordinates, binding the inner control-cage to a coarser outer-cage. This matrix defines how much a cage handle displacement propagates to neighbouring handles which substantially decreases and often eliminates cage self-intersections, reduces mesh distortion, and adds linear overhead at deformation time. Across robotic control--morphology co-design, source-to-target neural deformation transfer, and aerodynamic wing shape optimization, Structural Affinity produces more regular deformations and substantially reduces reliance on hand-designed structural loss terms. We report consistent gains on three complementary diagnostics: the cotangent Laplacian, a proxy for surface smoothness; cage distortion, capturing whether control handles move coherently; and surface roughness, which measures undesirable oscillation in wing geometry.
